# CLAUDE.md

Guidance for Claude Code (claude.ai/code) working in this repository.

**Present-tense invariants only**, audit-verified against the code at v2.21.1 (2026-09-16), with v2.22.0's coding-suite sections folded in from that release. Three companion files carry what does not belong here:

| File | Holds | Read it when |
|---|---|---|
| `docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md` | Living status: rig gates R1-R16 / E1-E9 / A-R1-A-R7 / G1-G5, open work, dead code, deliberate non-fixes | The task touches edu6/edu1 bring-up, Orange-Pi parity, or you need to know what is currently broken or unproven |
| `docs/CLAUDE-CHANGELOG.md` | Dated narratives: why each rule exists, what it cost, the measurements behind it | You need the history behind a rule before changing it |
| `docs/plans/` | Gitignored throwaway spec-before-code one-pagers | Starting a multi-layer feature |

**Maintenance rules for this file.** Fold new invariants into the matching section; the dated story goes to the changelog, living status to known-issues. Never stack dated sections here. State the rule and just enough "why" to stop someone undoing it; forensics (what the old bug measured, which prototype was rejected) belong in the changelog. **Cite symbols, never line numbers** (`gui_app.py::_on_cameras_changed`). If this file disagrees with the code, the code wins: fix this file in the same change.

## What this is

**EduBotics**: a vertically integrated stack for teaching Physical AI on **ROBOTIS OpenMANIPULATOR-X** arms (plus two EduBotics Feetech arms) to **German-speaking students**. Student lifecycle: install `.exe` → setup wizard → record demos (ROS 2 teleop) → train policy (Modal cloud GPU) → inference (local or classroom Jetson) → optional **Roboter Studio** (Blockly or real Python/Java authoring + AprilTag perception + manual arm control).

Students run Windows 11 PCs without GPUs; training runs on Modal (NVIDIA L4; A100 for VLA). The product ships as one `.exe` that installs a bundled WSL2 Ubuntu 22.04 distro named `EduBotics` containing Docker Engine + four containers; **no Docker Desktop**. Images pull from **GHCR (primary) with Docker Hub fallback**. The teacher/admin web dashboard is a separate Railway deploy. Installs are online-only. Single git repo, no submodules; ROBOTIS upstream (`open_manipulator/`, `physical_ai_tools/`) is absorbed as plain directories.

## Repository topology

| Path | Role |
|---|---|
| `VERSION` | Single-line product version (source of truth for the 4-site bump, see Deploy) |
| `open_manipulator/` | Absorbed upstream ROS 2 arm bringup + Dynamixel + C++ `ros2_control` (reaches the image via the **overlay** model) |
| `physical_ai_tools/physical_ai_server/` | The ROS node package: LeRobot recorder, inference, **Roboter Studio `workflow/`**, safety. **COPY-wholesale** into the image |
| `physical_ai_tools/physical_ai_interfaces/` | ROS msgs/srvs (the wire-int contract with React) |
| `physical_ai_tools/physical_ai_manager/` | React 19 SPA on Vite 8; one codebase, student + teacher-web builds |
| `robotis_ai_setup/` | EduBotics-authored core: `gui/` (EduBotics.exe), `installer/`, `wsl_rootfs/`, `docker/`, `cloud_training_api/`, `modal_training/`, `supabase/`, `jetson_agent/`, `pi_agent/`, `tests/`, `scripts/` |
| `docs/` | Checked-in docs; `docs/plans/` is gitignored throwaway |
| `tools/` | Operator utilities: `docker-hub-cleanup.sh`, `modal-cleanup.sh`, URDF export, AprilTag/ChArUco/mat generators, `edu6_provision.py` |
| `classroom_kit/` | Printable calibration assets |
| `.github/` | `ci.yml` (25 validator jobs), 6 deploy workflows, lint/parity scripts |

Never treat as source: `robotis_ai_setup/gui/dist/` and `installer/output/` (build output, absent from the tree), `installer/assets/edubotics-rootfs.tar.gz(+.sha256)` (gitignored, produced by `wsl_rootfs/build_rootfs.sh`). `docker/versions.env` is CI-generated and absent from the tree (GUI readers fall back to `latest`; the Pi's `pi_agent/constants.py` falls back to `APP_VERSION`). `supabase/_archive/` holds 20 pre-squash `.sql` files (19 numbered 002-021, 014 absent, plus `migration.sql`): git-blame reference only, never applied; its README's "21" count is wrong. The parent `C:\Users\svend\cloud\CLAUDE.md` is a 2-line pointer stub; leave it alone.

## Big-picture architecture

10 layers, top to bottom:

1. **Windows installer + WSL2 rootfs**: `robotis_ai_setup/installer/` (Inno Setup + PowerShell), `robotis_ai_setup/wsl_rootfs/` (Ubuntu 22.04, Docker `5:27.5.1-1~ubuntu.22.04~jammy` + containerd `1.7.27-1` pinned)
2. **Windows tkinter GUI (`EduBotics.exe`)**: `robotis_ai_setup/gui/` (PyInstaller); wraps every Docker call as `wsl -d EduBotics -- docker …`
3. **Robot-arm bringup (ROS 2 Jazzy + Dynamixel / Feetech)**: `open_manipulator/`, overlay + plain-COPY scripts in `robotis_ai_setup/docker/open_manipulator/`
4. **Docker Compose (3 containers on `ros_net`, plus the network-less `code_runner`)**: `robotis_ai_setup/docker/`
5. **Dataset recording (LeRobot 0.5.1, dataset codebase v3.0)**: `physical_ai_tools/physical_ai_server/`
6. **React 19 SPA (student + web dashboard)**: `physical_ai_manager/`; `Dockerfile` (student), `Dockerfile.web` (Railway), `Dockerfile.opi` (Orange Pi)
7. **Cloud training (Railway + Modal + Supabase)**: `cloud_training_api/`, `modal_training/`, `supabase/`
8. **Inference**: `physical_ai_server/inference/inference_manager.py`
9. **Roboter Studio**: `physical_ai_server/workflow/` (Blockly and the Python/Java coding suite) + `robotis_ai_setup/docker/code_runner/` + `cloud_training_api/app/routes/workflows.py` + migrations 008→041
10. **Classroom Jetson Orin Nano**: shared remote inference target (Inferenz tab only); `jetson_agent/` + `routes/jetson.py`

```
Windows GUI ─wsl→ EduBotics distro (Docker; images from GHCR, Hub fallback)
              ├─ open_manipulator   (ROS 2 + Dynamixel/Feetech + USB)
              ├─ physical_ai_server (ROS 2 + PyTorch + LeRobot + s6 + Roboter Studio)
              │        ▲  two unix sockets in the code_runner_ipc volume
              ├─ code_runner        (CPython + JDK sandbox; no network, no device)
              └─ physical_ai_manager (nginx + React, :80)
                        │  HTTPS
   Railway FastAPI (cloud_training_api, scintillating-empathy-production-1068)
   ├─ Modal SDK .spawn() ─→ Modal worker (L4 / A100 GPU) ─→ HuggingFace Hub
   └─ supabase-py (service-role) ─→ Supabase (Auth/Postgres/Realtime)
```

## Six non-negotiable rules

These exist because we paid for the lesson. Don't undo without explicit user agreement.

### 1. German UI / English code

- **German** for everything a student/teacher/admin reads: tkinter labels, React UI, API `detail` fields, user-visible log strings, toasts. Literal `ä ö ü ß` (legacy files use `Schueler`; new code uses `Schüler`).
- **English** for everything the maintainer reads: code, comments, docstrings, internal logs, JSON keys, function names, commit messages.
- **One sanctioned exception, an invariant and not a lapse: the `[TECHNIK] ` line** (owner decision A14). A student running their own Python or Java is shown the tool's own last line (a CPython traceback line, a `javac` message) in English. The German sentence leads (`code_errors_de.sentence`); the raw line is a SEPARATE `log_message` prefixed `[TECHNIK] ` (`code_program.py::_raise_error`), rendered monospace by the Protokoll and repeated under the run bar's error banner (`RunControls.jsx`, `CODE_DE.ERROR_TECHNIK_LABEL`), never spliced into the sentence and never replacing it. The reason does not generalise: for Java, `javac`'s text is the only thing that says what was wrong. Do not "fix" it as a German-strings violation: `german-strings-lint` cannot see `code_errors_de.py`, and `test_code_errors_de.py` enforces that the SENTENCE stays German with no English tool fragment.
- CI guard `german-strings-lint` = a grep for transliterations on `[FEHLER]`/`[WARNUNG]`/`[STOPP]` lines + `.github/scripts/german_detail_lint.py`, an `ast.parse` walker over exactly two scopes (`SCAN_SCOPES`): cloud-API `detail=`/`error_message=` and pi_agent `message=` (the pi_agent `{"ok","message"}` JSON is student-facing). `# english-ok` is the escape hatch. The two violation classes are deliberately asymmetric: TRANSLITERATION runs on every string BEFORE the language test; ENGLISH only on strings that do not classify as German. Never nest the transliteration check inside `is_german` (a string with no umlaut and no `GERMAN_WORDS` hit classifies as neither language and would never be checked). The walker cannot see React or `tools/`; the umlaut rule binds there by review (`tests/test_feetech_bus.py` runs the lint pattern over `tools/edu6_provision.py`'s literals).

### 2. Hardware safety lives in xacro + entrypoint, not software overlays

Software inference safety envelopes (NaN guard, joint clamp, velocity cap, stale-camera halt) were removed deliberately to fix recording↔inference asymmetry; inference runs upstream `predict()` raw. Remaining protection is hardware-enforced or warning-only:

- **Xacro Dynamixel limits** (`overlays/omx_f.ros2_control.xacro`, `omx_l.ros2_control.xacro`): restrictive position limits exist only on dxl12/dxl13; dxl11 (Op Mode 4) and dxl14/15 are full-range, and the leader xacro has no position limits (torque-free). The real floor is the gripper current limits (follower dxl16 `Current Limit 350` + `Goal Current 350` + `Shutdown 21`, Op Mode 5; leader trigger 300 mA).
- **ros2_control YAML**: 100 Hz; follower JointTrajectoryController `trajectory` tolerance 2.0 rad (effectively off), `goal` 0.10 rad arm / 0.50 rad gripper, `goal_time` 5.0 s. Upstream's tight values aborted the 3 s quintic boot-sync → compose restart-storm.
- **Torque-disable on shutdown**: `entrypoint_omx.sh::disable_torque()` runs from `cleanup`, which is `trap`ped on `SIGTERM SIGINT EXIT`, so `set -e` aborts are covered. Leader path is the namespaced `/leader/omx_l/set_dxl_torque`.
- **Entrypoint phases**: 1 leader, 2 follower, 3 quintic sync + verify (0.30 rad tolerance, arm joints only, ≥50 % of commanded delta traversed, soft-fail `[WARN]`; a hard exit looped compose restarts), 3.5 activation agent, 4 cameras. Exactly two hard-exit paths: camera disambiguation (exit 3) and `wait_for_device()`'s 60 s serial timeout (exit 1).
- Recording-side quality guards (stale camera, a camera slower than 0.9×fps — the page's amber banner —, the per-take capture warning: a lost slot, excess repeats, late arm data) are warning-only and never re-record. **A source that dies is not a quality issue** (round 5, owner decisions R5-2/O2/C7): a required camera, follower or leader silent for `signal_status.SOURCE_STOPPED_S` (2 s, by server-side arrival, judged only on an on-time tick) ends the session like „Beenden" — within ≤ 3.5 s of the source's last message (2 s silence + the ~1 s official discard of the running take; owner bound, round 7) — the running take dropped, saved episodes finalized and uploaded by the usual guards, one German sentence naming the source („Aufnahme beendet: …"); a take during which a source was silent ≥ `SOURCE_GAP_S` (0.25 s) on its OWN timeline is re-recorded („Signalaussetzer: …"), and so is a take the encoder dropped frames from, under ONE shared cap per episode, `MAX_REDOS_PER_EPISODE` (2, gaps and frame loss together, round 6): beyond it a gapped take is kept with a warning and a frame-loss take ends the session like „Beenden" (C7); under FINISH a discarded take ends the session with the saved episodes. Every error stop (a convert failure, the dataset init, a `record()` exception, the data gate before a dataset exists) finalizes what was saved without uploading (D5, `DataManager.end_after_error`, which answers True/False/None for finalized/failed/no dataset); the crash marker is cleared ONLY by a successful finalize (F3, round 6), otherwise it stays and the READY says `ERROR_STOP_INCOMPLETE_DE` instead of `ERROR_STOP_SAVED_DE`. After a dataset exists the data gate ends like a stopped source instead (finalized AND uploaded). Below `DISK_CRITICAL_FLOOR_BYTES` (1 GB) the server ends the session itself like FINISH (committed episodes kept, a running episode ≥ 1 s kept).
- **Leader arm joints 1-5 are torque-free by design** (`Torque Enable 0`, Goal/Present Current commented out; broadcasters only). The leader gripper trigger is not limp: `trigger_position_controller` + a 50 Hz `ros2 topic pub` of −0.7 rad simulate spring resistance under the 300 mA cap. Two stale comments still claim gravity compensation exists (`docker/open_manipulator/Dockerfile` leader block; `collision_monitor.py` header); the xacro + launch files are the truth.

**Boot motion is GATED (`EDUBOTICS_REQUIRE_ACTIVATION`), and that is not a software safety envelope.** No profile moves at container start; the home glide, the leader sync and the teleop-controller spawn wait for a student's press on the Startseite (see „Nothing moves at container start" below). It removes an unattended motion and adds no guard to the pipeline. **Torque is deliberately not gated**: de-energising a backdriving arm at boot would be the worse failure.

**One sanctioned exception: the teleop force/collision e-stop**, scoped to teleop/recording only. Gated OFF during inference (inside the detector via `mode_is_inference`) and OFF during workflow and manual mode (`collision_monitor.py`, one combined `on_workflow or on_manual` gate; the manual half is a sanctioned scope change, since hand-guiding presses the arm at ~0 velocity). It never modifies samples: a trip DISCARDS the in-progress episode. A leader-arm Vormachen take (`on_leader_teach`) is teleop and claims neither flag, so the e-stop stays armed.

**A second execution path drives the arm, and its four conditions are its price** (owner decision A7). A student's Python or Java runs in the `code_runner` sandbox and reaches the arm over a unix socket; `workflow/code_rpc.py` dispatches into the SAME `workflow/handlers/` a Blockly program uses, so every arm-facing refusal in that layer (table floor, IK reach, no-go-zone reroute, velocity floor, `motion._hold_motion_lock`) applies unchanged and nothing new touches `ctx.publisher`. The four conditions are not optional and no agent may descope them:

1. **A call-rate budget at both ends**: a runner-side floor in the stub before the socket, and a server-side token bucket charged for EVERY decoded frame BEFORE validation (`robot_api.RPC_LIMITS`: 200 calls/s, burst 50; `sees`-class rows 20/s, burst 5). A unix socket carries ~466 k calls/s, so the boundary relocates a flood rather than throttling it.
2. **Stop travels out-of-band** on a new control connection, and `WorkflowManager._run`'s `finally` never depends on the student process answering. Every wait in `CodeProgram.execute` is timeout-bounded, so `execute` returns within the DERIVED `CODE_STOP_DEADLINE_S` (`CODE_RPC_RECV_TIMEOUT_S + CODE_CONTROL_TIMEOUT_S + CODE_WORKER_JOIN_S`) of `_stop_event`, never a hand-picked literal.
3. **Every frame is hostile.** `code_rpc.py` validates method, arity, per-parameter kind and range server-side from `robot_api`'s own rows, whether the frame came from the shipped stub or a raw `socket` (the runner ships `socket`, `subprocess` and `ctypes`, decision A9).
4. **Different uids, and the supervisor is not PID 1.** `init: true` makes tini PID 1; the supervisor is uid 0 with exactly `SETUID`, `SETGID`, `KILL`, and every student process runs as uid 10001. A non-root `user:` was measured infeasible (Docker grants it no effective capabilities, so it could neither spawn as another uid nor signal it).

The debugger hook and the runner-side floor live INSIDE the student's process and can be uninstalled from it; they are not boundaries. The server-side budgets and validation are. Document that; do not "fix" it.

Two frame kinds reach NO handler and no arm, and sit inside the same four conditions rather than beside them: the public code-only row `zeige` (`robot_api.CODE_ONLY_METHODS`) and the internal live-values frame `__vars` (sent by the student's own thread, see „Live values" under the coding suite). Both are charged against the budget before validation like every frame, validated from their own `robot_api` rows (the `value` kind is bounded in depth, nodes and characters), and answered by the server itself (`code_rpc.RunSession._code_only` / `_emit_var` / `_queue_shown` / `_flush_shown`), which touches only `ctx.log`; `test_code_rpc_zeige_vars.py` AST-fences that nothing there reaches `ctx.publisher` or the motion lock. What a charged frame may cost AFTER the charge is bounded too (the flood limits): one `__vars` frame per `VARS_MIN_INTERVAL_S` (0.4 s) is looked at, any other answered at once with `VARS_REPLY_SKIPPED`, unrendered; the shown values of one `__vars` / `__paused` frame are held to `SHOWN_FRAME_MAX_NODES` / `SHOWN_FRAME_MAX_CHARS` IN TOTAL by `code_rpc.fit_shown_values`, which replaces the largest values with `SHOWN_TOO_BIG` („<zu groß>", linear, measured once per value) and never drops a frame whole; and `zeige` is coalesced — the latest payload per name waits and `[VAR:]` goes out at most `SHOWN_EMITS_PER_S` (20/s, burst 20), flushed by the next call, the worker's idle tick (`SHOWN_FLUSH_TICK_S`) and the run's close, so the last value of every WAITING name arrives before the final status. At most `SHOWN_VAR_NAMES_MAX` (256) names wait at once; a new name past that is dropped with one German `[WARNUNG]` per run (`ZEIGE_TOO_MANY_NAMES_DE`). **The runner's bounds and the server's caps have ONE source** (`robot_api`: `LIVE_VALUE_MAX_NODES`/`_CHARS`, `PAUSED_VALUE_MAX_CHARS`, and the frame caps DERIVED from them as `PAUSED_MAX_LOCALS` × the larger worst case, today 15 030 nodes / 30 000 characters), so a runner-rendered frame provably fits and only a hand-made one is ever trimmed; a mismatch once dropped whole `__vars` frames the runner thought delivered and showed none of a breakpoint's 20 variables (`tests/test_code_runner_limits_lockstep.py` pins the runner's copies). **The known cost ceiling of this surface is by design, not a defect**: the most expensive frame a hostile raw socket can send inside the budget is a maximal `zeige` value, about 2 ms of CPU per frame, so the 200/s budget caps a flood at roughly half of one core (measured on an Apple M5 Pro under CPython 3.12 with client and server in ONE process, so the server's own share is lower; a flood of maximal `__vars` frames costs about 1 ms each, since only one per 0.4 s is rendered). Lowering it means lowering `RPC_LIMITS` or the value bounds, both of which a student program feels.

If you need to reintroduce any OTHER software safety guard that modifies the pipeline, **stop and ask the user**.

### 3. The shipped image must reflect the repo

**physical-ai-server: COPY-wholesale, no overlays.** `build-images.sh` stages `physical_ai_tools/physical_ai_server/` as `pkg_src/` (excluding `test/`, the three base Dockerfiles, `CHANGELOG.rst`, bytecode); the thin `docker/physical_ai_server/Dockerfile` `rm`s the base's package, `COPY`s the staging tree and re-`colcon`s, so "image == repo HEAD" holds by construction (edits, new files and deletions propagate). The Dockerfile asserts that the stripped `safety_envelope.py` and the dead `training/trainers` + `evaluation` trees never re-enter (`build-images.sh` re-checks `safety_envelope.py` only). Six build-time smoke gates: policy imports + `CODEBASE_VERSION == 'v3.0'`, node import, `control_msgs` gpio import, functional cv_bridge rgb8 decode, the `HF_TOKEN_PATH` gate (`get_token()` honours the path, re-reads a rewritten file, returns None after deletion, `HF_HUB_DISABLE_IMPLICIT_TOKEN` unset; its Python source must start in column 0, `tests/test_hf_token_compose.py` compiles it), and the `[DATEN]` gate (Daten 2.0: the sidecar `daten.http_server`, the download worker and `dataset_sync`/`hub_sync`/`v3_surgery` import, `v3_surgery.DEFAULT_DATA_PATH`/`DEFAULT_VIDEO_PATH` equal `lerobot.datasets.utils`'s, so a LeRobot bump that moves the layout fails the build, and `huggingface_hub.utils._http.default_client_factory` exists). The interfaces check also asserts the generated `_daten_command.py`.

**open-manipulator: still the overlay model.** `apply_overlay()` in `docker/open_manipulator/Dockerfile` does sha256 pre/post verification over the **7-file** chain (both `ros2_control.xacro`s, both `*_ai.launch.py`s, both controller-manager YAMLs, `camera_usb_cam.launch.py`); the build aborts on a missing upstream target. **A new overlay must join the chain with a unique path filter**, or the edit never reaches the image. Standalone scripts (`identify_arm.py`, `camera_ingest_node.py`, `calibrate_collision_currents.py`, `edu6_arm_node.py`, `feetech_bus.py`, `edu6_geometry.py`, `activation_agent.py`) are plain `COPY`s. The broadcaster C++ lives in the BASE image's `git clone`, so editing this repo's `open_manipulator/` C++ ships nothing.

**code-runner: COPY-wholesale too, with its own parity kind.** The image's `/opt/edubotics/runner` is a verbatim copy of `robotis_ai_setup/docker/code_runner/runner/` minus what the build adds (`java-classes/`, bytecode). Four files under it (the Python stub and the three Java files) are GENERATED from `workflow/robot_api.py` and checked in, so an edit there without re-rendering fails `test_robot_api_generated.py`.

**All three are guarded by `image-source-parity`** (`.github/scripts/image_source_parity.sh`, per-arch in `docker-publish.yml::smoke-test`): byte-identity (`diff -r`) for the server package and the runner tree, sha256 for every overlay and plain-COPY script. Without the runner kind, an edit to the shipped stub library or the supervisor ships nothing, silently. The parity script mirrors `build-images.sh`'s staging exclusions; change one, change both. LeRobot is not overlaid but pip-installed from PyPI (Rule §5); `patches/fix_server_inference.py` is retained only so CI `overlay-guard` can test the patch harness. The server **amd64 BASE image is pulled, never built** (`robotis/physical-ai-server:amd64-0.8.2`, resolved via `docker manifest inspect`); `Dockerfile.amd64` is forward-compat scaffolding (keep its pins in lockstep).

**The other two server bases are ours, and no workflow builds them.** `BUILD_BASE_ARM64=1 PLATFORM=arm64` → `Dockerfile.arm64` (Jetson); `BUILD_BASE_OPI=1 PLATFORM=opi` → `Dockerfile.arm64cpu` (Orange Pi CPU base). CI only *resolves* the tag pinned in `build-images.sh`, so **editing those Dockerfiles ships nothing on its own**. Two fences: `ci.yml::base-version-guard` fails a non-comment edit whose `PAS_BASE_IMAGE` tag didn't move in the same commit; `docker/bump-upstream-digests.sh` reports the self-built pins and exits non-zero on a tag never pushed. The rebuild is a Rule §6 manual step.

### 4. Service-role key bypasses RLS: authorization is your job

Every Supabase query in `cloud_training_api/app/` runs as **service-role** via `services/supabase_client.py::get_supabase()` (lazy singleton); RLS is dormant under it. Every endpoint touching another user's data **must** call an ownership assert. Teacher/workgroup/workflow helpers raise a German 404, never 403, to avoid existence leakage; the Jetson pair differs deliberately (`_assert_classroom_member` → 403; `_assert_jetson_owner` → 404 or **410 „Lock verloren"**; `_assert_workgroup_in_classroom` can also 400).

- `_assert_classroom_owned` / `_assert_student_owned` / `_assert_entry_owned` / `_assert_workgroup_in_classroom` — `routes/teacher.py`
- `_assert_workgroup_owned` — `routes/workgroups.py`
- `_assert_workflow_owned` (write) / `_assert_workflow_visible` (read ladder: owner OR group sibling OR classroom template) — `routes/workflows.py`
- `_assert_classroom_member` / `_assert_jetson_owner` — `routes/jetson.py`

**One missed assertion = silent IDOR.** Writes additionally re-scope the query (`.eq("owner_user_id", …)`). Dataset registration is enforced in Postgres: `register_dataset_safe` RPC (migration 026, `FOR UPDATE` on the caller's users row, HF-author anchor, P0034 → 403). The anchor is the author of the student's OLDEST `datasets` row OR (043) the proven account name of the student's stored token, `user_hf_credentials.hf_username`, both compared `lower()`; never a name the client asserts.

**`public.users` has a column guard, and it must stay SECURITY INVOKER** (migration 044, `users_guard_protected_columns` on `trg_0_users_protected_columns`, BEFORE UPDATE FOR EACH ROW). Role `authenticated` holds UPDATE on the table and the only UPDATE policy is own-row, so without the guard a student could PATCH their own `role`, `training_credits`, `hf_username`, `classroom_id` and `workgroup_id` through PostgREST (confirmed on the live database). The guard raises 42501 when `current_user IN ('authenticated','anon')` changes any of those five (`IS DISTINCT FROM`; a column written back with its current value passes); service_role, migrations and SECURITY DEFINER functions (current_user = owner) pass. A definer trigger would see its owner as current_user and let everybody through. Its second rule binds EVERY caller: while a `user_hf_credentials` row exists, `users.hf_username` may only become that row's name (P0044, mapped to PATCH /me's 409), which is what makes PATCH /me atomic against a token save. `username`, `full_name`, `email`, `created_by`, `deletion_requested_at` and the two vision-quota columns are still student-writable (`docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Any new server-only column on `users` joins rule 1 in a migration of its own. The workflow read-visibility ladder is implemented FOUR times in `routes/workflows.py` (`_assert_workflow_visible`; inline in `list_workflows` + `get_workflow`; ad hoc in `clone_workflow`) plus near-twin resolvers in `services/workgroups.py`; semantics changes must touch every copy.

**Students write no table directly; every write goes through the cloud API (service role) or a SECURITY DEFINER RPC** (migration 046). Supabase's default privileges grant every public table to `anon` and `authenticated` in full and RLS was the only fence, so own-row write policies made `trainings`, `datasets`, `workflows` and `tutorial_progress` student-writable through PostgREST: a training set to `canceled`/`failed` or deleted refunded its credit, free GPU time outright for a finished run, which keeps its model (credits are DERIVED from `trainings.status`, see „Two-phase Modal cancel"; on a running job the worker stops at its next progress write), and a direct `datasets` INSERT skipped `register_dataset_safe`'s anchor. 046 revokes INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE, REFERENCES and TRIGGER on those four from both request roles and drops their twelve own-row write policies; SELECT, the „… read consolidated" policies, the Realtime publication and service_role are untouched, and the migration aborts if `has_table_privilege` still answers true for either role (a PUBLIC grant or a membership would otherwise survive the revoke). The audit behind it: the cloud API is service-role only, every SQL writer of the four is SECURITY DEFINER (`start_training_safe`, `update_training_progress`, `register_dataset_safe`, `update_workflow_blockly`, `update_workflow_code`, `restore_workflow_version`), the Modal worker's only call is `update_training_progress`, and the SPA's only table call is the trainings SELECT. **A future direct student write needs BOTH a policy AND a grant again, in a migration of its own.** Still request-role-writable, outside 046 (`docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`): `users` (own row, with 044's column guard), `jetsons` (teacher/admin write policies) and `workflow_versions` (the snapshot trigger's INSERT policy). One credit path stays open outside the table grants: a training's `worker_token` is readable through the trainings SELECT and `update_training_progress` is executable by both request roles, so a student can still mark a RUNNING own training `failed` (`docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`).

The Modal worker uses the **anon key** + per-row `worker_token`. Its only DB write is the `update_training_progress(p_token, …)` RPC, guarded by `WHERE status NOT IN ('succeeded','failed','canceled','cancel_requested')` (migrations 010 + 032): a late write is a P0001 no-op, and that P0001 is the worker's in-band cancel signal. GRANTs are asymmetric: `update_training_progress` anon+authenticated, `register_dataset_safe` service-role only. `update_training_progress` is deliberately absent from the boot schema probe (overload-ambiguous signature), so drift there is invisible to `/health`.

### 5. Don't introduce drift between the LeRobot pinning sites

The pin `lerobot[pi,smolvla,peft]==0.5.1` must agree across `modal_training/modal_app.py::LEROBOT_VERSION`, `physical_ai_server/Dockerfile.amd64`, `Dockerfile.arm64` (Jetson AI Lab pip index), `Dockerfile.arm64cpu` (Orange Pi, stock `ros:jazzy-ros-base`, plain PyPI; **torch `2.7.0` + torchvision `0.22.0` pinned explicitly BEFORE lerobot**, because that base inherits no torch and an unpinned resolve pulls torch 2.10.x), and the thin overlay `docker/physical_ai_server/Dockerfile`, which CI actually builds on. Bumping LeRobot is a one-PR multi-site change; `meta/info.json` `codebase_version: "v3.0"` and `training_handler.py::EXPECTED_CODEBASE_VERSION` move with it. The recorder leans on LeRobot's writer behaviour, and only on its PUBLIC surface (round 5: the recording path names no private LeRobot symbol; `test_data_manager_record_fsm.py` AST-fences `_streaming_encoder`, `_dropped_frames`, `_frame_queues`, `_stop_events`): an EMPTY writer buffer at episode start; a take discarded through `clear_episode_buffer()` (`lerobot_dataset_wrapper.py::discard_episode`) whose ~1 s encoder cancel must land in a frameless tick; the „Encoder queue full for <key>, dropped N frame(s)" warning on logger `lerobot.datasets.video_utils` (the frame-drop detection); `create(..., encoder_queue_maxsize=…)`; `create_lerobot_dataset_card`; `sanity_check_dataset_robot_compatibility`. The thin Dockerfile's seam step asserts each of them in the built image, and the smoke test (`.github/scripts/record_fsm_smoke.py`, run in the amd64 and opi server images by `docker-publish.yml::smoke-test`; the Jetson image is not smoked) re-checks the behaviour on a bump. **Neither the recording nor the Daten path names a private LeRobot symbol** (Daten 2.0 retired the last one, `dataset_tools._copy_and_reindex_videos` and its `_force_recorder_vcodec` monkeypatch; KNOWN-ISSUES Q1 is closed). The edit engine (`data_processing/v3_surgery.py`) writes through the PUBLIC `LeRobotDatasetMetadata` API (`create` with the parameters it passes, `save_episode`, `save_episode_tasks`, `finalize`, `get_task_index`) and verifies through `video_utils.decode_video_frames`; the seam step asserts exactly those, the `[DATEN]` gate pins its layout templates to LeRobot's, and `.github/scripts/daten_smoke.py` re-proves the lossless claim with the real 0.5.1 writer, loader and decoder.

**Hard dependency caps, never widen silently:** `numpy==1.26.4`, force-reinstalled AFTER lerobot in every server image (ros-jazzy-cv-bridge's boost `.so` is built against NumPy 1.x; numpy 2.x segfaults the rgb8 decode path, proven by the build-time decode gate); `scipy>=1.14.0,<1.18` in all four server Dockerfiles (scipy 1.18 dropped numpy-1.x support).

**No torchcodec in any server image; LeRobot's default video decoder is PyAV**, as on the Modal worker. LeRobot 0.5.1 depends on torchcodec on linux x86_64 (not on aarch64), so the thin Dockerfile uninstalls it AFTER the lerobot install (an uninstall before it was undone, and the amd64 image shipped a torchcodec that cannot load under the `torch==2.7.0+cpu` swap: every frame read without an explicit backend failed there). The seam step asserts `find_spec('torchcodec') is None` and `get_safe_default_codec() == 'pyav'`; the Daten engine also passes `backend='pyav'` explicitly.

**One PyTorch surface (2.7.x), three CUDA channels.** Modal worker: `nvidia/cuda:12.6.1-devel` + `torch==2.7.0`. Student amd64: the base ships torch+cu128, then the build-arg-gated `SLIM_CUDA=1` step (set by `build-images.sh` for amd64 only) swaps to `torch==2.7.0+cpu` and drops CUDA, and `flatten_image` (`docker export | import`) reclaims the whiteouts (also slims `-opi`). Dropping CUDA is not just `/usr/local/cuda*`: `/opt/nvidia/nsight-compute` and `/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libnccl.so*` + `libnccl_static.a` live outside that prefix and are removed in the same branch; **they must stay in the `SLIM_CUDA=1` branch** (Jetson keeps GPU torch and legitimately needs NCCL; opi has neither path). CPU-only students are safe: `load_policy()` returns a German „needs GPU" error, recording never loads a policy, training is cloud-only.

### 6. CI/CD deploys

Seven workflows in `.github/workflows/`:

- `ci.yml`: 25 validator jobs on every push/PR to `main`.
- `supabase-migrate.yml` (**W1**): `supabase db push --linked --include-all` (password extracted with `urllib.parse`) against project `fnnbysrjkfugsqzwcksd`; project-scoped concurrency group `supabase-migrate-production`. A `workflow_dispatch` `rollback_to` input applies `rollback/<name>.sql` via psql.
- `railway-deploy-cloud-api.yml` (**W2**): pre-deploy import probe → `railway up` → `/health` gate asserting `body.commit == github.sha` (never a bare 200; blue-green keeps the old pod serving 200). **Its `schema-probe` job does not probe the schema**, whatever its name and comment say: `python -c "from app import main"` never fires the `@app.on_event("startup")` that schedules `_validate_required_schema`, so it proves only that the requirements install, every router imports and `_validate_required_secrets` passes. Schema drift is still caught, later and with a worse message, by the app SIGTERM-ing itself and failing the `/health` gate (`docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). The same blind spot covers the encryption key of the per-student token: the CI probe carries no Railway variables, so a malformed `EDUBOTICS_HF_TOKEN_KEY` is judged only at the Railway boot (`main.py::_validate_hf_token_key`), where it fails the `/health` gate while the old pod keeps serving; set the key BEFORE W2 (`docs/deploy/DEPLOY.md`).
- `railway-deploy-teacher-web.yml` (**W3**): placeholder-build validate → `railway up` via `scripts/railway-deploy.sh` → `/version.json` gate asserting the buildId contains the SHA.
- `docker-publish.yml` (**W4**): builds + dual-pushes **10 images** (4 amd64 + 2 `-jetson` arm64 + 4 `-opi` arm64; the Jetson pair carries no manager and no runner, the whole Roboter-Studio tab being `jetsonIncompatible`) to **GHCR primary (`ghcr.io/svendanilborodun/*`) + Docker Hub fallback (`nettername/*`)**. Fires on `main` pushes (`:latest`), `workflow_call`, `workflow_dispatch`; its standalone tag-push trigger was removed, so on a tag only `release.yml` builds. `build-images.sh` pushes to Docker Hub FIRST; the `retag` job creates GHCR tags from Hub via `imagetools create`.
- `release-installer.yml` (**W5**): Windows `.exe` build; `workflow_call` + `workflow_dispatch` only.
- `release.yml`: dispatcher on `vX.Y.Z` tags. **Golden order W1→W6**: supabase → cloud-api → teacher-web → docker → installer → **W6 `publish-gui-version`** (inline job: sets Railway `GUI_RELEASE_REPO`/`GUI_DOWNLOAD_URL`/`GUI_INSTALLER_SHA256`, then `GUI_VERSION` LAST, after the `.exe` is attached).

**opi is decoupled from the core release.** Its build + smoke-test legs are `continue-on-error: matrix.platform == 'opi'`; the build leg uploads an `opi-build-success` marker (carrying the run's sha) last, and `retag` retags opi only when that marker exists AND matches `github.sha`, never off an existence probe of the mutable `:latest`. Dual-push integrity warns rather than fails for opi.

**`version-preflight` runs first on tag pushes** and hard-fails when the tag ≠ the `VERSION` file (W6 advertises the TAG while the tarball/installer bake the FILE; a mismatch puts every Pi and Windows GUI into an infinite forced-update loop) and when any of the three fallback literals differs from it.

**The Pi tarball.** `release.yml::pi-agent-tarball` attaches `edubotics-pi-agent.tar.gz` (top-level `pi_agent/`) with three baked, gated files: the repo `VERSION` as `pi_agent/VERSION`, a generated `pi_agent/docker/versions.env` pinning `IMAGE_TAG=<VERSION>`, and the tracked `docker-compose.opi.yml` twin, which must `cmp` equal to `robotis_ai_setup/docker/docker-compose.opi.yml`. The post-`tar` cleanup deletes only the GENERATED `versions.env`; `rm -rf …/pi_agent/docker` would take the tracked twin. W6 publishes `PI_AGENT_SHA256` before the `GUI_VERSION` flip, emptied on hash failure AND when any of the four `-opi:<VERSION>` images (the runner included) is anonymously pullable on NEITHER registry; **an empty hash ⇒ the Pi advertises no self-update**. `pi-agent-tarball` is an ordering-only `needs` of W6, which gates only on `installer` success. The reverse direction (a failed installer skips both Pi jobs after `-opi` images published) is an owner call (`docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`).

**The Modal app (`edubotics-training`) deploys MANUALLY** (the image builds in Modal's infrastructure and the CI loop is too slow to debug remotely). Run it BEFORE pushing a tag if the release touches Modal:

```bash
cd robotis_ai_setup/modal_training
modal deploy modal_app.py
modal run -m modal_app::smoke_test    # optional
```

**The three self-built Docker BASES also deploy manually** (Rule §3): rebuild + push and bump the tag in `build-images.sh` BEFORE the tag, or the release silently ships on the old base. Every other manual `railway up`, `psql -f` or full `build-images.sh` run is EMERGENCY-only; CI refuses to build from a dirty tree.

**Applying migrations:** `supabase db push` works; the Supabase MCP `apply_migration` also works but re-stamps the version prefix into the ledger, so **rename the on-disk file to the MCP-stamped value afterwards** (the doubled-prefix filename on migration 026 is this scar).
## Critical architectural choices (don't undo silently)

### Windows host, WSL2, container lifecycle

- **No Docker Desktop.** Docker Engine runs in the bundled WSL2 distro `EduBotics`; the GUI calls it as `wsl -d EduBotics -- docker …` (`docker_manager.py::_docker_cmd`), never bare `docker`. USB attaches via `usbipd attach --wsl EduBotics --busid X` (5.x positional form), always through `usbipd_resolver.find_usbipd()`'s absolute path (the Inno-launched GUI inherits explorer's pre-MSI PATH). Docker `5:27.5.1` + containerd `1.7.27-1` are pinned (`apt-mark hold`, `containerd-snapshotter: false` in `daemon.json`): 29.x's snapshotter corrupts multi-layer pulls on a custom rootfs. `start-dockerd.sh` is the distro's `[boot] command`, not systemd: it modprobes serial/UVC modules, starts udev, watchdogs dockerd and exports its own PATH (`wsl.conf` sets `appendWindowsPath=false`). **Its modprobe list must stay in sync with `device_manager.diagnose_usb_environment`**; `device_manager.self_heal_wsl_serial` is the scan-time counterpart. Installer Step 0 `migrate_from_docker_desktop.ps1` removes a pre-existing Docker Desktop (one-shot `%ProgramData%\EduBotics\.migrated` flag). The GUI runs `start_keepalive()` for the session (WSL2's ~60 s `vmIdleTimeout` would kill dockerd mid-lesson; `atexit` reaps it). usbipd-win is a pinned, SHA256-verified 5.3.0 MSI; a `RELEASE_PIN_NEEDED` sentinel in the `.iss` hard-fails `install_prerequisites.ps1` on a forgotten pin.

- **The GUI is the sole owner of the container lifecycle.** The stack comes up ONLY on „Umgebung starten", enforced twice: every student-stack service in `docker-compose.yml` is `restart: "no"`, and `gui_app.py::_run_prerequisite_checks_body` calls `ensure_environment_stopped()` immediately after the dockerd-ready gate and BEFORE the rootfs-mismatch gate, which `return`s. **Any new early return after a distro boot must sit below the teardown.** `_scan_arms` calls it first too (`identify_arm.py` opens the `/dev/serial` ports a live 100 Hz controller holds). **„Arme scannen" ends the previous student's session** (teardown closes the WebView2 child via `destroy_all()` first), so it confirms first, but only when a student window is open (`has_live_window()`); a decline is a total no-op. The scan is REFUSED with a German sentence, before confirmation and any state change, while finalize runs or prerequisites are unfinished (`_scan_blocked_reason`, separate from the silent re-entrancy guard). That sentence comes from `_setup_unfinished_de`, reading `_last_setup_outcome` / `_last_setup_detail`, what THIS session concluded, kept in-process and never re-derived from `.reboot_required` (finalize's Phase 0 may legitimately delete it).

  Two sanctioned `restart:` exceptions. **Jetson** uses `unless-stopped` (the agent owns that lifecycle). **Orange Pi** runs `physical_ai_manager` `unless-stopped` at boot (the manager is the setup wizard); `open_manipulator` + `physical_ai_server` stay `restart: "no"`, and the pi-agent's `ensure_environment_stopped` stops only the robot tier, never the manager, never `compose down`.

- **Compose is boot-order- and security-opinionated** (`docker/docker-compose.yml`). Health-gated: `physical_ai_server` waits on `open_manipulator: service_healthy`, the manager on the server. Only `open_manipulator` is `privileged` + `/dev:/dev`. **`physical_ai_server` publishes no host port** (`:9090`/`:8080` went with the rosbridge move behind nginx; that removal is what makes the nginx Origin allowlist non-bypassable). Published: the manager's `127.0.0.1:80:80` and `open_manipulator`'s `127.0.0.1:5557:5557` (native camera ingest), loopback-bound because rosbridge is unauthenticated. Both robot containers get `SYS_NICE` + rtprio/memlock ulimits; `mem_limit` 2g/6g/512m + `pids_limit` are what OOM-kill a leaky node. **`code_runner` is the opposite of all of it**: no port, no device, no network, no `depends_on` in either direction (the server runs without it and refuses code runs in German), and its only mount is the `code_runner_ipc` volume carrying the two sockets; its sandbox keys are under „The coding suite". The `open_manipulator` healthcheck inline-duplicates the entrypoint's WSL2 `uname -r | grep -i microsoft` camera-source detection; change one, change both. `CAMERA_NAME_1` defaults `gripper`, `CAMERA_NAME_2` defaults EMPTY: that asymmetry is how a 1-camera Roboter-Studio kit is told from a 2-camera rig. Compose bind-mounts `/etc/timezone` + `/etc/localtime` read-only, so the rootfs must keep `tzdata` (Docker auto-creates a missing mount target as a directory).

- **Students see the React UI through an embedded WebView2 child process.** „Web-Oberfläche öffnen" spawns `EduBotics.exe --webview` → `gui/app/webview_window.py`, a CHILD process because pywebview 6.x demands the main thread tkinter owns (`edgechromium`; system-browser fallback on import failure or a fast exit). The URL carries a cache-busting `_v=<IMAGE_TAG>` param, `?cloud=1` in cloud-only mode (appended by the GUI, not React) and `?fresh=<nonce>` (`secrets.token_hex`) for the SPA boot scrub. The module exposes `is_available()` / `runtime_missing()` / `has_live_window()` (the one sanctioned read of `_process`, under `_lock` like `destroy_all()`); both are **process-local**, so never describe `destroy_all()` as cleaning up a previous GUI process's window. `open_student_window`'s live-child short-circuit stays by design. `open_student_window` hands its watcher the Popen and the session generation captured under `_lock`, and the module exposes `set_exit_callback()` / `begin_session()` / `current_generation()` / `is_current_generation()` (see „A student window that ends on its own clears the robot's Hugging Face token" under Windows GUI + installer). `_check_prerequisites` hits the Cloud-API `/version` first: a newer release opens a non-closable modal that downloads the installer (`update_checker.py`: Content-Length + SHA-256 verified, HEAD-precheck against 404'd assets); „Überspringen" appears only after a failed attempt.

- **Elevated repairs share `_run_privileged`.** With an admin token already held the command runs directly, no UAC, INFINITE wait (a timeout kill reaches only powershell.exe while the wsl.exe grandchild survives; killing `wsl --import` mid-copy risks a corrupt VHDX). A standard token routes through `_elevate_and_wait` (ctypes `ShellExecuteExW`, `SEE_MASK_FLAG_NO_UI`, German SE_ERR classification). `exit_code is None` means „never launched"; launch failures and ≥2 UAC cancels open a manual-command fallback dialog whose command **must stay `$`-free** (interactive PowerShell interpolates `$` inside double quotes). The post-reboot finalize (`_prompt_finalize_install`) runs automatically with no consent dialog; `reason="rootfs_mismatch"` is the one path that still asks; guarded by `_finalize_in_progress`, cleared in a `finally`. Elevated transcripts reach the Protokoll through the one helper `gui_app.py::_transcript_excerpt` (HEAD + TAIL, dropping only the `****` rules and the `WSManStackVersion`/`PSRemotingProtocolVersion`/`SerializationVersion` noise; read as `utf-8-sig` because PS 5.1's `Start-Transcript` writes a BOM). **The previous finalize transcript is rotated, never deleted**: `_finalize_worker` moves `edubotics_finalize.log` to `edubotics_finalize.prev.log` (the name .NET's `ChangeExtension` gives, matching `finalize_install.ps1`'s own rotation) before each launch, falling back to delete on a failed rotate; the marker is always deleted, its absence being the „never launched" signal. `preflight_system.ps1` runs once per launch on a daemon thread: five non-fatal checks (temp-path write probe, UAC feasibility, WSL2, dockerd, account scope from the finalize marker), always `exit 0`, output forced to UTF-8 by a `[Console]::OutputEncoding` wrapper and decoded `errors="replace"`.

### Configuration, registries, version coupling

- **The GUI-managed `.env` (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\EduBotics\.env`) is the compose interface.** Compose resolves `${REGISTRY}`/`${IMAGE_TAG}`/camera keys only from the `--env-file` the GUI passes; Windows env vars do not cross `wsl.exe`. `config_generator.MANAGED_KEYS` (9: `FOLLOWER_PORT, LEADER_PORT, ROS_DOMAIN_ID, REGISTRY, REGISTRY_FALLBACK, IMAGE_TAG, EDUBOTICS_CAMERA_NAMES, EDUBOTICS_FOLLOWER_ONLY, EDUBOTICS_ROBOT_TYPE`, plus `_MANAGED_PREFIXES` `CAMERA_DEVICE_`/`CAMERA_NAME_`) are re-emitted on every regenerate, so a hand-pinned value is superseded; everything else is preserved verbatim by `_read_unmanaged_lines`, except a legacy `HF_TOKEN` + `HF_TOKEN_MACHINE` pair, which is dropped unconditionally (no Hugging Face token lives in the `.env` any more, see the account-token bullet below). **Keep `MANAGED_KEYS` in lockstep with the emitted lines.** Per-rig overrides go through env vars (`EDUBOTICS_IMAGE_TAG`, `EDUBOTICS_REGISTRY`); writes are atomic (temp + `os.replace` + fsync). The Pi has its own `MANAGED_KEYS` (13 vs 9); the divergences read as deliberate but nothing asserts them (`docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`).

- **IMAGE and COMPOSE are version-coupled, so `EDUBOTICS_IMAGE_TAG` is NOT a one-variable rollback on Windows.** `docker-compose.yml` ships with the installer (`{app}\docker\`, `constants.COMPOSE_FILE`) while the tag pins only the images. Three boundaries: the rosbridge move (`nginx.conf`'s `location = /rosbridge` and `= /video/stream` live in the manager image), the v2.15.0 student login gate (`utils/authGate.js`, also in that image) and 042's token slot (the compose sets `HF_TOKEN_PATH` plus the `/run/edubotics-hf` tmpfs and no longer forwards `HF_TOKEN`: a server image from before 042 under the new compose has no token at all, and the new image under an old compose has no slot to write). **Rollback is two-step: install the older `.exe` AND pin, or pin nothing.**

- **Registry resolution: env override → `docker/versions.env` → default**, shared by `REGISTRY` (`ghcr.io/svendanilborodun`), `REGISTRY_FALLBACK` (`nettername`) and `IMAGE_TAG` (`latest`) in `gui/app/constants.py::_resolve_setting`. **`EDUBOTICS_REGISTRY=nettername` IS a one-variable rollback to Hub** (the registry does not change what the image contains). The runtime fallback pulls the digest-identical Hub twin and `docker tag`s it to the GHCR name; that ref is a prefix swap, never `split('/')`, and the auto-pull digest pre-check uses set membership of the remote candidate set. `prune_superseded_tags()` is a no-op while `EDUBOTICS_IMAGE_TAG` is set (GUI and pi_agent twins, `.strip()`-guarded); the caller additionally gates on `any_updated and not any_failed`.

- **Both fleets pin `IMAGE_TAG` via a baked `versions.env`.** Windows gets `docker/versions.env` from `release-installer.yml`; the Pi gets `pi_agent/docker/versions.env` from `release.yml::pi-agent-tarball` at `/opt/edubotics/pi_agent/docker/versions.env`, the first path `pi_agent/constants.py::_read_versions_env`'s upward walk probes (nearest wins per key). Two writers on the Pi: the tarball and `pi_agent/setup.sh` at provision time. **With no `versions.env` the Pi falls back to `APP_VERSION`, its own release, not `latest`** (`_default_image_tag()`; only an actually-read VERSION counts, via `_VERSION_IS_AUTHORITATIVE`), so a release whose `-opi` images never published fails loudly, and `pi_agent/docker_manager.py` names the missing version in German.

- **The Hugging Face token belongs to the student's ACCOUNT, not to the PC or the robot image.** The student pastes it once on the Startseite (`Home/HfTokenCard.jsx`); `PUT /me/hf-token` validates it with the Hub's `GET /api/whoami-v2` (`services/hf_credentials.py::validate_with_hub`, a direct httpx call with a 10 s client timeout, because `HfApi.whoami` sets none; a read-only token is rejected at save time, and so is a fine-grained one whose PARSEABLE scopes grant no repository write on the owner's own user entity, `fine_grained_repo_write`, where a repository write is any permission starting with `repo` and ending in `.write` (`is_repo_write_permission`, so a Hub rename of today's `repo.write`/`repo.content.write` still passes, while `discussion.write`/`post.write` never count); an unrecognised scope block fails OPEN) and the `store_user_hf_credential` RPC stores it AES-256-GCM encrypted (key `EDUBOTICS_HF_TOKEN_KEY`, AAD = user id) in the service-role-only `user_hf_credentials` table and sets `users.hf_username` to the proven account name in the same transaction; the plaintext leaves the cloud ONLY through `POST /me/hf-token/reveal`, to its owner. The SPA relays it (browser memory only: plain thunks, never `createAsyncThunk`, never Redux/storage/console) to `/register_hf_user`, whose callback (`physical_ai_server.py::set_hf_user_callback` → `_apply_hf_token_request`, under `_hf_token_lock`) writes it atomically (0600, temp + fsync + replace) to the tmpfs file `$HF_TOKEN_PATH` (`/run/edubotics-hf/token`; `data_processing/hf_token_store.py`, stdlib only). `huggingface_hub` re-reads that file on every call and the spawned upload child inherits the env var, so no consumer passes a token. **Compose must never set `HF_TOKEN`, `HUGGING_FACE_HUB_TOKEN` or `HF_HUB_DISABLE_IMPLICIT_TOKEN`** (an environment token outranks the file; `tests/test_hf_token_compose.py` fences the student, opi and twin composes and the Jetson's absence of a path), and the thin Dockerfile carries a build-time gate on the `HF_TOKEN_PATH` contract. The node refuses a change while recording or while the HF worker is busy (the same token again is a no-op success); a refused CLEAR is remembered (`_hf_token_clear_pending`, under `_hf_token_lock`) and applied by the 1 Hz state tick on the first idle tick (`_apply_pending_hf_token_clear`, a bounded 0.5 s lock wait), unless a newer token was really written (or the same token arrived again; a set the node REFUSES, busy, bad shape or a failed write, leaves the clear queued, because the previous token is still in the slot), and the refusal says so (`HF_TOKEN_CLEAR_QUEUED_DE`); always answers `user_id_list` empty and runs neither `huggingface-cli` nor `whoami` for it, publishes only a fingerprint and booleans on `/edubotics/hf_token_state` (`hf_token_store.STATE_TOPIC`, latched + 1 Hz, published even when `accepts` is false so the SPA can tell a Jetson from an old image) and purges the legacy `huggingface-cli` token files from the cache volume at boot (`_init_hf_token_state`). `hf_…` is scrubbed from the HF/HTTP logs by `hf_token_store.install_log_scrubber` (the named loggers AND every handler: installed at node import, again in `_init_hf_token_state` after `HfApiWorker.__init__` ran `basicConfig`, and inside `hf_api_worker._worker_process_loop`, because the spawned child never imports the node; the older `_BearerTokenScrubber` stays Supabase-JWT-only); `print()` in `data_manager.py` bypasses logging, so "no token in the robot logs" holds only because a shape-validated token never enters an exception text. The SPA reconciles cloud and robot on login, rosbridge connect and focus (`features/hfToken/syncDecision::decideSync`, with a `taken_over` dampener for two students on one Pi and a circuit breaker). An image without `HF_TOKEN_PATH` (the Jetson, which keeps its read-only classroom token `EDUBOTICS_JETSON_HF_TOKEN`) reports `accepts:false` and is never sent a student token. The legacy `.env` pair `HF_TOKEN` + `HF_TOKEN_MACHINE` is dropped unconditionally by both `config_generator._read_unmanaged_lines` (GUI and Pi) and the GUI removes it at start with one German Protokoll line; the machine stamp, `hf_token_is_foreign`, `EDUBOTICS_HF_TOKEN_ANY_MACHINE` and the Pi's `/hf-token` route are deleted. A student window that ends WITHOUT „Abmelden" is cleared by the Windows GUI (`webview_window.set_exit_callback` → `docker_manager.clear_robot_hf_token_after_window_close`, through the node's own `/register_hf_user`), so the previous student's token does not wait for the next login. Residuals (one token slot per Pi, a plaintext rosbridge websocket on the Pi LAN, a token that outlives a window the GUI cannot watch) are in `docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`.

- **Per-machine `ROS_DOMAIN_ID`: derived every time on Windows, cached on the Pi.** `config_generator::_resolve_ros_domain_id` on both: `EDUBOTICS_ROS_DOMAIN` env clamped to [0, 232] → (Pi only) the persisted `/var/lib/edubotics/.ros_domain_id` → `int.from_bytes(sha256(seed).digest()[:2], "big") % 233` (`tests/test_ros_domain_twin_lockstep.py` pins the 2-byte truncation by AST statement order and asserts both intended divergences, seed source and cache). The seed is a stable machine identity, never `getnode()` first (unstable on multi-NIC/VPN/dock PCs; last resort only): `gui/app/config_generator::_read_machine_id` composes `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Cryptography\MachineGuid` (opened `KEY_WOW64_64KEY`) plus the computer name from `ActiveComputerName`, falling back to `%COMPUTERNAME%` then `socket.gethostname()`; all three go through `_canonical_computer_name` (`upper(strip(name.split('.')[0]))`), itself an invariant, since with no cache the seed alone pins a PC to one domain. The Pi twin reads `/etc/machine-id`; `EDUBOTICS_MACHINE_ID` is the test seam. **Windows has no cache and must not grow one back; the Pi keeps its cache and that is correct.**

### The Orange Pi agent

- **The Pi agent repairs its own systemd units (`SYSTEMD_UNIT_NAMES`), udev rules (`UDEV_RULES_DIR`, `UDEV_RULE_NAMES`, `_drifted_udev_rules`/`_renew_udev_rules`) and opi compose.** `agent.py::_check_system_files_version` byte-compares installed files against the shipped copies and, on drift, `_install_system_file` writes the shipped bytes atomically (temp → fsync → chmod 0644 → `os.replace`, one `.edubotics-bak`, then an fsync of the destination directory) + `systemctl daemon-reload`; **never `restart`, never `enable`, never a unit not already installed**. Repair is per-file via `_install_each`, never `all(install(n) for n in drifted)` (short-circuits). udev renewal runs `udevadm control --reload-rules`, never `udevadm trigger`; a failed reload is non-fatal. **The repair only ever replaces, never creates**, so treat any new system file as undeliverable to the fielded fleet until create-vs-replace is settled (`docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`).

  **`docker-compose.opi.yml` is in that set** (`SHIPPED_COMPOSE_RELPATH`, `_compose_drifted`/`_renew_compose`) via a byte-identical twin at `robotis_ai_setup/pi_agent/docker/docker-compose.opi.yml`. `_shipped_compose_path()` resolves package-relative off `__file__` (repointing it at the `robotis_ai_setup/docker/` copy leaves tests green and kills the feature in the field); `boot()` repairs BEFORE `docker_manager.start_manager`, so the manager adopts the new compose in that boot. The twin's path is derived, never restated: both CI gates read `SHIPPED_COMPOSE_RELPATH` via `.github/scripts/pi_shipped_compose_relpath.py`, so keep that constant a string literal or an `os.path.join()` of literals. **The compose repair is validate-then-swap** (`docker compose -f <tmp> config -q`); isolating that gate from the student's `.env` takes both omitting `--env-file` AND `env=docker_manager._scrubbed_env()`. `_compose_up` passes `--env-file`, the gate deliberately does not; **do not align them**.

  `.s6-keep` cannot join the set (0 bytes) but is required on disk (the compose bind-mounts it), so `setup.sh` creates it when there is no source `docker/` dir.

- **The Pi has no token step any more** (042). `handle_hf_token`, the unauthenticated, LAN-reachable `POST /hf-token` route and `/status`'s `hf_token_saved` are gone, and the System tab's whole token step with them: a Pi student enters the token on the Startseite like a Windows student, and the Pi's own `.env` never holds it (`config_generator._read_unmanaged_lines` drops a legacy `HF_TOKEN` on the next regenerate, i.e. at the next „Umgebung starten"; `setup.sh` and the firstboot script keep their `upsert_env_var("HF_TOKEN", "")` calls as legacy hygiene). A Pi is concurrently multi-tenant but the robot has ONE token slot: a second student's push replaces the first one's, and the SPA's `taken_over` dampener turns that into a recording block plus an explicit „Erneut übertragen" instead of a tug of war (`docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`).

- **Every mutating endpoint acquires `_lifecycle_lock` with a bounded `acquire(timeout=_LIFECYCLE_LOCK_WAIT_S)` and answers a German 503 on failure, never a blocking `with`**: „Umgebung starten" holds it across a resilient image pull and nginx's `proxy_read_timeout` is 3600 s. `_LIFECYCLE_LOCK_WAIT_S` is derived from `_PREVIEW_HANDOFF_TIMEOUT_S` (+1 s), since `stream_camera_preview` legitimately holds the lock across its handoff; background workers (`_run_update_job`) keep the blocking form and `start_robot_tier` pulls with `attempts=1`. `UPDATE_CHECK_TTL_S=600` is paired with `_UPDATE_CHECK_FORCE_MIN_INTERVAL_S=30` and an Origin check on `force` (a no-cors GET sends no Origin, so a looping page amplified against the cloud `/version`). The pi_agent's `docker_manager.py` never runs `compose down` (it deletes `ros_net`).

### Robot control, cameras, env forwarding

- **NOTHING MOVES AT CONTAINER START.** The gate is `EDUBOTICS_REQUIRE_ACTIVATION` (default 1), honoured in FOUR layers that must agree or the gate leaks: `entrypoint_omx.sh` skips its whole Phase 3 (and the boot leader-pose read); `omx_l_leader_ai.launch.py` leaves `joint_trajectory_command_broadcaster` out of the boot spawner; `edu6_arm_node.py::main` calls `boot_energise()` instead of `start_boot_home()`; `activation_agent.py` reports `active` immediately when the gate is off. `0` is the one-variable rollback, and all four normalise the value the same way (the shell's `tr -d '[:space:]'` is load-bearing: `config_generator.upsert_env_var` always quotes, and a `"0 "` once split the readers into gate-on-shell + gate-off-launch). `tests/test_activation_agent.py::TestGateParserLockstep` runs all four over one value table.
  - „A logged-in student" is the ordinary case, not a guarantee: `authGate.js` has its attempt-gated offline escape and rosbridge authenticates nobody. What the gate removes is the UNATTENDED motion; never record the login gate as closing a wire-level hole.
  - **It gates motion, not power.** Servo torque still comes up with the container (both Feetech arms backdrive; the OMX follower is held only by its torque). Energising is itself motionless (`set_torque(True)` seeds `Goal = Present`; the OMX JTC holds its boot pose). Do not "finish the job" by de-energising at boot.
  - **Teleop is held off structurally**: with the broadcaster unspawned, nothing publishes a command on `/leader/joint_trajectory` until activation (bare publishers exist from boot but never write). Reusing `/collision_flag` was rejected (conflates „not activated" with „collision").
  - **`activation_agent.py`** (plain COPY, launched by the entrypoint on every profile) serves `/edubotics/activate` (`std_srvs/Trigger`, returns immediately; the sequence runs on a worker thread) and publishes `/edubotics/activation_state` (`std_msgs/String` + JSON, latched AND 1 Hz, because rosbridge does not always match TRANSIENT_LOCAL).
  - **The sequence is `home → sync → teleop`, order load-bearing** (the broadcaster republishes the leader's live pose at 100 Hz with `time_from_start = 0`, so spawning it while the follower sits at HOME is a step command into a 0.10 rad goal tolerance). Follower-only and Feetech rigs stop after `home`. A Feetech arm homes itself through `/edubotics/home_arm` (the family-neutral alias beside `/edu6/home` | `/edu1/home`); `activation_agent.py::FEETECH_ROBOT_TYPES` picks the branch.
  - **The Jetson is exempt on the UI side only.** Its arm container honours the gate (`docker-compose.jetson.yml` carries the key), so its boot home is gone too; `jetson_agent/agent.py::_move_to_safe_home` already drives `SAFE_HOME_JOINTS` on every claim, and a Jetson has no leader. `ActivationCard` renders `null` while `jetson.status === 'connected'`.
  - Known, accepted: recording/inference/workflow are not blocked on an un-activated rig; on `omx_full` there is no `/leader/joint_trajectory` before activation, so the recorder never leaves its data gate and stops 5 s after START with a German error; the Aufnahme page refuses Start before that („Der Roboter ist nicht aktiviert …", link to the Startseite).

- **ROS 2 `/leader/joint_trajectory` is the command rail.** The follower `arm_controller`'s JTC topic interface is remapped there in `omx_f_follower_ai.launch.py`. Writers: teleop (once the broadcaster is spawned), `activation_agent.py`'s home + leader-sync glides, inference (`communicator.publish_action`), the Roboter Studio `_trajectory_publisher` (the sole workflow-side writer), the server's manual holds/glides, and the collision monitor's recovery moves. **Anything publishing there drives the follower.**

- **Teleop collision e-stop: model-aware effort fractions, student-paced two-step recovery.**
  - dxl11-13 are XL430-W250 (`Present Load` only; declaring `Present Current` on them aborts hardware init → follower dead on every rig, and `EDUBOTICS_COLLISION_ENABLED=0` does not help, the xacro being baked in); dxl14-15 are XL330-M288 (`Present Current`). `collision_detector.effort_fraction_from_values()` normalizes both to 0..1 (Load/1000, Current/1750) and maps unsigned-published int16 back to signed first. The server image must apt-install `ros-jazzy-control-msgs` (else the guard fail-opens silently) and must NOT contain `controller_manager_msgs` (Fast-CDR 2.x ABI mismatch, exit 127).
  - **Detection** (`safety/collision_detector.py`, pure): effort ≥ per-joint threshold AND |velocity| ≤ 0.05 rad/s, debounced 150 ms; firmware Overload bit (0x20) = immediate trip. Thresholds `EDUBOTICS_COLLISION_EFFORT_J1..J5` default 0.30/0.65/0.40/0.30/0.30; gripper excluded. Effort is exposed via a read-only `gpio_command_controller`. Tests: `tests/test_collision_detector.py`, `test_collision_monitor_contract.py`, `test_collision_discard_contract.py`, `physical_ai_server/test/test_collision_workflow_gate_c1_high6.py`; HW runbook `docs/COLLISION_ESTOP_WINDOWS_VALIDATION.md`.
  - **Where it runs (C1-A, round 5, Rule §2 approved):** the monitor's three subscriptions (`/joint_states`, `/leader/joint_states`, gpio) live on the sensor node (depth 32, reliability unchanged), so `_process_gpio_states` — the gates, `effort_fraction_from_values`, `detector.update()`, all unchanged — runs on the sensor thread and a slow record step can no longer make the detector deaf. A trip is handed to the main executor: under `_record_publish_lock` it sets `_collision_trip_pending` and bumps `_record_session_gen`, then triggers the guard condition `_trip_gc`; `_on_trip_gc` (default group, as all trip handling was) performs the unchanged stop once and clears the latch. From the latch on no record step adds a frame or publishes (the record tick re-checks the generation after taking the recorder lock: a straggler step once skipped a „Zurücksetzen" after a resume). `_collision_detector_lock` serialises `update()` with every reset (one helper, `_detector_reset`), and `_collision_watchdog_cb` does nothing while a trip is pending, so a stray False never follows a detected trip. Measured e-stop (spike → `/collision_flag` True, incl. the 150 ms debounce): 146–151 ms, HEAD 151 ms. **The hand-over and the watchdog have their own group** (round 6, owner decision F2, O4's trigger met): `_on_trip_gc` and `_collision_watchdog_cb` run in the node's `_collision_cb_group` (MutuallyExclusive), never queued behind `/task/command` in the default group (e-stop with four commands queued: 148–154 ms). Because the watchdog no longer shares a group with the resync completion, both publish the flag under `_collision_flag_lock`, so a watchdog True can never land after the resync's False. The relax/home/resync timers stay on the default group.
  - **Gating**: OFF during inference (in the detector), OFF during workflow + manual mode (`collision_monitor.py`); `COLLISION_SETTLE_WINDOW_S=0.5` re-arms on every re-torque and mode exit. Leader gate: a trip is dropped when `not leader_appears_active()`, keyed solely on `/leader/joint_states` freshness (2 s), never on `EDUBOTICS_FOLLOWER_ONLY`. **`/leader/joint_states` delivery is safety-load-bearing** (`test_press_trips_in_teleop_recording_mode`).
  - **Recovery is student-paced; the arm never auto-homes**: trip → `/collision_flag=true` (leader-side broadcaster skips publishing; a 5 Hz watchdog re-asserts) → relax-in-place (delayed 0.15 s, re-sent ≤3×, commands the MEASURED pose) → in-progress episode discarded → `COLLISION(7)` → modal Schritt 1 → `HOME_FOLLOWER(9)`: verified quintic glide to `SAFE_HOME_ARM` (`COLLISION_HOMING(8)`); a firmware-latched joint is first recovered on its own thread (`_recover_overloaded_joints`: `reboot_dxl` awaited → settle → hold the measured pose → `set_dxl_torque(True)` awaited; the overload list is cleared only on success; clients on a reentrant group) → `COLLISION_HOMED(9)` → Schritt 2 → `RESUME_TELEOP(8)`: refused until homed, leader proximity ≤0.30 rad, quintic resync, flag clears only after resync. A mid-recording collision resumes the same dataset; `FORCE_RESUME_TELEOP(10)` is the escape hatch, `EDUBOTICS_COLLISION_ENABLED=0` the rollback. `FORCE_RESUME_TELEOP` during a recording ends the session like FINISH (`end_session_now`: finalize + upload per the existing guards) (the finalize runs in `_on_resync_complete` while the arm is still frozen, before `/collision_flag=False`) and then publishes the record status (SAVING, carrying any warning the finish raised: frame loss on save, a missing video file) and its terminating READY, as the record timer's last tick does; a plain resume re-arms the same session (owner decision F1, `collision_monitor.py::_end_interrupted_recording`).

- **React dual build.** `Dockerfile` (student, `REACT_APP_MODE=student`, `REACT_APP_ALLOWED_POLICIES=act`) and `Dockerfile.web` (Railway, `REACT_APP_MODE=web`, full policy list, `${PORT}`, 5 strict security headers re-declared in every nested nginx `location` that sets any `add_header` of its own). `vercel.json` is an intentional empty kill-switch; `railway.json` intentionally has no `buildArgs` (the buildId is stamped as a Railway service variable by `scripts/railway-deploy.sh`; restoring buildArgs re-introduces the `buildId='dev'` regression).

- **Native camera capture bridge (WSL2/Windows): cameras bypass usbipd entirely** (the `vhci_hcd` bridge caps each forwarded UVC camera at ~6-10 Hz and jitters the 100 Hz Dynamixel reads). `EduBotics.exe` captures natively via OpenCV **MSMF** (`CAP_MSMF`; DSHOW rollback via `EDUBOTICS_CAMERA_BACKEND=dshow`; enumeration and capture MUST share the backend or index order scrambles the role pick; on MSMF set only the MJPG fourcc) and streams JPEG over `127.0.0.1:5557`, one connection per camera, wire format `struct ">BIQ"` = `[u8 cam_id][u32 BE jpeg_len][u64 BE capture_ns][jpeg]`, cam_id 0=gripper, 1=scene, 2=phone (spelled identically in `camera_bridge.py` and `camera_ingest_node.py`). `camera_ingest_node.py::_publish` stamps each message with the wire's `capture_ns` (unix-epoch ns), and the recorder maps that stamp onto its own monotonic clock (`communication/capture_timeline.py::ClockMap`), so a frame keeps its capture time however late its callback runs; how large the MSMF stamp jitter and the camera's pipeline latency really are is rig gate G3. `camera_ingest_node.py` republishes as `sensor_msgs/CompressedImage` on `/gripper/image_raw/compressed` + `/scene/image_raw/compressed` (RELIABLE QoS; web_video_server drops BEST_EFFORT publishers silently). Camera roles in the `.env` must be `gripper`/`scene` (`generate_env_file` raises otherwise; `omx_f_config.yaml` hard-codes the topics). Gated by `EDUBOTICS_CAMERA_SOURCE` (auto: `native_bridge` on WSL2, `usb_cam` elsewhere); `EDUBOTICS_CAMERA_SOURCE=usb_cam` is the rollback. Bridge delivery is event-driven and uncapped (a rate cap made the recorder re-grab duplicate frames). The phone-as-3rd-camera path (`phone_camera.py`, HTTPS `:8444` on `0.0.0.0`, unauthenticated by design, body-size gate only; cert from `gui/app/scripts/new_phone_cert.ps1`) rides the same wire as cam_id 2 with a ~2 s staleness gate.

- **`usb_cam` is the Jetson / native-Linux path** (and the WSL2 rollback). Pixel format defaults `yuyv`; `entrypoint_omx.sh` flips it to `mjpeg2rgb` only on WSL2 unless overridden. `raw_mjpeg` is unsupported (usb_cam 0.8.1 falsifies the encoding, green tiles).

- **Two identical-serial cameras anchor by USB topology, three defenses**: `wsl_bridge.list_video_devices()` dedups on `v4l2-ctl Bus info` and downgrades colliding by-id paths to by-path; `entrypoint_omx.sh` hard-exits `[STOPP]` (exit 3) if both configured devices resolve to the same `/dev/videoN` (usb_cam path); native-side, Windows PnP enumeration is filtered to `USB\`-rooted instance IDs.

- **`EDUBOTICS_*` env vars reach a container only if listed in a compose `environment:` list** (compose's `${VAR}` interpolation is config-time). Two CI jobs do different halves: `env-forwarding-guard` proves a name is forwarded SOMEWHERE (a union over all composes; blind to a key missing from one target or on the wrong service); `compose-env-parity` is the per-target, per-service check (student vs opi). `EDUBOTICS_FOLLOWER_ONLY` is forwarded to both student containers for parity, but the server does not read it. **`EDUBOTICS_ROBOT_TYPE` has two readers in two containers**: the server (`robot_profiles.resolve`) and `entrypoint_omx.sh`, which branches the whole arm bringup on it (`case` → `FEETECH_ARM`). It plus the five `EDUBOTICS_EDU6_*` knobs sit on the opi `open_manipulator` list. Defaults: student + opi `omx_full`, Jetson `omx_follower`. The Jetson compose (`jetson_agent/docker-compose.jetson.yml`) forwards `EDUBOTICS_ROBOT_TYPE` on `physical_ai_server` + `EDUBOTICS_FOLLOWER_ONLY=1` on `open_manipulator` and none of the `EDUBOTICS_EDU6_*` knobs; it is outside `compose-env-parity` because it uses compose's bare pass-through form (`- ROS_DOMAIN_ID`), which that parser refuses by design.
### Robot type + the ArmProfile registry

- **Robot type is a setup-time, hardset decision: `EDUBOTICS_ROBOT_TYPE` + the ArmProfile registry.** The old React robot-type picker is gone; re-adding one is a regression (`SetRobotType.srv`/`GetRobotTypeList.srv` deleted). On both platforms the `follower_only=None` derive runs BEFORE the leader-null guard (`pi_agent/config_generator.py` carries an explicit "ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING" comment); reversed, both follower-only profiles raise „Der Leader-Arm muss konfiguriert sein" on every start. Both generators raise the byte-identical German `ValueError` on a contradictory explicit `follower_only=False` for a leader-less profile. Only the Pi validates the id at the emit site (`resolve_robot_type` → unknown becomes `DEFAULT_ROBOT_PROFILE`); Windows emits `robot_type` verbatim and fails open to `follower_only=False`.

  Two Pi/Windows divergences that may not be harmonised: (a) on the both-arms branch the Pi emits `EDUBOTICS_FOLLOWER_ONLY=0` explicitly where the GUI omits it and leans on compose's `${…:-0}` (the Pi leader toggle reads that key back as its rollback anchor, `pi_agent/docker_manager.py::set_leader_mode`'s `prev_val`); (b) the Pi's `upsert_env_var(…, quote=False)` exists for ONE key, `EDUBOTICS_ROBOT_TYPE` (`agent.py::handle_set_robot_type` its only production caller), because `entrypoint_omx.sh` matches it as a bare `case` literal. `quote=False` callers must pass a `resolve_robot_type()` value.

  The server resolves the profile ONCE (`robot_profiles.py::resolve`; unknown/absent → `omx_full` = one-variable rollback). `physical_ai_server.py::__init__` sets `self.operation_mode='collection'` first and outside the try (missing it = AttributeError → 2 s respawn crash-loop), boot-calls `init_ros_params` try/except-degraded (on failure the node stays alive with `communicator=None`, a German `[FEHLER]` and a bounded re-init retry, 3 × 30 s); `_init_robot_profile` stays LAST. `self.robot_type` stays `omx_f` for both OMX profiles (dataset repo naming unchanged). The profile carries the kinematics seam (`build_ik()` consumed by `_build_ik_solver`; HOME/gripper/joint-names/urdf id mirrored from the still-authoritative `motion.py`/`sim_arm.py` constants, locked by a no-drift test).

  Capabilities ride `TaskStatus.robot_profile` + `capabilities_json`: six required booleans `recordable`/`editable`/`trainable`/`inferable`/`roboter_studio`/`has_leader`, plus kinematics keys and the three identity keys `display_de`/`help_de`/`camera_roles` (14 keys on the OMX profiles, 18 on edu6/edu1; `help_de` omitted when empty; `tool_tip_tracks_gripper` sent only when true). The identity keys are what React names a robot by; putting them here rather than in a JS table is deliberate (the registry already exists three times). `test_robot_profiles.py::test_german_identity_matches_the_gui_registry` fences `display_de`/`help_de` against `gui/app/constants.py`. Adding a key is safe on an old client (`isValidCapabilities` checks only the six booleans). There is no steady idle status; hence the React `setTaskInfo` gate and the `phase===READY && !running` editability signal.

  **Adding a robot type** = (1) a server `config/<type>_config.yaml` (glob-shipped; selection key nested `physical_ai_server: → ros__parameters: → <robot_type>:`, equal to the profile's `data_robot_type`); (2) a `ROBOT_PROFILES` entry in `robot_profiles.py` (literal kwargs only; the lockstep test ast-parses it); (3) the two thin descriptors `gui/app/constants.py::ROBOT_PROFILES` and `pi_agent/constants.py::ROBOT_PROFILES` (`display_de` mandatory; `scan_requires_leader == not follower_only`); (4) React assets (a URDF directory under `public/`: `omx-urdf/`, `edu6-urdf/`, `edu1-urdf/`, plus a `URDF_ASSETS` row in `UrdfTwin`); (5) forwarding any new env on the compose lists; (6) new launch/xacro joining the overlay chain, or (Feetech) a spec row in the shared plain-COPY driver + the `entrypoint_omx.sh` family `case` + `activation_agent.py::FEETECH_ROBOT_TYPES`; (7) the family in both `_ARM_MARKERS` copies and both `ARM_USB_IDS` copies (byte-equal, asserted by `pi_agent/tests/test_constants.py`), plus, for a Feetech arm, `_FAMILY_PROTOCOL` and `_FAMILY_SERVO_COUNT` in both scan twins. `edu1_studio` is the worked second example of every step.

  **The registry exists three times (server authoritative + Windows + Pi); `tests/test_robot_profile_lockstep.py` is the one test fencing all three.** The DOF generalisation landed with edu6: every workflow/jog/replay/sim site derives `n = num_arm_joints` (gripper index `n`, Contract-B width `n+2`) through the motion ctx accessors, `ik.num_joints()` and the node's `_profile_n()`; OMX stays bit-identical (golden fixtures `test/fixtures/dof_golden.json`, tolerance 1e-9; `EDUBOTICS_REGEN_GOLDEN=1` regenerates). Two permanent width rails: `extract_points` asserts exact `n+2`, and `Communicator.get_latest_follower_joints` returns exactly `len(FOLLOWER_JOINT_ORDER)`; a grep-guard test keeps `[:5]`-style literals out of the migrated modules. **Since `edu1_studio`, width no longer identifies an arm** (edu1 and OMX are both 7-wide): every place that must know WHICH arm keys on the profile/asset id, never on `n`.

### The profile-aware setup wizard (both platforms)

- **The Pi wizard is profile-aware end to end, every new key additive.** `GET /status` gained `robot_type`, `robot_profiles` (`[{id, display_de, scan_requires_leader, camera_roles}]`) and `hardware_ready`; `POST /robot-type` is the only added route. `hardware_ready` (`_hardware_ready`: follower present, a leader only when `scan_requires_leader`, every arm of the profile's own family) is enforced on both sides (`SystemPage.js` `canStart` is UX; `handle_environment_start` is the fence). `arms_identified.both` stays as it was (a follower-only rig is ready with it `false`). Graceful degrade: an old agent sends none of the keys, so `robot_profiles` → `[]` (rows filtered, not trusted), `scan_requires_leader` read as `!== false`, absent `hardware_ready` falls back to `armsBoth`.

  **Both platforms go singular on a leader-less profile** („Arm scannen" / „Roboterarm"; the Leader step hidden, not disabled). On Windows `gui_app.py::_apply_robot_type_labels` retitles `follower_frame` and packs a second button `btn_scan_arm` (a tkinter widget cannot be re-parented, so `_scan_arms` toggles both). `gui_app.py::ARM_FAMILY_MISMATCH_DE` and the Pi's `agent.py::_ARM_FAMILY_MISMATCH_DE` are byte-equal (`test_gui_robot_type.py::test_the_two_platforms_say_the_same_thing`). Fenced by `tests/test_gui_profile_help.py`.

  **Three scan outcomes, three status codes on the Pi**: a wrong-family arm yields 404 with the cross-family sentence (the by-id filter is family-scoped, so both slots come back `None`); nothing found keeps the generic 404; 409 means right family, wrong arm within it, naming the arm that answered.

  **Camera roles are profile-scoped on both platforms, enforced twice on each.** `camera_roles` is an allowlist: `edu6_studio`/`edu1_studio` declare `('scene',)` and their config YAML backs it with one camera topic (a camera named `gripper` would publish a topic nothing subscribes to, and the compose healthcheck would still report success). The OMX profiles carry both roles in opposite order (`omx_full=('gripper','scene')`, `omx_follower=('scene','gripper')`; the order feeds the Windows `camera_roles[0]` default). Pi: `/status.robot_profiles[]` carries `camera_roles`, `SystemPage.js` filters its `<option>` set from it, and `handle_cameras_roles` re-checks server-side with a German 400 (no `camera_roles` on the wire yields both roles). Windows: `_start_camera_previews` builds the role combobox from the profile, `_on_cameras_changed` assigns the 2-camera pair and caps the selection at `len(camera_roles)`, and `config_generator.generate_env_file` raises „Für den Robotertyp „…" ist die Kamera-Rolle „…" nicht vorgesehen."
