AFTER the pre-existing `('gripper','scene')` guard; `_on_robot_type_changed` re-derives roles on every switch. Fenced by `tests/test_gui_camera_roles.py`. Doctrine differs: `handle_cameras_roles` requires an explicit role on every camera; `_on_cameras_changed` auto-assigns a single camera `camera_roles[0]`; no lockstep test ties the two.

  The two `fast_rehydrate_arms` twins (`gui/app/device_manager.py`, `pi_agent/identify_arm.py`) are signature-locked by `tests/test_arm_scan_twin_lockstep.py` (`require_leader` 3rd, `arm_family` 4th).

- **Crossing arm families invalidates a scan on both platforms.** Nothing new is persisted: the recorded PORT is the family tag (the scan only stores a by-id path that matched that family's `_ARM_MARKERS`), read back by the twin pure predicate `serial_path_family_conflict` (`tests/test_arm_family_conflict_twin_lockstep.py`). It refuses on PROOF only and all three fail-open branches are load-bearing: a path matching the family we need is fine; a path matching no family means the markers are incomplete, not that the arm is wrong; a family with no markers is unjudgeable (fail-closed would make one forgotten checklist line a permanently unstartable profile), paired with a CI assertion that every registry family has markers on both platforms. The Pi's eager `.env` write (`_persist_env_if_ready`) skips on a conflict, leaving the last coherent scan in place.

### edu6_studio: the 6-DOF Feetech arm

7 × STS3215/STS3250 12 V on a Waveshare CH343P adapter (USB `1A86:55D3`, `cdc_acm` → `/dev/ttyACM*`), follower-only, **Roboter Studio only** (`inferable` false: the Inferenz tab renders, START refuses in German). Geometry from the `follower_arm_modified_final1` SolidWorks export, shipped as `public/edu6-urdf/edu6.urdf`. **Rig gates R3/R4/R5/R7/R8/R10 are open; read `docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md` before trusting any physical claim.** Nothing edu6 is hardware-validated on an Orange Pi.

- **Joint 5 is asymmetric (−90°…+110°)**; the strict-vertical grasp family is `q4=0, q5=π/2−q2−q3`. The URDF number is a description; the servo-EEPROM `Min/Max_Position_Limit` written by `tools/edu6_provision.py` is the hardware floor.
- **`workflow/edu6_ik.py`** is closed-form, pure NumPy, exact. World frame = URDF rotZ(π): `solve()` takes world xyz → URDF-native joints, `fk()` returns the world fingertip TCP at `_L_TOOL` 0.1724 m. Profile reach `reach_inner_m 0.09` / `reach_outer_m 0.21`.
- **Jaw/roll contract: `q6 = wrap(π − roll)`** (motion's shared formula, 90° default). q6 is folded into [−90°, +90°] (`_fold_jaw`, nearest to ZERO, never the seed): the edu6 jaws are a parallel pair symmetric about the roll axis, so `q6` and `q6∓π` are the same grasp (the OMX's are not).
- **`roll` and the roll JOINT are different numbers on edu6.** Both solvers expose `roll_from_joints(joints)` (OMX identity; edu6 `wrap(π − q6)`) and both call sites use it (`physical_ai_server.py`'s Cartesian jog, which also pins `arm_q[roll_idx]`, and `motion.py`'s lift).
- **The gripper channel is the end_gear servo angle in radians**, 0 = closed … 1.75 = open. `object_catalog.parse_catalog` takes a per-family `gripper_close_range` (`(0.0, 1.75)` edu6, `(0.0, 0.90)` edu1); the OMX negative-close rule is unchanged when absent.
- **`docker/open_manipulator/edu6_arm_node.py` + `feetech_bus.py`** are plain COPYs, byte-verified by parity. **The arm mixes servo models by design: joints 1/4/5/6/7 STS3215 (Model 777), high-load joints 2/3 STS3250 (Model 2825)**, register-identical, so every identity check accepts `feetech_bus.STS_ACCEPTED_MODELS`. Never hardcode 777.
- **`/joint_states` is created only after the boot probe succeeds**: ping 1..7, accepted model, and the EEPROM provisioning fingerprint (`Min/Max_Position_Limit` equal to the designed window via the shared `feetech_bus.position_limit_window`, `Operating_Mode` 0), so an unprovisioned/RMA-swapped/wheel-mode arm refuses to boot in German; the compose healthcheck is topic-existence-only. Consequence: **flipping `EDUBOTICS_EDU6_JOINT_SIGNS` requires re-provisioning.** The probe also refuses `Phase` bit 4 (multi-turn) and a boot `Present_Position` plausibility band (`EDUBOTICS_EDU6_BOOT_POS_TOL_TICKS`, default 400, ≥4096 disables; a no-op on the full-circle joints 4/6); the 5 s retry loop self-heals. HOME is `[0, 0.70, −2.40, 0, 0.70, 0]` + gripper 1.75, self-homed by a 3 s quintic.
- **The STS position loop is not wrap-aware** (naive `goal − present` over 0..4095, so a joint at tick 12 with the goal at 4090 drives the long way). Three guards, each with a `0` disable:
  - **Torque-ON edge refusal**: `EDUBOTICS_EDU6_EDGE_MARGIN_TICKS` (internal `EDGE_REFUSE_MARGIN_TICKS`, default 40; ≥2048 rejected back to default). Every torque-ON transition is refused while ANY joint rests that close to the tick-0/4095 edge, evaluated in `_seed_goal_from_present_locked` gated on `self._torque_on and not self._bus_fault`, the single choke point before any register write. Never judge an already-torqued arm (that broke jogging); the judged set is every joint.
  - **Post-energise abort**: `EDUBOTICS_EDU6_TORQUE_ABORT_TICKS` (default 2048 = `TICKS_PER_REV // 2`, definitional). Right after `Torque_Enable = 1`, `_verify_after_energise_locked` re-reads `Present_Position` (`POST_ENERGISE_READ_ATTEMPTS = 2`) and writes `Torque_Enable = 0` (`_TORQUE_OFF_PAYLOAD`) if it moved more than that from the EEPROM-clamped goal; not gated on the OFF→ON transition, so it covers an optimistic `_torque_on` after a brown-out. An abort forces `_torque_on = False` inside the bus lock and drops the trajectory outside it; **`_traj_lock` is never nested inside `_bus_lock`**.
  - **Commanded-tick clamp**: `EDUBOTICS_EDU6_GOAL_EDGE_MARGIN_TICKS` (default 128, ceiling 246 inclusive) in `rad_to_command_tick`, sole caller `_write_targets`. `_seed_goal_from_present_locked` is deliberately exempt (it writes a measured tick to hold a limp arm still).

  Residual: a joint forced across the edge while already torqued has no torque-on event; a continuous watchdog was assessed, not implemented (needs Rule §2 sign-off). `PHYSICAL_TORQUE_REFUSALS` + `BOOT_PHYSICAL_REMEDY_DE` declare which kinds (`edge`, `wrongway`) skip the retry rail.
- **`set_torque(True)` seeds `Goal_Position = Present_Position` before `Torque_Enable`** (else the servo drives to its stale RAM goal) and fails the torque-on on a failed read; boot torque-on retries `BOOT_TORQUE_ON_ATTEMPTS` (3). Boot-home arrival is verified (0.30 rad, arm joints only, one bounded re-send, soft German `[WARNUNG]`); every deliberate trajectory write goes through the single writer `_replace_trajectory`, which bumps `_traj_gen`, and the verifier bails rather than re-sending if that generation moved.
- **Trajectories are refused** while torque is off (re-torque also clears the buffer), under a latched bus fault, and whole on any non-finite value; accepted ones execute by 50 Hz interpolating SYNC_WRITE. The `/leader/joint_trajectory` subscriber is VOLATILE+RELIABLE (TRANSIENT_LOCAL never matches the VOLATILE publishers). Torque service `/edu6/set_torque` + the legacy alias `/dynamixel_hardware_interface/set_dxl_torque`; torque-off on SIGTERM/atexit (no servo watchdog: a dead host leaves the arm energised). ~1 s wall-clock of sync-read misses = latched hard stop (`READ_FAIL_STOP_S`), cleared only by a full 7-servo read. `Present_Load` is published as `JointState.effort` (a PWM fraction, normalized by `PRESENT_LOAD_FULLSCALE = 1000.0`, times `JOINT_SIGNS`); nothing consumes it today (rig gates R4/R6).
- **Register 40 is not a boolean**: `Torque_Enable` max is 128, a re-datum COMMAND that silently corrupts the calibration. `feetech_bus.assert_safe_torque_enable` refuses any write putting ≠ 0/1 into register 40 from both `write` and per-servo `sync_write`, before framing, as a SPAN test (the driver's own 7-byte goal block starts at register 41); it raises `ValueError`, deliberately not `FeetechBusError`.
- **`Angular_Resolution` (register 30) warns in German and still boots.** Every unnamed status-byte bit is reported by value off a table-derived mask; `0x02` keeps `'Winkel-Sensorfehler'`, `0x10` is deliberately unnamed.
- **Provisioning is calibrate-once-forever** (`tools/edu6_provision.py`), in order: `Response_Status_Level`→1 first when a servo ships 0 (un-awaited) → torque-off (verified) → explicit `Lock=0` → `Phase` bit-4 clear before the offset → jig-pose `Homing_Offset` (re-read gate ±4 ticks) → designed URDF limits (`position_limit_window`) → safety EEPROM (gripper `Max_Torque 150`, arm 800, `Operating_Mode=0`) → re-lock → read-back verify → final all-servo jig re-verify → sha256'd record as the commit point (local json + `edu6_arm_records` upsert keyed by the vendor sticker serial).
- **Hand-guide is torque-off limp by explicit user decision**; the arm falls when released. The jog-time self-collision guard is deferred to rig gate R7.
- **Scan**: the `ARM_USB_IDS` family table (`edu6` PID-pinned); `identify_arm.py --protocol=feetech --servos=7` proves identity by the servos answering (ping 1..7, id 8 silent, accepted model), with cross-family hints surfaced as one German sentence (`device_manager.LAST_SCAN_NOTICE`), fired presence-based (`_set_cross_family_presence_notice`, Windows-side enumeration of the other family). **`partial:N` counts contiguous ids from 1, not hits** (a hole is a mid-chain fault). The three PS allowlists (`configure_usbipd`/`verify_system`/`install_prerequisites`) accept both families.
- **The Orange-Pi scan is the same idea through a different mechanism.** `pi_agent/constants.py::ARM_USB_IDS` is byte-equal to the GUI's; a Pi has no usbipd step (every arm is already under `/dev/serial/by-id`), so family scoping is the by-id substring filter `identify_arm._ARM_MARKERS`. `identify_arm_via_docker` prefers the prober's STDOUT (exit code and stderr matter only when stdout is empty). The permission floor is `pi_agent/udev/99-edubotics-robotis.rules` (`1a86:55d3` → `GROUP="dialout", MODE="0660"`, no `SYMLINK` by design), in the self-repaired set. `_FEETECH_BYID_MARKERS` (six substrings, shared by `edu6` and `edu1` in `_ARM_MARKERS`) is an admitted guess, not a measurement (rig gate R1).
- **Cloud Contract-B is width-tagged, and the tag is not the width**: `validate_trajectory(..., robot_profile=)` in `validators/workflow.py`, `TRAJECTORY_ROBOT_PROFILES`: 7-wide for `omx_f`/untagged AND `edu1_studio`, 8-wide for `edu6_studio`, unknown ids 400 in German; the tag rides `workflow_trajectories.robot_profile` (035, widened by 039). Never "simplify" that table into a length lookup. Vormachen's keep stamps every saved „Bewegung" with `taskStatus.robotType` (the `data_robot_type` id space, NOT `taskStatus.robotProfile`); on replay `RunControls.normalizeTrajectory` refuses a cross-profile run client-side with a German toast.
- **`collision_enabled=False` skips the e-stop ROS wiring** on both Feetech profiles (safe only because nothing on those rigs publishes `/collision_flag`); both OMX profiles stay True forever.

### edu1_studio: the 5-DOF Feetech „Edu:1"

6 × STS3215/STS3250 on the same CH343P adapter (USB `1A86:55D3`), follower-only, **Roboter Studio only** (`has_leader` false). Geometry from the `5dof_assembly_urdf2` SolidWorks export, shipped as `public/edu1-urdf/edu1.urdf`. **Rig gates E1-E9 are open; nothing below is hardware-validated**, every number derives from CAD + shipped meshes.

- **One Feetech driver serves both arms.** `edu6_arm_node.py` keeps its name and gains an `if IS_EDU1:` block that rebinds `SERVO_IDS` / `JOINT_NAMES` / `HOME_JOINTS_RAD` / `GRIPPER_OPEN_RAD` / `JOINT_LIMITS_RAD` / `_DEFAULT_SIGNS` / `TORQUE_SERVICE` / `GEOMETRY_SPEC` from `_EDU1_*` literals. The edu6 constants stay plain literals under their historic names (the deps-free suite executes module-level assignments in order and `test_edu6_geometry.py` `literal_eval`s `HOME_JOINTS_RAD`). The override must stay ABOVE `JOINT_SIGNS = _parse_signs(...)`; `N_ARM_JOINTS` is derived from `JOINT_NAMES`. The per-rig knobs are the existing `EDUBOTICS_EDU6_*` env vars (one arm per rig). `entrypoint_omx.sh` decides the family once in a `case` (`FEETECH_ARM`).
- **`edu1` is its own arm family, USB-indistinguishable from `edu6`** (same adapter, VID:PID, by-id strings, servo models on ids 1..6). `_ARM_MARKERS['edu6'] is _ARM_MARKERS['edu1']`, so `serial_path_family_conflict` can never claim a stored Feetech port belongs to the other Feetech family. **The only discriminator is the servo count**: the prober takes `--servos=N` and asserts it exactly (every id up to N answers, id N+1 does not); a bus of another supported length returns that family's cross-probe token. Six answering servos are ambiguous by construction (a healthy Edu:1 vs an edu6 whose gripper dropped off), and the German sentence owns that ambiguity.
- **The count verdicts are three tokens, framed by `device_manager.feetech_bus_length_notice` (byte-equal on the Pi twin)**: `partial:N` (fewer than this arm has, matching no other arm) → the connector/power sentence; `bus_too_long:N` (extra devices, a duplicated id; N is a floor, so the German says „mindestens"); `partial:N` with N ≥ the selected length proves the in-container image is OLDER than the host (an old script ignores `--servos=`) → the „Abbild aktualisieren" sentence. A family name the prober returns that is not the selected family is a REFUSAL, never a follower. The USB-presence notice cannot name which EduBotics arm, so `_CROSS_NOTICE_OMX_WHILE_FEETECH` / `_CROSS_NOTICE_FEETECH_WHILE_OMX` are family-generic; both twins share one `_CROSS_NOTICES` table keyed `(found, selected)`.
- **The claw is a rotating two-blade claw, not a parallel jaw**; the shipped URDF flips `RL_joint`'s axis (and its `LF_joint` `<mimic>`) so the band runs 0 = closed … +1.5708 = open, because every shared consumer assumes open > closed numerically. `gripper_mm_per_rad` is deliberately omitted (the gap is affine in the angle).
- **The TCP is the closed fingertip** (`_L_TOOL` = 0.02125 + 0.065 = 0.08625 m below the `end_effector` origin). The tip's height varies with the opening, so „Tisch vermessen" must be taught with the claw CLOSED: an instruction, not a guard, carried by the additive `tool_tip_tracks_gripper` capability into an extra German step in `TableTouchStep`. A future rotating-claw arm must set the flag, not add a second special case.
- **`workflow/edu1_ik.py`** is closed-form, pure NumPy, exact. joint2/3/4 are parallel, so a strict-vertical tool is one scalar equation in `q2+q3+q4`; `q4` is determined and the rest is a textbook 2R. World frame = URDF rotZ(π), and **`base_yaw` returns the azimuth, not `theta1`**: joint1's axis is `(0,0,−1)`, so `solve` emits `theta1 = −base_yaw`. `_FK_TOL_M` is 1e-5 (this CAD export rounded its right angles; at 1e-6 the solver rejected every solve).
- **The jaw fold is mandatory here** (joint5 spans ±90° while a tag presents any yaw), legitimate because the two blades are mirror images about the claw's own centreline, 0.800 mm off the roll axis; consequence: the two folded twins seat an object 1.600 mm apart along the jaw width axis (`docs/CLAUDE-CHANGELOG.md` for the measurement). Same reference rule as edu6: nearest zero, never the seed.
- **The shipped URDF restores the CAD's 2-decimal right angles to four** (1.57 → 1.5708): joint5's window IS the jaw-fold window, so at ±1.57 a required roll of exactly ±90° fell outside both twins. The URDF header enumerates all five semantic differences from the raw export (the fifth is joint4's widening to ±115°) plus three non-semantic ones (header, two comments, CRLF → LF; normalise before diffing).
- **HOME is `(0, 0.64, 1.48, 0.90, 0)`**: arm upright over its base, claw pointing up, chosen by measurement to maximise every joint's distance from its own limit (binding joint2 at 0.640 rad) under TCP-radius, table- and self-clearance constraints. HOME is outside the solver's image, so the guard on the way to it is the joint-space box model. (Its stated optimality is not independently reproduced; `docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`.)
- **`EDU1_LINK_BOXES`** exists twice (server `arm_geometry.py`, driver `edu6_geometry.py`), AST-compared by `tests/test_edu6_geometry.py`. `_self_pairs(n_links)` is sized per instance (the two tables have different lengths). base_link spans z ∈ [0, 0.0481] m, so z = 0 is the table. Soundness is structural (each box is an AABB of its link's mesh in its own frame).
- **joint4 is ±115° (`2.0071`), not the ±90° it shipped with.** Treat ±115° as a ceiling (owner's statement); `2.0071` rounds inward. **Declared four times and cross-pinned**: `edu1_ik.py`, the shipped URDF, `edu6_arm_node.py`, `tools/edu6_provision.py`; reverting one alone fails a test. **It is a hardware-provisioning change**: the boot probe verifies the EEPROM window, so a limit that moves without `tools/edu6_provision.py --arm edu1` being re-run makes the arm refuse to boot in German.
- **The elbow-down branch is never selected**, by dominance (elbow-down legal ⇒ elbow-up legal, and elbow-up is tried first), no longer by limits; the posture set is unchanged, which every clearance figure and `path_guard`'s "no elbow flip" rest on.
- **The reroute ladder was re-measured, not inherited**: `safe_travel_z_m=0.075`, `swing_heights_m=(0.05, 0.07, 0.03)`, `swing_radii_m=(0.18, 0.28, 0.14)`, `tool_clear_m=0.0` (the EE frame IS the TCP). Chosen under the old ±90° ceiling and deliberately unchanged; raising the cruise to exploit the taller ceiling is a reroute behaviour change needing its own measurement.
- **The student reach ring is the intersection of the grasp plane and the hover, not the grasp plane alone** (`reach_inner_m=0.07`, `reach_outer_m=0.35`); checking only the grasp plane once shipped a rim solvable only 18 mm up against the catalog's 60 mm `approach_clear_m`.
- **`tools/edu6_provision.py --arm edu1`** writes the 6-servo EEPROM windows. `provision()` takes the arm spec as a required argument; the spec carries `profile_id` + `display_de`, and `profile_id` is the only machine-readable family tag in the `edu6_arm_records` artifact (deliberately not in `record_checksum`). `apply_arm_spec` rebinds `SERVO_IDS`/`JOINT_LIMITS_RAD` (AST-compared against the driver's literals) and `provision` asserts BOTH halves agree, so a half-migration fails loudly instead of writing one family's window under the other's tag.

### Server packaging + cloud invariants

- **Recording RAM is bounded by upstream `streaming_encoding=True`** (`lerobot_dataset_wrapper._apply_edubotics_defaults`): frames stream to ffmpeg as they arrive.
- **Roboter Studio is bolted onto `physical_ai_server`, not a separate container.** The thin Dockerfile rebuilds `physical_ai_interfaces` (the base predates the Workshop srvs) and installs `opencv-contrib-python==4.10.0.84` + `pupil-apriltags==1.0.4` (`CMAKE_POLICY_VERSION_MINIMUM=3.5` for CMake 4). Interfaces changes require an interfaces rebuild: the CI image build, never a workstation push.
- **Daten 2.0 runs beside the node in processes of its own, and the node never does their work.** The wire constants are `daten/contract.py` above its marker line, mirrored name-for-name by the page's `datenContract.js` (`tests/test_daten_contract_lockstep.py`, which also compares the nginx blocks' port).
  - **The sidecar** `daten/http_server.py` (the bringup launch's `daten_http` `ExecuteProcess`: `python3 -m physical_ai_server.daten.http_server` with `prefix='nice -n 10'`, respawn 2 s; priority from the PREFIX, never `os.nice`, which changes only the calling thread after the imports started theirs) listens on `contract.HTTP_PORT` 8095 inside the container and is reached only through nginx's `location /daten-api/` over `ros_net` (no compose publishes it). It imports neither rclpy/`physical_ai_interfaces` nor LeRobot (`tests/test_daten_sidecar_no_ros.py`); every byte-serving, parquet-reading, PyAV-muxing, hint-computing and hub-reading step runs there, so none can take the node's GIL. It reads, with ONE exception (T2-1, owner: „remember the state first", narrowly reversing S-1's „no adopt writer"): the sync record of a dataset WITHOUT one that a decision (`library.sync_map`, `hubstate`) found `current` is written by the background thread `daten-remember` (`library.py::remember_if_current` → `queue_remember` → `hub_sync.remember_identical`), after the reply and never inside a request, in the node or in the record tick: exactly what a download or an upload writes, only on proof (the same file set under `data/`, `meta/`, `videos/` as the hub listing at that head and every file hashed equal to its `lfs.sha256` / git sha1, videos included), only for the folder's own repo, never over a record that names another repo or is synced (`tag_ok: false` included), never with a crash marker, the final check and the write under the dataset's stage lock and only if nothing changed since the hashing began; once per state (keyed by digest, head and a stat signature). The step never waits for that lock, and `hub_sync.stage_lock` re-opens the lock file on every attempt and waits up to `STAGE_LOCK_WAIT_S` (1 s), so its short hold never refuses an edit, a download or a delete. A Start writes no record (the session's upload does). ANY local change before the Daten tab first sees the dataset identical to its hub copy (a recording with upload off, an edit through the old page, an edit before the step ran) leaves it without a sync record; a later conflict then merges as the union, and `keepBoth.tipNoBase` says so (R2-5). Routes under `/daten-api/v1`: `health`, `lib/<T>/library`, `lib/<T>/hub/probe`, `ds/<T>/summary|hubstate|episode/<i>/data|episode/<i>/video/<cam>.mp4|thumb.jpg`; GET/HEAD only (any other method 405), errors are JSON `{"error": code}` that never echo a path; at most `MAX_CONNECTIONS` (32) sockets with `SOCKET_TIMEOUT_S` (30), beyond that 503 + `Retry-After: 2`; a media pool (`MEDIA_*`: 2 workers, `MEDIA_QUEUE_MAX` = `MAX_CONNECTIONS` waiting, 20 s — a request the connection cap admits waits for a build slot and never meets a full queue, T2-6) and a separate hub pool (`HUB_*`: 2/8/20 s), so a slow hub never holds a media slot. Its hub reads (`daten/hub_reads.py`) are read-only as the robot's current token, bounded by `HUB_CALL_TIMEOUT_S` through a REQUEST hook on the client that lowers, never raises, a caller's timeout (a client-level timeout is not enough: `list_repo_tree`, `list_datasets`, `dataset_info`, `repo_exists` pass `timeout=None` per request), and cached in one LRU keyed `(repo_id, head)` (`HUB_CACHE_MAX` 512, no TTL: a head is immutable); the namespace listing is never cached.
  - **Link tokens** (`daten/link_tokens.py`) are minted ONLY by the node (`/daten/command` action `link`) and verified by the sidecar, so rosbridge stays the only root of trust: `v1.<payload>.<HMAC-SHA256[:32]>`, scopes `lib` and `ds:<ns>/<name>`, `TOKEN_TTL_S` 1800, constant-time compare, the 32-byte secret shared through `/run/edubotics-daten/secret` (dir 0700, file 0600, published atomically). A `ds` route takes its dataset from the token, never from the URL. They exist because a `<video>`/`<img>` GET carries no Origin.
  - **The download worker** `daten/download_worker.py` (`nice -n 10`, `HF_HUB_DISABLE_XET=1` so bytes land progressively, its own session) is the ONE dataset download: the page's `new`/`replace`/`copy`, the recorder's D14/D7 `sync` and „Beide behalten"'s `keep`/`base` (`hub_sync.download`: every file checked against the hub's `lfs.sha256` or git sha1, swapped in with its record). It reads the token once, requires the request's fingerprint, and ends itself (`token_changed` / `orphaned`) when the token changes or its parent dies — never in the middle of a swap. It is not the HF worker, so a Daten download never blocks `/register_hf_user`; Daten UPLOADS do run in the HF worker and block the token handover like any upload.
  - **The node side** is `daten/node_service.py::DatenService`, built in `PhysicalAIServer.__init__` after `_init_ros_service`; a failure leaves `node.daten = None` and the node lives on (recording never depends on Daten, R-18). It serves `/daten/command` (`DatenCommand.srv`, its own MutuallyExclusive group; `link | edit | delete_dataset | upload | download | keep_both | cancel | state`; every callback is local and fast and makes no network call: network work runs in the HF or download worker, edits in `edit_worker.py` at `nice -n 19`, and the node's one hub call of its own, the slot token's account for the `namespace` refusal, is a bounded whoami in the background thread `daten-account` (`_refresh_account`, started by the 1 Hz state tick and by `_account`, once per token fingerprint, a failure retried after `ACCOUNT_RETRY_S` 30 s; `_account` uses an account only for the fingerprint now in the slot, V2-16)) and publishes `/edubotics/daten_state` (RELIABLE + TRANSIENT_LOCAL depth 1, 1 Hz from its own group and at once on every job change; `busy` lists every `(id, kind)` holding a dataset, so a Start waiting for its dataset's upload is listed `record` AND `upload`, in that order). Its LEASE registry (one lock held only around in-memory reads and dict writes, never across a file-system call or a worker call) answers `busy_kind(path)`; one edit-class job and one download run at a time; per-dataset lock files are taken by each STAGE's own process, never by a job across stages; boot recovery finishes or undoes a swap from its rename state alone (`hub_sync.recover`). The bringup launch sets `HF_HUB_DISABLE_TELEMETRY=1` FIRST, for every process (huggingface_hub 1.23 otherwise sends one hidden request before a process's first hub call).
- **`.s6-keep` is load-bearing.** `physical_ai_server` bind-mounts the 0-byte `./physical_ai_server/.s6-keep` at `/etc/s6-overlay/s6-rc.d/user/contents.d/physical_ai_server:ro`; that mount is what starts the ROS node. Remove it and s6 reports healthy while the node never runs.
- **The thin overlay disables the s6-agent service because it deletes that service's app.** The strip-bloat block `rm -rf /opt/talos_system_manager` (dev-only) would leave `docker/s6-agent/run`'s `uvicorn talos.agent.s6_agent:app` crash-looping, so the same `RUN` also `rm -f`s `…/user/contents.d/s6-agent`; keep the two together.
- **Single uvicorn worker on Railway** (`--workers 1`, explicit in the Dockerfile CMD). The in-process rate limiter and the three background sweeps (dataset reconciliation, Jetson lock sweeper, training-cancel retry) are process-local; raising the worker count silently doubles every quota.
- **Boot-time schema fingerprint.** `cloud_training_api/app/main.py::_validate_required_schema()` probes exactly 15 tables, 11 column sets and 21 RPCs (040 added the `workflow_submissions` table and `code_files, code_language` on both `workflows` and `workflow_versions`; 041 changed no count, only the `update_workflow_code` probe's argument set, so an API on a database without 041 fails its own `/health`; 042 added `user_hf_credentials`, its column set and `store_user_hf_credential`, pinned by `test_schema_probe_040.py` and `test_schema_probe_042.py`; 045 changed no count, only the writer's probe, which names `p_expected_fp`, so an API on a database without 045 fails its own `/health`; 043, 044 and 046 change no API-visible shape and are not probed) as a background startup task; `/health` returns 503 `status:starting` until it passes, SIGTERM on failure, errors secret-scrubbed (`_sanitize_probe_error`). **Add any new table/column/RPC to the probe.** `EDUBOTICS_SKIP_SCHEMA_CHECK=1` is for unit tests only.
- **Two-step Jetson pairing + exclusive claim** (`/pair-intent` → `intent_token` → `/pair`; RPCs `claim_pair_intent`, `pair_jetson`, `claim_jetson`). The `release-beacon` route is the only one accepting a token in the body, re-validated via `supabase.auth.get_user`; do not generalize that pattern.
- **Two-phase Modal cancel + derived credits.** `/trainings/cancel` writes `status='cancel_requested'` (not terminal), then attempts the Modal cancel (blocking API in `asyncio.to_thread`; never `.aio()` on a `from_id` handle); `training_sweep.py` retries every 30 s up to `MAX_CANCEL_ATTEMPTS` (5), then flips to `failed` (that give-up transition also runs synchronously inside `/trainings/cancel` when the counter fills). **Credits are derived, never counted**: every credit check counts `status NOT IN ('failed','canceled')`, so flipping a row terminal IS the refund; workgroup members share `workgroups.shared_credits` atomically inside `start_training_safe`. GPU routing is single-sourced in `services/policy_profile.py` + `modal_app.py`: ACT-class → L4 `train` (7 h cap), VLA (pi0/pi05/pi0_fast/smolvla) → A100 `train_vla` (11 h cap); `pi0_fast`'s base repo is the hyphen form `lerobot/pi0fast-base`. The cloud enforces its own `ALLOWED_POLICIES` + `POLICY_MAX_TIMEOUT_HOURS` (act/vqbet/tdmpc 4, diffusion/pi0_fast 6, pi0/pi05/smolvla 10) + `MAX_STEPS` 500k / `MAX_BATCH_SIZE` 256. `/trainings/start` dedupes within `DEDUPE_WINDOW` (60 s), and the timeout-cap + VLA-tuning mutations run BEFORE the dedupe compare. Self-healing in `_sync_modal_status`: `STALLED_WORKER_MINUTES` (25) / `DISPATCH_LOST_MINUTES` (10) auto-fail.
- **Cloud-API `/health` and `/version`**: `/health` returns `{status, commit, schema_ok, boot_completed_at, version}`; deploy gates assert `commit == github.sha`. `/version` derives `download_url` from `GUI_VERSION` + `GUI_RELEASE_REPO` and returns the additive `pi_agent_download_url` + `pi_agent_sha256`, consumed by `pi_agent/update_checker.py`, which advertises no update when the hash is empty. Cloud API URL: `https://scintillating-empathy-production-1068.up.railway.app`.
- **HF identity anchor + GDPR deletion.** `users.hf_username` (migration 030) is the sole per-student anchor for dataset discovery (`/datasets/sync` + the sweep); never guess authors from `trainings`/`datasets` rows. RLS-recursion lesson (031): never let a `users`/`classrooms` policy read those tables directly; use the `rls_*` SECURITY DEFINER helpers; Supabase Realtime is the canary.

  Deletion is asymmetric by design: teacher-initiated `DELETE /students/{id}` attempts to erase the student's HF repos (skipping workgroup-shared ones); self-service `/me/delete` only cancels trainings, deletes the caller's stored Hugging Face token (best effort, never blocking: the response says so in `hf_token_removed` and names it in the message only when it happened) and writes a `deletion_requested_at` that nothing reads. The platform `HF_TOKEN` does not own repos in a student's namespace, so the PARTIAL outcome is the ordinary one until the org-namespace change lands. `delete_student`'s order is reversible-first: `_assert_student_owned` → enumerate the HF candidates read-only (`_enumerate_student_hf_artifacts`) → 503 and delete nothing if the `datasets` registry is unreadable → `auth.admin.delete_user` → erase the enumerated repos → build the response. Five outcomes: 503; account delete fails → 500 with nothing erased; all erased → 200 `hf_erasure_complete: true`; failures → 200 `false` + `hf_failures`; and **`attempted == 0` → 200 `hf_erasure_complete: false`** with a German `detail` (the endpoint must never claim an erasure after contacting HuggingFace zero times). `StudentRow.handleDelete` surfaces it on an explicit `=== false` (an older API sends a bare `{ok:true}`). `_delete_student_hf_artifacts` returns `(failures, attempted)`; the `datasets` registry is the only source of the shared-repo skip list; `RepositoryNotFoundError` counts as success. Migration 038 made `trainings.user_id` `ON DELETE CASCADE` (training history goes with the account). A persisted erasure-retry queue remains the durable answer for a partial HF failure after the account is gone. When a credential exists the anchor is PROVEN against the API (`whoami` at save time) and `PATCH /me` answers 409; a direct PostgREST UPDATE of one's own `users` row can still overwrite it (`docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`), so "proven" is a statement about the API, not about the column. Without a credential the anchor stays self-asserted, and removing the token keeps the proven `hf_username`.
## Roboter Studio (layer 9): consolidated reference

The programming surface for a follower-only kit: one follower + one scene camera. Everything below lives in `physical_ai_server/workflow/` + `components/Workshop/` unless noted.

**A workflow is written in ONE notation, chosen at creation and immutable after** (`Blöcke` / `Python` / `Java`). A code workflow's `blockly_json` holds its Ziele and Positionen and NOTHING else (`{}` or `{"edubotics-destinations": …}`, migration 041) and it never mounts the Blockly canvas; a Blockly one keeps `code_files = {}`. Code does not ride inside `blockly_json`: it is two columns on the same PATCH, which is why the SAVE/RUN allowlists of `blocklyPayload.js` are untouched. The code-row rule is enforced three times: the DB CHECK `workflows_code_blockly_json_destinations_only` (the floor), `update_workflow_code`'s own 22023 refusal, and `routes/workflows.py::_code_blockly_json_ok` answering in German first; a PATCH that carries a code row's `blockly_json` must carry its `code_files` too (`_CODE_ZIELE_NEED_FILES_DE`), so the Ziele and the files are one call and one version snapshot.

### The coding suite (Python + Java)

- **The run payload routes on `language`, and an OLD image must fail loud.** `WorkflowManager.start` parses `language` BEFORE `Interpreter.from_json`; `'python'`/`'java'` build a `CodeProgram`, anything else is the existing Blockly path byte-for-byte. An old server reads only `blocks`, so the client sends a **poison block** with it, `edubotics_code_program_v1` (`codeProject.js::CODE_POISON_BLOCK_TYPE`), a type no handler table has ever carried, so that server aborts with „Unbekannter Block-Typ: …" instead of running an empty program green. The client refuses first anyway: `capabilities.code_languages` must NAME the language (absence is not permission).
- **`code_languages` is an additive capability key**, a list and not a boolean, on every profile's manifest (`robot_profiles.py`), because every image that ships the runner runs both. `isValidCapabilities` checks only the six booleans, so an old client is unaffected. A start still refuses in German when the runner container is down (`runner_down`): a static image capability is not a live one.
- **`workflow/robot_api.py` is the ONE table and the single source of five checked-in artifacts.** One `ApiCall` row per handler-table key (a bijection with `STATEMENT_HANDLERS | VALUE_EVALUATORS`, fenced by `test_robot_api.py`), carrying the Python name, the Java spelling, the dispatched block type, each parameter's KIND and RANGE, and a German docstring. Rendered from it and byte-equality-fenced by `test_robot_api_generated.py`: the Python stub (`runner/lib/robot.py`), the Java client (`Robot.java` + `Greifobjekt.java` + `RpcClient.java`), and the editor's `robot_api.json` (autocomplete, each entry carrying its German docstring in the completion's info panel; a command already in the text has no hover help, owner decision R3-O3 in `docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). **`INTERNAL_METHODS` sit in the same shape** (`__hello`, `__line`, `__paused`, `__vars`, `__exit`, `register_object`), so the client renderers and the server validator read one row. **`CODE_ONLY_METHODS` (`zeige`) are public but have no block type**, so the 32-row bijection stays intact; they are rendered into both stubs and `robot_api.json` like any row (Java gets `zeige` overloads for the primitives plus exactly ONE reference overload, `Object`, which `RpcClient.shownObject` dispatches at run time; two reference overloads made `zeige("x", null)` a compile error). **`ApiParam.asset` tags the first parameter of every asset call** (`recording`, `place`, `place_def`, `counter`, `object`, `variable`); the editor's scanner reads the tags from `robot_api.json`, never from a second list. Seven Blockly types deliberately have NO row: `forever` / `wait_until` / `while_visible` are `while` loops in a text program, and the three hats plus `broadcast` mean nothing without hats (a text program uses functions).
- **Two sockets in `code_runner_ipc`, and the split is the security model.** `rpc.sock` is the server's DATA socket, `0666`, the one the student uid reaches; every frame on it is hostile (Rule §2). `runner.sock` is the supervisor's CONTROL socket, `0600`, opened only by the server: `start` (carrying the whole project, hence its own larger `CONTROL_MAX_FRAME_BYTES`), `kill`, `breakpoints`. A per-run token is minted by the server, handed to the supervisor over the control socket, presented in the data socket's `__hello`, and RETIRED the instant the run ends.
- **The Python debugger is PEP 669 `sys.monitoring`, and `restart_events()` is not optional.** Breakpoint ids are `<file>:L<line>` on the EXISTING `WorkflowSetBreakpoints.srv` + `WorkflowStatus.current_block_id` (`components/Workshop/code/codeBreakpoints.js` is the one predicate that tells the two id spaces apart, both directions). The LINE callback returns `DISABLE` for every location without a breakpoint (which is what makes it free), so a breakpoint added mid-run never fires without `sys.monitoring.restart_events()` on every change (measured: 0 hits without it). **That change reaches the runner only because `WorkflowManager.set_breakpoints` forwards it**: the Blockly interpreter sees the rebound frozenset through `ctx.get_breakpoints()`, but a code program runs in another PROCESS, so `CodeProgram.set_breakpoints` writes the `breakpoints` frame on its own control connection, outside `self._lock`, best-effort and never raising, like `stop()`'s `request_stop` (`test_code_program_stop.py::test_a_breakpoint_set_mid_run_reaches_the_runner_on_its_own_connection`). The „where am I" indicator is a SAMPLER THREAD reading the main thread's frame, never a per-line event; only the main thread is debugged. **Java gets run and stop only** (decision A8), and the UI says so (beside the file tree and in the Debug-Panel's code tab), never papering it over as parity.
- **Errors are German at the right line, and the SENTENCE is where the line is named.** `workflow/code_errors_de.py` maps a `kind` (`syntax`, `indentation`, `name`, `robot_method`, `type`, `zero_division`, `index`, `recursion`, `import`, `memory`, `timeout`, `killed`, `compile`, `robot`, `runner_down`, other) to a sentence with `file`/`line` placeholders and never interpolates the CPython/JVM message; `robot_method` adds a `difflib` suggestion from the API names, and when that suggestion IS the name the student wrote (a method this server has and the runner's older stub lacks: mixed image tags) the sentence is `robot_method_version` instead, which says the two are not on the same version and what to do, never „Meintest du robot.zeige?" for `robot.zeige`. **The editor does not highlight the error line, and that is not an oversight to „finish"**: `_raise_error` publishes `current_block_id = <file>:L<line>` with phase `error`, but `useRosTopicSubscription`'s `/workflow/status` callback dispatches `setWorkflowStatus` AND `setRunState('error')`, whose terminal branch nulls `currentBlockId` and blanks `phase`. `CodeWorkspace::highlight` therefore derives only the two kinds a live run reaches (`running`, `paused`); reviving an `error` kind means changing that reducer FIRST (`docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). The raw tool line rides the separate `[TECHNIK] ` entry (Rule §1).
- **A code run's recordings are SCANNED out of the project, and the one thing a code run skips is a name the cloud answers 404 for.** `robot.replay("…")` resolves against the run payload's `trajectories` sibling like every Blockly replay (the server has no other source). `codeProject.js::collectCodeReplayNames` reads the names out of the files (both call shapes; the name rule taken from the generated `replay` row as an ANCHORED pattern, so the 40-char cap binds; capped at `MAX_CODE_REPLAY_NAMES` = migration 034's per-workflow prune cap of 16) and `RunControls` fetches them through the existing Contract-C path. It also takes every name the editor's exact scanner reports for `replay` (`scanCodeAssets(…).replay`), FIRST — and among those, the names with at least one call outside a comment before the names seen only in comments (`RunControls::codeReplayNames`, the same predicate as its save-first check; review round 5, md6: sixteen commented names crowded out the one really played) — so the cap never cuts a real call for a loose hit and a call the one-line scan cannot see (`robot.replay(speed=2,` with `name="…"` on the next line) is fetched too (review round 4, mc7). **Loudness is asymmetric in exactly one cell, split by CAUSE, never by caller**: a block tree's names are exact, a text scan can hit a comment or an unrelated string, so a code run drops a name the cloud SAYS it does not have (`WorkflowApiError.status === 404`, the `NO_SUCH_RECORDING` sentinel) and lets the server speak if the call is really made. Everything else is loud on both paths, because „wir konnten nicht fragen" is not evidence of a phantom (a timeout or dead connection is status `0`, a 5xx/401 its own code; the same split as `isCloudUnreachableAuthError`). A row that arrived and will not parse is corrupt data, loud too; a cross-profile recording is refused loudly on both paths.
- **A13: a student may define a new `Greifobjekt` IN CODE, for that run only.** `register_object` is validated from its own `robot_api` row like any frame (`gripper_close_rad` per family: negative on the OMX, `(0.0, 1.75)` edu6, `(0.0, 0.90)` edu1; a tag-id collision with a built-in type is a German refusal, never last-writer-wins). The merged catalog is built from `_CATALOG_BY_PROFILE`'s base for the running profile and rebound on `ctx.object_catalog` atomically, only on success. **Nothing is persisted and no table is involved**: „Eigene Objekte" keeps its reserved migration 037 and its whole design space. Objects are still found by PRINTED AprilTags, so tags 30/31 that are not on the table fail at `sees()`/`grasp()` with the ordinary „nichts sichtbar"; there is no registration-time check that they exist, nor can there be.
- **The sandbox keys are a sandbox only TOGETHER**: `init: true`, `user: "0:0"` with `cap_drop: ALL` + exactly `SETUID`/`SETGID`/`KILL`, `read_only`, `network_mode: none`, `no-new-privileges`, the two sized `tmpfs`, `mem_limit`/`pids_limit`/`cpus`, and NO `environment:` block. `tests/test_code_runner_sandbox.py` asserts every one on all three compose files (in the shape of `test_rosbridge_origin_gate.py`). The runner is `restart: "no"` like the rest of the student stack (decision A15): a Java program that OOM-kills it stays dead until „Umgebung starten".
- **A code program has the Sammlung (owner decisions O1–O9, migration 041), through ONE asset-document contract.** The drawer, Vormachen (`TeachHost`/`TeachOverlay`) and the drawer's detail views reach „the document" only through an asset document: `sammlung/assetDocument.js::createBlocklyAssetDocument` or `code/codeAssetDocument.js::createCodeAssetDocument`, one contract run against both by `sammlung/__tests__/assetDocument.contract.test.js`. A caller that hands a bare `workspace` gets that workspace's Blockly document, which is why every Blockly path and its tests are unchanged.
  - **The code sidebar's Aufnahmen, Ziele and Positionen rows carry their own „… vormachen" button** (owner decision D5; `code/CodeWorkspace.jsx::SammlungSection`): the kind icon beside the count in a 24 × 24 hit area, `newActions.teachActionFor(capabilities, tab)`, never on Variablen or a read-only editor, and never disabled (TeachHost refuses in German, like the flyout). „+ Neu" is the shared `MenuButton`, opening upwards with its chevron pointing up, in its `size="sm"` form (one-line small items, the menu as wide as its longest item).
  - **The page owns ONE detached Ziele store per code document** (`destinationStore.js::createDetachedDestinationStore`, never `getDestinationStore(null)`), created only in `WorkshopPage::openCodeDocument`, the single way a code document opens (hydrate, its failure, „Neu", a draft, a version restore). `applyCodeFiles` and `openCodeDocument` write `codeFilesRef`/`codeLanguageRef`/`codeStoreRef` SYNCHRONOUSLY: a rename rewrites the code and awaits a save in the same tick, so a ref updated by an effect saved the old text. `runSave`'s code branch sends the store as `blockly_json` (through the same `slimSavePayload`) ONLY when the document opened with Ziele or its store changed since opening (`codeZieleSendRef`, set by a store subscription in `openCodeDocument`; clearing them all still sends `{}`), so a Ziele-free program also saves against an API from before 041, which refuses any `blockly_json` on a code row; `RunControls` (its `destinationStore` prop) sends it as the run's `destinations` exactly like Blockly: a program's own `pin()` still wins. A code run whose files call `replay` is refused before save with the Blockly sentence (`SAVE_FIRST_RECORDINGS_DE`).
  - **Names in code are found by a tokenizer, never a regex over raw text** (`code/codeAssetUsage.js`): an asset name counts only as the WHOLE literal argument (no f-string, concatenation or escape), so a rename rewrites only text it is sure of. A rename rewrites every reference call in every file, never a `pin()`/`pin_current()` definition; a call inside a comment is a switched-off use (Blockly's disabled block) and is rewritten too. A backslash keeps the next quote from ending a Python RAW string too (`r'\''` is one string). A `pin()` inside a comment defines no Ziel for completion or the warnings, but still RESERVES its name for automatic names (`codeDefinedPlaceNames(…, {includeComments: true})` in `takenPlaceNames`), as a disabled block's name stays taken. Identifiers are Unicode (`\p{L}\p{N}_`, both languages: `größe`, `Würfel w`), and so is the boundary before a receiver (`Größrobot.replay("W")` is no call; Java also accepts `edubotics.Robot`). A KEYWORD first argument is the same reference as a positional one (`robot.replay(name="W")`, `move_to(target="A")`, a `pin`'s `x=`/`y=`/`z=`): the parameter names come from `robot_api.json` (`ASSET_CALLS` rows carry `param`), and usage, rename, the asset lint, completion and the save-first replay scan (`codeProject.js::collectCodeReplayNames`) all read both shapes; inside a comment the keyword counts wherever it stands (`# robot.replay(speed=2, name="A")` is a switched-off use of „A"). `variableOccurrencesAll` answers every variable's uses in ONE pass per file (the drawer once re-tokenized the project per variable). Usage rows are `<file>:L<line>` ids (`codeBreakpoints.js`'s id space). Variables are display-only (`collectCodeVariables`, a declaration scan of depth-0 lines: chained `a = b = 1`, annotated `x: int = 5`, never a keyword-argument line inside brackets): no rename, no delete, because there is no language server (decision D7), and the drawer says so.
  - **Indentation follows the BLOCK, in the editor and in every insertion (owner decision R2-O2, review round 3 MB1)**: `codeInsert.newlineIndentAt(content, pos, language)` answers what the line after `pos` needs, and the editor's Enter and every insertion ask it. Inside an existing block the answer is that block's OWN sibling indentation, whatever the rest of the file uses (after one hand-typed 2-space block in a 4-space file, Enter in the 4-space body still gives 4; after a 4-space snippet was pasted into a 2-space file, Enter in the old 2-space block still gives 2); a new block's first line gets its opener's indentation plus the unit of the block the opener sits in, else the file's unit, else `CODE_INDENT_UNIT` (4 spaces: PEP 8 and the Java starter). **An EMPTY row (or a comment at column 0) chose nothing** (review round 4, MC1): it takes the level the next statement needs — the level of the statement that follows it (inside a block the previous statement's sibling level; in front of an `else`/`elif`/`except`/`finally` the block it closes) — and only at the end of the file, with nothing after it, column 0. **A deliberate step back is only explicit, NON-EMPTY whitespace** at a level the block stack has, and only when the next statement really stands at that level (or nothing follows): whitespace shallower than the statement after it is not a choice, and takes that statement's level like an empty row (review round 5, md4: four spaces between two 8-space statements made Enter plus typing an IndentationError where stock CodeMirror gives 8). Tab on a blank row skips the levels a line could not take there (one that would cut the next `else:`/`except:`… off its compound, or make the next statement an unexpected indent), so Tab before an `else:` agrees with Enter. (An empty row read as column 0 made Enter between two statements of a body write an IndentationError, and „Einfügen" refused the spot.) Enter on a whitespace-only line leaves no trailing whitespace behind (`pythonEnter`, like the stock command). **Only the step from a block opener to its first STATEMENT votes** for the file's unit (`detectIndentUnit`): a bracket continuation, a backslash line or a line inside a string never does, or a clean 2-space program with aligned multi-line list literals detected a 5-space unit and Enter produced an IndentationError. The editor wires it in two places (`CodeEditor::structureIndentExtensions`): an `indentService` (both languages) and a `Prec.high` Python keymap for Enter, Tab/Shift-Tab and Backspace (`indentStepAt`: the next / previous level of the block structure, never a fixed unit). An open completion list still takes Enter first (the autocomplete keymap is `Prec.highest`). Java's Enter goes through the indent service and the stock command, so it lines up by COLUMN (exact for spaces). `fileIndentUnitExtensions` still holds CodeMirror's `indentUnit` in a per-view `Compartment` for everything else. `CodeEditor.indent.test.jsx` drives the real view commands and keys over review 3-A's and 4-A's probes (the empty-row cases in every style, CRLF included), the hand-typed-block cases, 3-B's mixed Java starter and all three styles, and writes the results to a fixture that CPython 3.12 compiles and runs (`test_code_insert_cases.py`).
  - **A paste goes in EXACTLY as copied (owner decision R4-O1).** No re-indent: CodeMirror's own paste handler runs, so a multi-line string or a snippet's structure stays byte for byte (a general re-indent once changed both). **The one exception is the text Vormachen itself just copied** (`code/vormachenClipboard.js`: remembered in MEMORY, never browser storage, with its language; recognised by exact match, line breaks aside, since the Windows clipboard may hand LF back as CRLF): pasted into a program of that language, it lands like „Einfügen" — whole lines directly below the cursor's line with the block's indentation, through the same `insertionTargetAt`, or nothing and the German reason — and never joins the cursor's line (`CodeEditor::vormachenPaste` and the drop share `insertLinesBelow`). An edited copy, or one made for the other language, is ordinary text and pastes verbatim. The copied text ends with a line break, so a paste anywhere else never runs two statements together.
  - **The student chooses where a line goes; the app only checks (owner decision R3-O4, replacing O6's no-cursor rule and R-O2).** „Einfügen", a dropped Sammlung row and Vormachen's „Als Programm einfügen" write ONLY directly below the line the cursor is on (a drop: the drop line), indented by `newlineIndentAt`; nothing is placed automatically and a line is NEVER moved to another spot. **No known cursor → nothing is written** and the toast says „Klicke zuerst in deinen Code, wo die Zeile hin soll." (`CODE_DE.CLICK_FIRST_HINT`); Vormachen then puts the lines on the clipboard with „Kopiert – klicke in deinen Code und drücke Strg+V." (no clipboard: the click hint). `codeInsert.insertionTargetAt` is a VALIDATOR (pure): it refuses, with a short German reason and no write, a spot inside the file's leading block (module docstring, comments, `from __future__`, imports — a line above `import robot` is a NameError), anywhere between a decorator and its `def`/`class` (a blank or column-0 comment row between them included: a SyntaxError; review round 5, md1), on a `def`/`class` line whose body opens with its docstring or on a blank row between that line and the docstring (it would demote the docstring; nd1), on a match/case or switch/case/arrow-rule line, directly in an arrow-rule switch body or above a colon-style switch's first label, inside brackets, a multi-line expression, a `\` continuation or a string, a Java spot outside a method body (a class body, a local or anonymous class), a Python spot whose new line would cut an `elif`/`else`/`except`/`finally`/`case` off from its compound or no longer fit the next line's indentation, and a spot that can never run: below `return`/`raise`/`throw`/`break`/`continue`, an exit call (`sys.exit`, `exit`, `quit`, `os._exit`, `os.abort`, `System.exit`, `Runtime.getRuntime().exit`/`halt`), an endless loop (Python: a condition that folds to a true constant — string literals included, `while "x":` — with no `break` out of THIS loop; Java: JLS §14.22 with §15.29 constants, where one non-constant operand makes the condition non-constant and interface fields are implicitly final; the constants are evaluated in JAVA's arithmetic — `int`/`long` as two's complement that wraps where the value is made, integer division, a division by zero not constant, `?:` a constant expression, a constant's declared type (a `byte`/`short`/`char` range, widening only; a `String`, boxed or array constant unsure) — review round 5, md2: JavaScript arithmetic judged `while (1 / 2 == 0)` and `while (2147483647 + 1 < 0)` completing and javac rejected the line as unreachable), an if/else, try or switch whose every way out leaves, a `try` whose body leaves QUIETLY (a `return`/`break`/`continue` raises nothing a handler could catch) or through an exit call its handlers cannot catch (`SystemExit` is not an `Exception`: `try: sys.exit(0) / except Exception: pass`; review round 5, md3), an `if` whose constant-true branch leaves (a constant-false branch is never taken), a `match` whose last case matches anything (`case _:`, a bare capture, no guard) and whose every case leaves, and a `try` WITHOUT a handler around a body that never completes (the `finally` runs, then the body's way out goes on). A `with suppress(…)` may swallow what its body RAISES, so the line after it runs (the rule a `try` with a completing handler gets) — but never a quiet `return`/`break`/`continue`, nor an exit call it cannot catch (md3); any other `with` is judged by its body. A switch opened and closed on its own Java row is a whole statement. Java's constant lookup reads only the declaration VISIBLE at the condition (a field in its class body, a local from itself to its block's end, a parameter/for/catch/pattern variable in what its parentheses head, an untyped lambda parameter in the lambda's body; an interface constant everywhere; a visible local shadows a field — review round 5, md5), a `continue` nested in an `if`, a block, a label, a `synchronized`, a `try` or a `switch` inside a `do … while` is a way to its condition (md5), and a keyword is never a declaration's type (`return LAUF;`, `case STUFE:`) — a same-named variable elsewhere once made another file's constant look variable and javac rejected the inserted line; a Java statement the checker cannot judge, and a name it cannot see, are refused as „unsure", never guessed. What it cannot read (unbalanced brackets, an unterminated string, a Python file with inconsistent indentation) is refused too, and the „nicht geschlossen" sentence is used only when a bracket or string really is open. **Every row the editor shows is a row of its own**, the empty one after the file's final line break included: an insertion there goes to column 0 of the top level, exactly where Enter on that row puts it, and never into the file's last block (review round 5, MD1: it was clamped onto the row above and wrote the line INTO a trailing `for`); a row past the end is the editor's last row, and an all-blank file is judged like any other. Every case is a fixture (`code-insert-cases.json`, written by `codeInsert.cases.test.js`) that is EITHER an insertion `robotis_ai_setup/tests/test_code_insert_cases.py` parses and runs to its marker with CPython and compiles and runs with javac (JDK ≥ 17: `$EDUBOTICS_JAVAC`, `$JAVA_HOME_21_X64`, `PATH`; CI sets `EDUBOTICS_REQUIRE_JAVAC=1`, so a lost JDK fails instead of skipping) OR a German hint, never both; the judge folds constant conditions and knows exit calls, a handler-less `try`, a catch-all `match` and `with suppress(…)` itself (from the AST, not from this module), and `TheJudgeHasTeeth` proves it rejects a dead marker. Since review round 5 an insertion also states HOW OFTEN its marker runs (`runs`, so a line inside a loop is told from the line after it), and every „would never run" hint carries a SHADOW (the line placed there anyway) that the judge proves never runs: CPython by running it with the exit calls trapped, javac by „unreachable statement" or by running it. A CRLF file gets CRLF and every line regex is CRLF-safe. `CodeWorkspace` keeps the last cursor line PER FILE in memory (never stored) and reports `{file, line}` or null; a file switch with no remembered line clears the editor's reveal, and `CodeEditor` keys a reveal on its nonce alone. An insertion that awaited its module writes nothing into a document opened meanwhile (`getDocumentToken`, the page's document token — see „Editor state"; `CODE_DE.INSERT_DOCUMENT_CHANGED`), and both buttons are disabled while one insertion is in flight.
  - **The editor is fed, never reconfigured**: `CodeEditor` reads the asset names through a ref (a new Ziel never rebuilds the view) — built from `CodeWorkspace`'s `assetSources` INSIDE the lazy editor chunk, so `codeAssetCompletion.js` stays out of the entry bundle (a source fence in `codeAssetCompletion.test.js`) — applies an external `value` as the smallest change (`codeInsert.minimalChange`, annotated and NOT echoed through onChange, so the cursor survives and Strg+Z undoes exactly it; the value is compared as LF lines, since the document keeps `\n` between its lines and a CRLF value's smallest change once carried a stray `\r` in, one extra line per sync), and reports the cursor only for the student's own transactions. After an insertion the adapter moves the page's cursor to the last inserted line (`setCursor`): the reveal moves the caret programmatically, which the editor never reports, and without it a second „Einfügen" landed above the first. The asset warnings have their OWN ship switch, `codeAssetCompletion.ASSET_LINT_LANGUAGES` (both languages), separate from the parse markers' `CODE_LINT_LANGUAGES`, which stays `[]`; they skip the cursor line, judge a recording only once the list is `ready`, never judge an object type, and carry no diagnostic `source` (the lint tooltip would print that English word). A drawer row dropped on the text (`code/snippetMime.js::SNIPPET_MIME`, its own module so the drawer does not pull the insertion code) becomes its call below the drop line, checked exactly like a cursor on that line. The asset lint is forced only when the names it judges by really changed (a content key over `assetSources`, which gets a new identity on every deferred keystroke).
  - **Live values are MODULE-LEVEL only, sent by the program's OWN thread between robot calls, and never run student code** (owner decisions R-O1 + R2-O1). The line sampler (`runner/lib/edubotics_debug.py::start_line_sampler`, every `SAMPLER_INTERVAL_S` 0.5 s, only when the RPC lock is free) sends ONLY `__line` and reads only `f_code`/`f_lineno`/`f_back` of the main thread's frames, never a variable (`SamplerSendsOnlyTheLine` AST-fences it): a stub call holds the RPC lock for its whole round trip, so a sampler that also sent values sent none in a program of robot calls (0 frames in 4 s), and on CPython 3.12 a cross-thread `f_locals` read re-synced the frame's own `locals()` dict. The values are `edubotics_debug.LiveValues`, wired by `student_main.py` as the stub's `_Rpc.before_call` hook: right before a PUBLIC robot call (`zeige` included, never `__line`/`__paused`/`__exit`; the stub's `_PUBLIC_METHODS`), on the calling thread, at most once per `LIVE_VALUES_INTERVAL_S` (0.5 s, measured from the previous check's end, so the server's 0.4 s floor never skips an honest frame; a `skipped` reply is sent again next time) and only when the snapshot changed, it sends `__vars` with `snapshot_vars` — the innermost project frame's MODULE globals, then `main.py`'s, never a function frame's `f_locals`, the robot library's own names excluded (`from robot import *`) — then `LiveValues.final` sends the last values once before `__exit`, from the finished program's module dicts: `main.py`'s (after an exception, its traceback's) first, then EVERY project module the program imported (`edubotics_debug.project_module_namespaces`, sorted by name; a helper module's value used to be missing). **The last values and `__exit` go out under ONE hold of the stub's RPC lock** (`student_main.finish_run`, review round 5 md7): the launcher takes that lock once — blocking, the wait `__exit` always had — and unwires the hook, sends the last values and `__exit` inside it, so no other thread's call comes between them and nothing is skipped because the lock was busy (round 4 skipped the last values after one interval and then waited for the same lock for `__exit` anyway, so a program with a busy worker thread ended showing stale values). `final` itself waits for nothing but the server's floor: when a `__vars` frame went out less than one interval ago (`_last_send`, set before the send), the rest of that interval, at most 0.5 s. Measured in the rebuilt arm64 image: a daemon thread looping robot calls while `main.py` ends, a 3 s call in flight, and a helper module's value all arrive, and the end after the program's last statement is never later than before the change (the 3 s call: 3.01 s before and after; a worker thread looping robot calls, two programs four runs each: 0.4–1.4 s, where it was 2.8–22 s). The end still waits for the RPC lock without a bound — the stub's `_Rpc._lock` is not fair, a pre-existing limit (`docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). At most `PAUSED_MAX_LOCALS`, the biggest values replaced by `SHOWN_TOO_BIG` by `fit_vars` (linear) so every name stays. **Any failure switches the values off for the rest of the run and is never raised**: an older server refusing `__vars`, a timeout, an oversized snapshot — the robot call then runs unchanged and `__line` keeps highlighting (measured in-image against the pre-branch server). Every value goes through `safe_render`: EXACT builtin types only, compared by identity (`t is list or …`, never `in` on a type tuple — a metaclass `__eq__` would run), exact builtin containers walked within `LIVE_VALUE_MAX_NODES`/`_CHARS` (dict KEY characters charged too) in one C-level `islice` pass, dict entries only for exact scalar keys (a dict with a skipped entry ends in the cut marker `'…'`, never reads as an empty `{}`; review round 5, nd6), and everything else `<Klasse>`, the name read through `type`'s own `__name__` descriptor and used only when `type(name) is str` (a metaclass property or a `str` subclass never runs); `project_relpath` requires `type(path) is str`. No `repr`/`str`/`__iter__`/`__len__`/`__eq__`/`__hash__`/`__format__` of a student object, and no full `repr` of a big container. The server emits `[VAR:]` only for names whose rendered value changed, and a name `zeige` has shown belongs to `zeige` for the rest of the run (`RunSession._zeige_names`; a module variable of the same name no longer flips the panel). The breakpoint path (`Hook`, `__paused`) keeps its repr-based rendering: it runs on the student's own stopped thread. A function's local values show at a breakpoint or through `zeige`. Java has no automatic values (A8) and says so; `zeige` works in both (Java: primitive overloads plus ONE `Object` overload, dispatched at run time, so `Robot.zeige("x", null)` compiles; `RpcClient.shownValue` renders EVERY array — primitive, `String[]`, `Object[]`, nested — and every `Iterable` as a JSON list by reflection, within the same depth and width bounds the Python stub renders from `robot_api._SHOWN_MAX_DEPTH`/`_SHOWN_MAX_ITEMS` and one character budget, never the JVM's „[J@…" identity string; **it never throws into the student's program**: a failing `toString()` or iterator of the student's — any `Throwable`, a `ConcurrentModificationException` or an `AssertionError` included — shows „<?>" like the Python stub; only the JVM's own failures (`VirtualMachineError`, bar `StackOverflowError`) go on (review round 5, nd2). Huge values are bounded BEFORE any text is built: a `Map` is a JSON object of at most `SHOWN_MAX_ITEMS` entries (keys as text, cut to 100 characters, like the Python stub's `str(k)[:100]`) with a `"…"` key when cut, never its `toString()`; a `CharSequence` is cut before it becomes text; a `BigInteger`/`BigDecimal` of more than `SHOWN_BIG_BITS` (3322, i.e. more than the 1000 characters shown) is „sehr große Zahl" (the Python stub's `_SHOWN_TOO_BIG_DE`, one constant in `robot_api.py`), decided on its bit length. A `java.nio.file.Path` is its text (not the `Iterable` of its parts), a `BigInteger`/`BigDecimal` too big for a double but within that its text, and a whole number past ±2^53 — `zeige(name, long)`, a `Long`, a big `BigInteger` — a double, the Python stub's convention; so the student sees the double's value there, never an exact last digit, in either language).
- **The Pi starts three robot-tier containers, not two.** `pi_agent/docker_manager.py::_ROBOT_TIER` names `open_manipulator`, `physical_ai_server` AND `code_runner`; `_compose_up` names services explicitly, so a Pi with the new compose but the old agent would carry a definition it never starts. Both halves travel in the same tarball and the agent repairs the compose from its own package before `start_manager`, so a fielded Pi gets both in one self-update; the exposure is `pi_agent/setup.sh` from a source checkout with an older agent.

### Mode & lifecycle

- **Follower-only via a runtime container flip on `omx_full`, natively on `omx_follower`.** React `LeaderToggle` → GUI bridge (`gui/app/roboter_studio_control.py`, port **8769**, 127.0.0.1-bound) → `docker_manager.restart_open_manipulator`: regenerates the `.env` (`generate_env_file(robot_type=…, follower_only=True)` omits `LEADER_PORT`, emits managed `EDUBOTICS_FOLLOWER_ONLY=1`) and recreates only `open_manipulator` (`up -d --force-recreate --no-deps`, no pull; rolled back on failure). **Both the forward and the rollback call must carry `robot_type=self._rs_robot_type`**, else a failed switch silently rewrites the managed type. On `omx_follower` the toggle self-hides and the `:8769` bridge is never constructed. A mode toggle de-activates the robot (the arm container is recreated, so the activation gate is back at `idle`).
  - **The `:8769` Origin check is exact on host, scheme and port**: `_ALLOWED_ORIGIN_HOSTS={"localhost","127.0.0.1"}`, `_ALLOWED_ORIGIN_SCHEMES={"http","https"}`, `_ALLOWED_ORIGIN_PORTS={None, 80}`, matching `nginx.conf`'s `~*^https?://localhost(:80)?$` map. The wildcard `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` on GET/OPTIONS is deliberately not tightened (it only permits reading `/roboter-studio/status`); `_origin_allowed` gates the POST. The busy lock sees only another switch; the React-side toggle disable is the only mid-recording protection.
  - **Two HOME pose families, not four identical sites**: the ±π/2 boot/workflow HOME (`entrypoint_omx.sh::HOME`, `motion.HOME_JOINTS_RAD`) vs the more-folded ±π/4 collision/Jetson safe-home (`collision_monitor.SAFE_HOME_ARM`, `jetson_agent/agent.py::SAFE_HOME_JOINTS`). Reconciling them is an ask-first safety change.
- **The readiness gate holds the blocking overlay past the POST**: scene-camera frames + `/joint_states` flowing + the follower moved-then-settled (`PREP_MOVE_FLOOR_MS`, `PREP_MIN_MS`, `PREP_CAP_MS` → manual „Trotzdem fortfahren"). `LeaderToggle.jsx` hides on `available !== true`, `caps?.has_leader === false` or `bridgeHasLeader === false` (the third clause is not gated on `piMode`, which selects only the bridge base) and is disabled during RECORDING/INFERENCING/INFERENCE_LOADING and while `lockedReason` is set (Vormachen open). `handle_rs_set_mode` refuses a leader-less profile with a German 409 in both directions, before the busy lock.
- **`controller_manager_msgs` stays out of the server image** (ABI crash): no switch_controller bridge; follower-only means the leader is never launched.

### Manual-mode arbiter (do not regress)

- **THE invariant: `on_manual == (_manual_persistent or _manual_transient_ops > 0)`, mutated only under `_mode_lock` via `_recompute_on_manual_locked()`** (`physical_ai_server.py`; only the ctor and that helper write it). Jog/replay are transient (refcounted); hand-guide/record persistent (until „Beenden"/cancel/cap-finish). Every mode entry claims under `_mode_lock` around both the `_assert_no_other_active` check and the flag set.
- **Lock order is one-directional**: `_mode_lock` is held only around gate-check + flag-set and always released before `_manual_lock`/`_dxl_torque_lock`/motion (`_manual_lock → _mode_lock` only). Never nest new locks into this.
- **A hard-exit generation (`_manual_exit_gen`, bumped by „Beenden") is snapshotted at claim and re-checked** after acquiring `_manual_lock` and after the blocking re-torque, and folded into replay's `should_stop`: the arm can never drive after the student pressed stop. A torque-ON failure retains the session (fail-loud). A 30 s idle watchdog (own callback group) re-torques and resets an abandoned session; a frontend keepalive keeps a hand-guide alive.
- **Leader-arm Vormachen is a separate claim** (`on_leader_teach` + `_leader_teach_claim_gen`, set/cleared only under `_mode_lock`; `_leader_teach_lock → _mode_lock` only; never `_manual_lock`). It never changes torque, never publishes on the rail, never writes collision state; it samples the follower at 25 Hz. Data stops DISCARD the take: a collision (silently; `stop_leader` reads `_collision_active` itself), a stale `/leader/joint_states` (2 s), a stale follower (1 s); the 120 s cap keeps the take and releases the claim. `_assert_no_other_active` modes: `manual` (relaxes vs `on_manual`), `capture` (relaxes vs both), `leader_teach` (refused by every owner, itself included). Fenced by `test_leader_teach.py` (incl. AST forbidden-name and lock-order fences).
- **`_set_follower_torque` keeps the driver's refusal reason** on `_follower_torque_last_message` (cleared as the method's first statement, written only on failure) and `_last_torque_reason()` appends it at every German-composing site.
- The manual-control services on the reentrant preempt group (`WorkshopJog`, `WorkshopHandGuide`, `WorkshopRecordControl`, `WorkshopReplay`, `WorkshopCapturePose`, `/calibration/cancel`, `/calibration/status`): `WorkshopJog` (joint/cartesian/drive_to/home; velocity-floored quintic; refuses out-of-range, never clamps), `WorkshopHandGuide` (torque-off hand-teaching; the universal manual exit), `WorkshopRecordControl` (~25 Hz into Contract-B `points_json`, samples identical within 0.003 rad dropped; `RECORD_MAX_S=120`; actions `start`/`stop`/`cancel` and `start_leader`/`stop_leader`/`cancel_leader`), `WorkshopReplay`, `WorkshopCapturePose` („P"/„Z" in Vormachen; FK end-effector pose, Z verbatim, works uncalibrated). **`WorkshopCapturePose` additionally returns `joint_positions` + `joint_names`** (follower vector in `Communicator.FOLLOWER_JOINT_ORDER`), filled on success only, empty on every refusal and from an older server (a client treats empty as „unknown"); the two assignments sit inside a swallowing `try` because interfaces generated from the old `.srv` lack the slots and an `AttributeError` out of a service callback kills the node. Field order pinned by `test_workshop_manual_srv.py`.
- **A re-lock holds the arm where it is; the way home is a separate, warned glide, on every arm.** On the ros2_control rail the JTC keeps its pre-torque-off reference through a limp phase, so every re-torque used to SNAP a hand-guided arm back. `_set_follower_torque(True)` therefore publishes `_hold_follower_at_measured_pose()` and waits `_TORQUE_ON_HOLD_SETTLE_S` inside `_dxl_torque_lock`, gated on `_follower_rail_is_ros2_control()` (keyed on the profile's torque service) and torque not already confirmed ON. **The Feetech arms must not get the hold** (their driver seeds `Goal = Present` and drops every trajectory while limp). **Nor does a live leader-arm take** (`on_leader_teach` read lock-free before each hold publish; `test_leader_teach.py::test_T17*`). A missing/short/non-finite readback skips the hold and still energises. The way back is `/workshop/jog` mode `'home'` (`_run_manual_home_glide`): `home_planner.plan_floor_checked_home_route`, on the Feetech arms exactly the `home` block's box-model ladder, on the OMX (no box table) an added point-model rung over `ik.link_points` judged against z = 0 (never the touch-off's `z_table`, which is the EE height with fingers on the table) that goes direct → a small joint-space lift via (joint1 and roll untouched, joints 2/3/4 step ≤ 0.6 rad, ≥ 20 mm clearance gained, least travel first, 24-candidate budget) → German refusal. **Never a Cartesian re-solve for the via** (strict-vertical IK from a non-vertical start picks another branch). The Blockly `home` block uses the same planner; `go_to_observation_pose` still uses `plan_home_route` (an open question). The glide: every leg stretched to `_MANUAL_HOME_PEAK_RAD_S` (1.0 rad/s, ≥ `_MANUAL_HOME_MIN_DURATION_S` 3 s), gripper carried, a stop that publishes a hold, warn-only 0.30 rad arrival, „steht bereits in der Grundstellung" within `_MANUAL_HOME_ALREADY_THERE_RAD`, a broad `except`, and a REFUSAL when `_follower_torque_on is False` (never stiffen and drive a hand-guided arm). The pre-energise hold is published twice (settle, re-read, publish, energise) and accepts a stale readback there only (`allow_stale=True`). **The server never glides on its own**: `components/Workshop/HomeGlidePrompt` is its only caller (5 s countdown, „Jetzt fahren" / „Hier stehen lassen", „Stopp"), from `TableTouchStep`, `JogPanel` and Vormachen's „Fertig" (only after the session released the arm and the last re-lock was confirmed, never offline, never in leader mode); its `HomeGlideProvider` wraps WorkshopPage because every caller can unmount mid-countdown. Re-locks with no student watching (idle watchdog, calibration cancel, unmount teardown) hold in place and offer nothing.
- **Callback-group rule**: anything blocking (HF whoami, torque switch, jog drive, replay) must not run on the node-default MutuallyExclusive group, or it starves the 1 Hz heartbeat → React „Getrennt". Keep the React service timeout (10 s) above the server's 8 s whoami wait. `main()` catches only `KeyboardInterrupt`, so an uncaught exception in any callback kills the node (hence try/except-wrapped gpio/collision callbacks). `MultiThreadedExecutor(num_threads=8)`; `gc.freeze()` before spin and no explicit `gc.collect()` on the recording path (a full collection paused every thread ~70–100 ms). The record timer runs in its own `_record_cb_group` (collection only), the collision trip hand-over and watchdog in `_collision_cb_group`. **High-rate subscriptions live on a second node, never on the main one** (round 5): every camera, follower and leader subscription and the collision monitor's three are created on `physical_ai_server_sensors` (`physical_ai_server.py::_ensure_sensor_executor`, called in `__init__` before `_init_collision_monitor`), spun by a `SingleThreadedExecutor` on its own daemon thread, depth `SENSOR_QOS_DEPTH` (32) BEST_EFFORT. Why: the main `MultiThreadedExecutor` cost 2.23 ms of CPU per delivered message and saturated the GIL (the main thread 87–97 % idle waiting); a `SingleThreadedExecutor` dispatches one in ~0.18 ms. `use_global_arguments=False` is load-bearing: the launch's `-r __node:=physical_ai_server` would otherwise rename it too (two nodes with one name); `ros2 node list` shows exactly `/physical_ai_server` and `/physical_ai_server_sensors`. The thread is supervised (owner decision F3): every sensor callback body is guarded (`Communicator._camera_callback` → `_camera_callback_body`, the follower and leader alike), and `_sensor_loop` ends the PROCESS with `os._exit(SENSOR_EXIT_CODE)` (70) after `SENSOR_FAIL_LIMIT` (3) executor failures within `SENSOR_FAIL_WINDOW_S` (10 s), so s6 respawns the node — without it one exception killed the thread silently while the main node kept answering (recording stalled, collision detection deaf). `main()`'s `finally` shuts the sensor executor and node down before the main node. `EventsExecutor` (another ~2×) is `rclpy.experimental` in Jazzy; trigger: rig gate G1 shows the sensor thread above 25 % of a core.

### Motion, IK, floors

- **The Grundstellung is guarded in joint space.** `WORKSPACE_FLOOR_MARGIN_M` (0.01) sits only in `_solve_or_raise`/`_try_solve` and judges a Cartesian target; HOME is not one. The model is `workflow/arm_geometry.py`: one axis-aligned box per link in that link's own frame, transformed by `Edu6IKSolver.link_frames()`; `base_link` is a self-collision partner but excluded from the floor check. `resolve_geometry` keys on the solver's `backend`, and only the Feetech solvers have `link_frames`, which keeps OMX bit-identical. `base_link.STL` spans z ∈ [0, 0.0625] m on the edu6, so z = 0 is the table; no calibration needed.
  - **`workflow/home_planner.py` is the ladder**: direct → searched via → German refusal, the via searched over (j2, j3, j5). The two legs are held to different bounds and that is load-bearing: the lift leg may sit as low as the arm already is, every later leg must clear the allowance.
  - `EDUBOTICS_HOME_FLOOR_TOL_M` (default 0.020 m; `0` disables) is the box model's conservatism, not `WORKSPACE_FLOOR_MARGIN_M`. `EDUBOTICS_HOME_SELFCOLL_WARN_M` is warn-only, never gates a rung, ships disabled (0.0); promoting it is a Rule §2 change. Both forwarded on the student and opi composes.
  - **The driver refuses the boot-home glide the same way, and the refusal LEAVES THE ARM TORQUED** (`edu6_arm_node.boot_home_floor_decision` + the plain-COPY `edu6_geometry.py`, cross-checked against the server copy by `tests/test_edu6_geometry.py`). No via search in the driver; unevaluable geometry fails open.
  - Cartesian motion is clean on edu6; only `drive_to` from a hand-guided pose dips. The OMX equivalent is unmeasured.
- **Closed-form analytical IK only** (`workflow/ik_solver.py`): NumPy FK + strict-vertical 5-DOF IK, θ0≈20.14°, elbow-up first; unreachable/out-of-limit/non-finite → `None` → German „Arbeitsbereich" error. No numerical/PyKDL/TRAC-IK fallback. Reach annulus ≈ 0.04–0.28 m (`_REACH_MIN/_MAX` = 0.0415/0.2825; the authoritative bound is `solve(...) is not None`). Solver + perception are built once per lifetime.
- **Velocity floor** (`trajectory_builder._velocity_safe_duration`): 4.8 rad/s × 0.6 = 2.88 rad/s peak, quintic peak factor 15/8; it only ever extends a segment.
