A recorded replay is the one exception to the quintic shape: consecutive samples are joined with `build_linear_segment` under the same 0.6 × limit on the constant velocity actually commanded (the per-pair quintic replayed a take 76 % slow with a stop-and-go ripple); the lead-in stays a quintic; an abrupt recorded change is commanded as a velocity step. **Tempo** (run-bar 0.5/1/2 + per-move `GESCHWINDIGKEIT`, clamped `[0.5, 2.0]`, cloud rejects out-of-range) shrinks the duration BEFORE the floor re-extends it; a tempo-slowed segment is capped at `motion._MAX_STRETCHED_DURATION_S=4.5 s`.
- **`ctx.motion_lock` is an arbitration policy, not a safety guard: WAIT, WARN ONCE, NEVER RAISE.** One re-entrant `RLock` on the `WorkflowContext` serializes every rail writer. **Every acquire goes through `motion._hold_motion_lock`** (AST-fenced by `test_motion_lock_policy.py`): 0.05 s slices (`_MOTION_LOCK_POLL_S`) so `ctx.should_stop` is polled while queueing, one German `[WARNUNG]` (`_MOTION_LOCK_BUSY_DE`) past `MOTION_LOCK_NOTICE_S` (10.0, read at call time, never a default argument), then it keeps waiting. `_release_motion_lock` is its partner; `_reacquire_after_release` a thin `stop_raises=False` wrapper.
  - The slicing is what makes Stop work; a mixed discipline on one lock starves the polling waiter completely, hence the one-helper rule. The old 10 s bound is gone and must not come back as a deadline: every holder is bounded (publishes by trajectory duration, composites by a fixed sequence plus `GRASP_SETTLE_MAX_S` over `GRASP_RETRY + 1` attempts, `replay_trajectory` by `MAX_TRAJECTORY_SPAN_S / REPLAY_SPEED_MIN`).
  - A breakpoint inside a „wenn …"-Block pauses while `_run_hat_handler` holds this lock; `WorkflowContext.is_paused` (a non-consuming probe wired in `start()`) suppresses the notice and re-arms its clock while a human is looking.
  - **A wait must come back holding the lock and must never raise instead**: `wait_seconds`, `perception_blocks._poll_until` and `interpreter._exec_wait_until` drop the lock around their wait and re-take it in a `finally` inside `_run_hat_handler`'s `with`, whose `__exit__` releases unconditionally (a raising re-acquire retired the hat silently while the run reported green).
  - Lock order is shallow and one-directional: `motion_lock → claim_lock`, `motion_lock → var_lock`, held only around O(1) work. **Changing this policy is Rule §2 territory: ask first.**
- **Workspace floor**: any target below the (tilted-plane-aware) table → `'Zielpunkt liegt unter der Tischebene.'`, enforced in both solve paths.
- **`WorkflowManager.set_destination` is THE server-side gate on a destination**; all three service writers (`mark_destination_callback`, `capture_pose_callback`, the sim seed) and all three block handlers pass through it. A fourth input does not: the `/workflow/start` `destinations` sibling is judged by `WorkflowManager._parse_destinations` (same `destination_name_error_de` helper, finite x/y/z, a closed `kind` enum) and lands only in that run's dict, never in `_persisted_destinations`; a rule change must touch both gates. `set_destination` strips the name and refuses a whitespace-only one. `mark_destination_callback` persists before setting `response.success`; what stops a refusal being reported as „gespeichert." is its `except ValueError` handler.
- **Every cloud writer of `workflows.blockly_json` goes through `utils/blocklyPayload.js`'s SAVE allowlist, enumerated by a source-level fence in `blocklyPayload.test.js`**: `WorkshopPage::runSave`'s create and update (behind `saveWorkflowNow`; its code branch builds only `{"edubotics-destinations": …}` or `{}` and passes the same slimming) plus `WorkflowTemplatesPage.publishClassroomTemplate`. The RUN allowlist is deliberately narrower (`workspaceComments` and `edubotics-destinations` are the two differences), so the two are separate exports and `RunControls.runPayload.test.jsx` pins the RUN payload's exact key set.
- **A destination stores WHERE; a pinned one re-asks HOW HIGH on every run.** `motion.table_z_at` answers the plane height at (x, y); `motion.resolve_destination_z` answers which z this stored destination resolves to for this run, and provenance decides, not geometry. A camera pin's z was read off the plane at click time, and `EDUBOTICS_FORCE_RECALIBRATION` ships 1, so the touch-off re-draws that plane every lesson while nothing invalidates the two carriers that bake it (the saved workflow's `Z` field and `_persisted_destinations`). A measured height („P" in Vormachen) must survive verbatim. So `plane_tracked` is written by the WRITER in code, never read out of persisted data: True on `destination_pin` + `mark_destination_callback`, False on `destination_current` + `capture_pose_callback`; for a payload `destinations` entry `_parse_destinations` maps the closed `kind` enum (`_DESTINATION_KIND_PLANE_TRACKED`: `pin`→True, `pose`→False) and skips an unknown kind with a German `[WARNUNG]`. An entry with no flag keeps its stored z. Read at `_resolve_target` and re-stamped in `destination_pin`. The Blockly `Z` field stays (removing it is the `workspaces.load` MissingConnection trap; it is the fallback on an uncalibrated rig). `EDUBOTICS_GRASP_Z_FROM_PLANE` moves both `table_z_at` call sites.
  - Consequence: for a plane-tracked pin the workspace-floor refusal is definitionally satisfied (`pin_z == _floor_z_at(pin_x, pin_y)`); on a bad plane this converts „refuses" into „descends to the fitted plane", the owner-authorised RS-07 trade. `test_sim_node_wiring` pins both halves. `_ik_precheck` still reads the baked `Z` for a pin statement (advisory, millimetre-scale); a `destination_ref` no pin/current statement sets is checked against the merged destinations under the run's calibration (`_precheck_destination_points`).
- **Destination precedence at run time: program pin/current statements > payload `destinations`; the robot-local `_persisted_destinations` are consulted ONLY for a payload without the `destinations` sibling (an older client).** A client that sends the document is authoritative (`capture_pose_callback`/`mark_destination_callback` write robot-locally under automatic names every student on a rig shares; a fallback once resolved a deleted Ziel to another student's point). Payload entries are never written into the robot-local dict. Skipped entries each emit one `[WARNUNG]`, bounded at `MAX_DESTINATION_SKIP_REASONS` (8) plus a summary line (`MAX_PAYLOAD_DESTINATIONS` counts only accepted entries). Pinned by `test/test_payload_destinations.py`.
- **Two operator gripper knobs WARN when they leave the profile's band and are then OBEYED.** `motion._warn_gripper_knob_out_of_band` emits one `[WARNUNG]` per run (deduped via `WorkflowContext.gripper_knob_warned`) naming `EDUBOTICS_PICKUP_CLOSE_RAD` / `EDUBOTICS_GRASP_HELD_MAX_RAD`; clamping or refusing would disable the very rollback they are. `PICKUP_CLOSE_RAD` does not cover „schließe Greifer": `_pickup_close` is read by „aufnehmen" and `close_on_object`'s fallback only, while `close_gripper` commands `_gripper_closed(ctx)` unconditionally.
- **Motion details that surprise**: the observe pose is `EDUBOTICS_OBSERVE_POSE` (joint radians, default HOME; malformed → HOME); `home` carries the current gripper state; `drop_at` releases at `EDUBOTICS_DROP_HEIGHT_M` (0.05) above the table (higher than the grasp clearance, to clear container rims) and bisects its clearance to what is reachable. The grasp-split blocks re-solve each step without the composite grasp's `motion_lock` atomicity (known, accepted).
- **A grasp with no room to approach from above is REFUSED, not reported as success.** `_solve_grasp_and_approach` bisects the hover; the floor is `_MIN_APPROACH_CLEARANCE_M = _APPROACH_BISECT_TOL_M` (2 mm). It raises `GraspSkip`, not `WorkflowError`; `grasp_object` marks the tag SKIPPED so a „Solange sichtbar" loop terminates, while a standalone „Greife" still fails loud. `drop_at` passes `refuse_without_clearance=False`.
- **`GraspSkip` is classified by CAUSE, never by CALLER.** `GraspSkip` is for a cause the WORLD can fix (nothing visible, out of reach, yaw unreadable, no approach room, not held after retries), because a later loop pass on another instance can succeed. A cause the PROGRAM has to fix is a base `WorkflowError`: a socket wired to the wrong KIND of value, and an empty socket with no `ctx.last_find_failure`. `variables_get` carries no Blockly output type, so any variable connects to a `check:'Greifziel'` socket; raised as a `GraspSkip` the loop swallowed it and the arm descended three times with an open gripper while the run reported green. All three consumers share `motion._NOT_A_GREIFZIEL_DE`, and `move_above`/`descend_to` route through `_greifziel_xyz_roll(ctx, ziel, block_de)`. The `_is_greifziel` gate sits AFTER the `None` check and BEFORE `_resolve_target`; it is a KIND test (a `Detection` with `world_xyz_m is None` must pass it).
- **The clamped-approach warning fires only below `_APPROACH_WARN_FRAC` (0.25) of the request** (on edu6 any reduction is every grasp); its reason is the arm's height over that point, never „Ziel liegt am Rand".
- **„hebe an" after „Greife" is a reported no-op on edu6 and must never raise.** `_execute_pickup` ends at the hover; a following `lift` publishes nothing and says so. Its step 1 splits two guards: BELOW THE TABLE stays a hard `WorkflowError` (Rule §2); NOT SOLVABLE warns and continues (both Feetech HOMEs are outside the strict-vertical image, so home→lift used to abort the run blaming an object).
- **The three "rise as high as this arm can" bisects are one helper** (`_max_reachable_rise`): approach hover, release rim clearance, `lift`.

### Calibration (3 steps + optional check; wizard in `CalibrationWizard.jsx`)

- **Per-rig ChArUco intrinsics are mandatory, 20 frames** (`INTRINSIC_FRAMES_REQUIRED=20`), solved with `CALIB_RATIONAL_MODEL` (the ~102° lens overwhelms the 5-term default). Factory-default seeding is deleted (never re-add); any YAML with `source == factory_default` is skipped.
- **Scene extrinsic**: board against the 3D-printed L-jig → `EDUBOTICS_EXTRINSIC_BURST_FRAMES` (8) reads, ≥4 passing per-frame gates → MAD outlier rejection → SO(3) chordal mean. Per frame: ChArUco `matchImagePoints` → `cv2.SOLVEPNP_SQPNP` + `solvePnPRefineLM` (ITERATIVE only as fallback; not `IPPE_SQUARE`) → compose with the jig-known `T_board_to_base` (yaw from `EDUBOTICS_BOARD_YAW_DEG`); `_check_extrinsic_orientation` cannot catch a 90°/180°/mirror error.
- **Touch-off („Tisch vermessen")**: torque-off, the student taps **≥4** spread points (`TABLE_TOUCH_POINTS_REQUIRED`) → FK → least-squares plane (`z_table` + tilt); gates reject near-collinear/high-residual captures (`TABLE_TOUCH_MIN_MINOR_M=0.015`, residual ≤0.008, camera cross-check 0.12 m). Four, not three, because a plane has 3 parameters and at 3 taps `TABLE_TOUCH_MAX_RESIDUAL_M` could never fire; no minimum-separation gate exists and none is needed. **The per-tap verticality gate (12°) asks the solver which axis the tool points along** (`ik.approach_axis_local` → OMX `+x`, edu6 `−z`, edu1 `+z`), forwarded to the manager as `get_approach_axis`; an absent provider falls back to `+x`. A hardcoded FK column is 90° wrong on both Feetech arms and rejected every correct tap. A rotating claw needs the jaws CLOSED for the tap (`tool_tip_tracks_gripper` → extra German step in `TableTouchStep`).
- **Dual Z-key convention**: `board_table_z` = camera-side table surface written by the extrinsic solve (the projection plane); `z_table` = MEASURED end-effector grasp height written only by the touch-off (the descend target). **There is no `z_table=0.0` fallback anywhere**; a missing touch-off → grasp/`mark_destination` refuse in German. Never reintroduce it.
- **`scene_handeye.yaml` is rewritten whole by three writers, and who preserves what is a contract.** `cv2.FileStorage` cannot append, so each writer read-modify-writes the file. `_solve_scene_extrinsic` truncates everything (correct: a new `T_cam_to_base` invalidates the touch-off and any correction). `write_verify_correction` and `_write_table_plane` must each carry the other's keys forward (a second touch-off used to silently delete `xy_correction`/`yaw_bias_rad`/`verify_residual_mm`/`verify_points`, daily, since the touch-off is re-run every lesson). Adding a key means adding it to both read-back lists; `test_a_fresh_extrinsic_deliberately_DOES_clear_the_correction` pins the asymmetry.
- **Optional ground-truth XY/yaw check („Genauigkeit prüfen")**: closed-form 2D similarity fit (`fit_xy_correction`, Umeyama) + circular-mean yaw bias, persisted as `xy_correction`/`yaw_bias_rad` and applied live. Four refusals: a mirror fit (`det(R)<0`, `mirror_detected=True`); `VERIFY_MIN_POINTS=3` (a 2D similarity has 4 DOF, so n=2 fits any input at 0.0 mm); `VERIFY_MAX_ROTATION_DEG=30.0` and `VERIFY_SCALE_MIN=0.75` (`_MAX` its reciprocal), because a mis-PAIRED capture produces an exact similarity that no residual gate can catch. `AccuracyVerifyStep.jsx`'s own `MIN_POINTS = 4` sits above the server floor. `fit_xy_correction` always returns the dict, never raises (a non-finite point is its own German case).
- `EDUBOTICS_FORCE_RECALIBRATION` (default ON) reports calibration as absent until a touch-off completes in the current boot; `=0` opts out.

### Perception + object catalog (AprilTag-only)

- **AprilTag (tag36h11, `pupil_apriltags`, thread-locked) is the only perception path.** Colour/YOLOX/open-vocab detection, `onnxruntime`, `color_profile.py`, `coco_classes.py`, `CalibrationCaptureColor.srv` were deleted. `_SCENE_FRAME_MAX_AGE_S=1.0` refuses detection on a frozen camera; `_require_*` guards fail loud in German at the block. The `when_object_seen` hat path deliberately swallows detect errors.
- **The object catalog is hardcoded and profile-split** (`workflow/object_catalog.py`): `_FIXED_CATALOG` (OMX, `gripper_close_rad −0.5`), `_FIXED_CATALOG_EDU6` (`1.0`, band `(0.0, 1.75)`), `_FIXED_CATALOG_EDU1` (`0.10`, band `(0.0, 0.90)`), routed by `_CATALOG_BY_PROFILE` through `fixed_catalog(profile_id)`; the node passes `self._arm_profile.profile_id`. A profile missing from that table falls back to the OMX set (negative close, outside a Feetech band); a test asserts every non-negative-close profile has a row. No per-rig file, override or I/O. Changing the set = edit the constant + pinned tests + rebuild + reprint the tag sheet. Current set: `wuerfel`/„Würfel", `tag_ids=[20,21]`, `object_height_m=0.030`, `grasp_depth_m=0.015`, `approach_clear_m=0.06`. `EDUBOTICS_TAG_SIZE_M` (default 0.024) is re-read on every `fixed_catalog()` call. `GetObjectCatalog` returns six parallel per-type arrays (`type_names`/`labels_de` + `object_height_m`/`object_width_m`/`color_hex`/`max_instances`) from `build_object_catalog_response()`, all six empty together on failure. The builder takes an optional `profile_id` and `get_object_catalog_callback` passes the resolved profile's (omitting it reproduces the old OMX payload byte-for-byte). The response is profile-independent today (`gripper_close_rad` is not one of the six arrays), so the fence is a WIRING test: `test_object_catalog.py::test_get_object_catalog_callback_passes_the_profile_id` AST-checks the call site, because `physical_ai_server.py` cannot be imported without rclpy.
- **The „Solange sichtbar" reclaim asks the ROBOT where it put the object, never the camera.** `handlers/perception_blocks.py::_reclaim_recycled` un-claims an object a person moved: for a CLAIMED object, a sighting ≥ `EDUBOTICS_RELEASE_MOVE_M` (0.05) from the commanded release point (`ctx.claim_release_xy`, written by `motion.drop_at` after the release publish, and by „öffne Greifer" via `motion._current_tool_xy`, an FK of `ctx.last_full_joints`); for a SKIPPED object, ≥ `EDUBOTICS_RECLAIM_MOVE_M` (0.02) from where it lay (`ctx.claim_pick_xy`). With no reference for a tag the branch adopts the current sighting and gives no verdict that pass. Everything else fails closed: a tag in the gripper (`ctx.carried_tag`) is never judged; a claimed tag with no recorded release point keeps its claim; every FK failure answers `None`. `_RECLAIM_MOVE_M <= 0` is the master rollback (disables both halves); a non-positive `_RELEASE_MOVE_M` falls back to it. `claim_tag`/`skip_tag` (`workflow/claims.py`, re-exported by `perception_blocks` so `motion` can import without the cycle) touch no position state. `EDUBOTICS_RECLAIM_ABSENT_S` is not a reclaim knob; it is the „Wenn <Typ> gesehen" hat's absence grace, imported by `workflow_manager.py` as `_HAT_ABSENT_GRACE_S`. **Every block that looks runs the reclaim** (`count_unclaimed_visible` and the four value blocks share `_detect_named_unclaimed`), and so does the hat trigger poll: `workflow_manager::_wait_object_visible`'s frame-in-hand shape calls `reclaim_from_detections` (no camera read) and re-reads the exclusions; its bare-`False` shape calls `reclaim_only`, floored at `_HAT_RECLAIM_MIN_INTERVAL_S` (1.0 s, keyed by object type). Neither may call `ctx.emit_detections`. The blind state (wanted set empty, every instance done) emits `_nothing_to_grasp_message` once per (run, type) via `ctx.all_done_notified`. `_tag_table_xy` is the single projection shared by the reclaim and `_attach_named_world`.
- **Marker grasp orientation**: `joint5 = base_yaw(x,y) − tag_yaw + GRASP_ROLL_RAD`. `EDUBOTICS_GRASP_ROLL_DEG` default 90° is the jaw convention with no runtime guard. `EDUBOTICS_GRASP_CLEARANCE_M` default 0.012; OMX gripper open 0.8 rad / closed −0.5 rad.
- **Grasp success is verified**: `check_grasp_held` reads the achieved gripper angle after a `GRASP_SETTLE_S` settle (0.3 s; `EDUBOTICS_GRASP_SETTLE_S` folded into `[0, GRASP_SETTLE_MAX_S=2.0]` by `_clamped_settle_s` and slept in stop-polled slices, since the settle is held under `ctx.motion_lock`) against a per-object threshold: last commanded close + `GRASP_HELD_MARGIN_RAD` (0.15). That close lives in `ctx.last_commanded_close_rad`, written only by the three close paths and reset at workflow start; None → the derived fallback `_grasp_held_max` = `gripper_closed_rad + grasp_held_margin_rad` (−0.35 OMX, 0.12 edu6, 0.10 edu1). `EDUBOTICS_GRASP_HELD_MAX_RAD` non-empty restores the fixed global threshold; both composes forward it with an EMPTY default. There is no `ArmProfile.grasp_held_max_rad`; re-adding the lookup requires declaring the matching ctx field. `grasp_object` retries `EDUBOTICS_GRASP_RETRY` (default 1) times, then skips the tag. The tag yaw is circular-averaged over `EDUBOTICS_TAG_YAW_FRAMES` (7; floored at 1, capped at `_TAG_YAW_FRAMES_MAX` 30 with a `[WARNUNG]`) frames (resultant ≥0.9), and the back-projected tag edge must match `tag_size_m` within `EDUBOTICS_TAG_EDGE_TOL_FRAC` (0.5) or the yaw is dropped.
- **`find_object` returning `None` records WHY on `ctx.last_find_failure`**, and the split motion blocks report that. `_out_of_reach_reason` probes the real solver along the target's bearing for too-near / too-far / wrong-bearing.
- **The sim's per-type tag cap is visible to the student**: `SimPerception` caps each type at `len(recipe.tag_ids)` and assigns ids by placement order; `_warn_sim_objects_beyond_tag_capacity` emits one `[WARNUNG]` through `_emit_workflow_status`.
- Blockly grasp is split into discrete blocks (finde / fahre über / senke / schließe / hebe an) alongside the composite grasp, plus while-visible-loop and control blocks.
- **Both „Solange sichtbar" endings state a fact about the world.** The clean-completion line names the skipped count (`interpreter._skipped_count_for_type` intersects `recipe.tag_ids` with `ctx.skipped_tags` under `claim_lock`). The stall line names the missing block only when `Interpreter._body_can_claim` (a static scan recursing through nested slots and resolvable procedures) proves no claiming block is reachable; it runs only after a measured stall (`WHILE_STALL_PASSES` 3) and fails OPEN (an unresolvable call or the depth cap withholds the hint). Both diagnostics are best-effort inside `try/except`.
- **The split blocks deliberately do not auto-claim; re-adding one is a regression.** Only „merke … als erledigt" (`edubotics_mark_done`) and the composite „Greife …" (`edubotics_grasp_object`, which claims after `check_grasp_held`) claim. `close_on_object` fires before `check_grasp_held` (would claim a missed grab), `lift` takes no Greifziel, and `find_object` also feeds „falls Ziel"/„Position von"/counting (claiming at selection would consume a merely-looked-at object). `test_greifziel_socket_contract.py::test_mark_done_still_does_the_work_and_is_not_bypassed` fails if any split block starts claiming.

### Trajectory record/replay

- **Contract-B**: `{"fps":25, "points":[[j1..jn, grip, t_s], …]}`, `n+2` floats per point (7 on OMX/edu1, 8 on edu6). `WorkshopReplay` + `edubotics_replay_trajectory` share `handlers/trajectory.py::resegment_trajectory`: consecutive pairs joined linearly (`build_linear_segment`) under the velocity floor (speed clamp `[0.25, 3.0]`), a floor-checked synthetic lead-in from the current pose and per-waypoint `point_floor_check`. **The time column needs both bounds**: `extract_points` caps the endpoint span at `MAX_TRAJECTORY_SPAN_S` (600 s) AND requires it non-decreasing (the cost is `round(dt * 30)` waypoints per consecutive pair, so a 115-byte payload with an internal time spike once resegmented to 600 002 waypoints). Replay bypasses the IK reach check but never the velocity floor.
- **Replay pacing + no velocities on the wire**: `chunked_publish` paces each chunk by its actual span; `should_stop` is polled per point and every ≤50 ms in `trajectory_builder._pace`. **Every replay is published position-only** (`REPLAY_PUBLISHES_VELOCITIES = False`, read by both callers): ros2_controllers' JTC rejects any message whose last point has non-zero velocity (`allow_nonzero_velocity_at_trajectory_end`, default false on Jazzy), and the last point of every ~1 s chunk is mid-motion, so 13 of 14 chunks were rejected and the robot did not move. `resegment_trajectory(with_velocities=True)` still exists as a pure option; do not re-enable it without every chunk ending at rest or that parameter set through the overlay chain, plus a rig measurement. The same rule binds every writer of the rail.
- **A replay whose lead-in would turn a full-circle joint more than π is refused** (`refuse_wrapped_lead_in`, both paths; the server runs it inside `_segment_from`), and **a take that crosses a full-circle joint's ±180° seam is refused** (`refuse_wrapped_joint_jumps`, judged only on joints whose `ik.joint_limits` span 2π): the servo cannot cross the seam, so there is nothing to unwrap to. A positive sub-millisecond pair step is floored at `_MIN_PAIR_DT_S` (1 ms).
- **The replay floor checks credit a rotating claw's tip rise** (`motion.tool_tip_rise_m` → `Edu1IKSolver.tool_tip_rise_m` via `edu1_ik._TIP_RISE_TABLE`, per-0.05 rad band minima derived from the finger STLs and rounded down; a test recomputes the mesh curve). It is negative near 0.14 rad; never go back to a closed-form guess. Bounded to [−5 mm, +60 mm]. Recording/replay only.
- **`/workshop/replay` rebuilds its lead-in from the live pose inside `_run_replay`**, after it owns `_manual_lock` and has re-torqued (`rebuild` closure); the callback still builds once so a floor/zone refusal answers synchronously.
- **A record stop whose re-lock fails still returns `points_json`** (the session stays claimed) and Vormachen keeps the take in review; no glide is offered while it failed.
- **The command-rail publisher is created at boot** (`__init__`, before `_init_robot_profile`), and `_hold_follower_at_measured_pose` waits up to `_COMMAND_RAIL_MATCH_WAIT_S` for a matched subscriber (a publisher created on first use lost the first message to DDS discovery).
- **A real (non-sim) program start asserts follower torque** in `_launch_workflow`, after the leader gate and before `manager.start` (a program on a limp Feetech arm finished green with no motion).
- **The replay daemon never fails silently**: `/workshop/replay` answers „Wiedergabe gestartet" before `_run_replay` can refuse, so each refusal also publishes a German `/task/status` notice through `_publish_manual_notice`.
- **Recorded samples are rounded at the sampler** (`_MANUAL_RECORD_JOINT_DECIMALS` 4, `_MANUAL_RECORD_TIME_DECIMALS` 3), and `utils/trajectoryCompact.js` applies the same rounding on save and when RunControls injects recordings into the run payload (full-precision floats put a 120 s take past the cloud's 256 KiB cap). RunControls refuses a run payload over 256 KiB that carries recordings with a German sentence naming them.
- **`teach/useTeachSession` closes its manual session exactly once** (`sessionOpen`, set on `record start` / `hand_guide(true)`, cleared only by a confirmed `hand_guide(false)` or a successful `record cancel`); `hand_guide(false)` is the universal manual abort, and a second close would stop a still-playing preview. **Every Vormachen service call goes through one FIFO queue** (the server does not order overlapping manual calls); teardown chains onto an in-flight start.
- **Vormachen (`teach/TeachHost` → `teach/TeachOverlay` → `teach/useTeachSession`) is the one place a student creates a Bewegung, Position or Ziel on the real arm, and it opens as ONE focused window per kind** (owner decisions D1–D4): „Bewegung vormachen" (Space records, F frees and locks, the review), „Position vormachen" (P, F), „Ziel vormachen" (Z, F); `RecordPanel` and the Kamera-tab „Position merken" are retired. **`teach/teachGates.js::teachKeyOffered({kind, mode, key})` is the one predicate of that contract**: Esc always; F only in hand mode (never leader, never pending); P only in the Position window, Z only in the Ziel window; Space/Enter/R/Entf only in the Bewegung window; no valid kind → Esc only. The engine asks it FIRST in `handlerFor`, so it gates every key AND every button press (a key without its button is dead: still prevented, never handled), and again in `capture()`; the overlay ANDs every button cell with it and renders only the offered buttons (hand: the kind's button + F; leader: the kind's button; pending: the kind's button, disabled). So there is no mixed session and no P or Z during a take (D4, accepted). **There is no kind-less window**: `studioAssetsSlice.requestTeach` drops a request without a valid kind (`TEACH_KINDS`), and `TeachHost` drops one silently too; the old cosmetic `focus` is gone. The list takes only the window's own kind (`onKeep` only in a Bewegung window, `onCapture` only a capture of the window's kind), except the Ziel window's „Als Position speichern" answer (D3); „Weiter vormachen" keeps the kind; the window titles itself with the kind and its icon (`TEACH_KIND_LABEL_DE`, `TEACH_KIND_ICON`) and each kind has its own hints. Entry: the toolbar's „Vormachen" chooser (`components/Workshop/MenuButton.jsx`, a WAI-ARIA menu button whose handled keys never reach Blockly and whose auto-repeated Enter/Space never chooses; choosing focuses the trigger before `onSelect`, so the overlay restores focus there) and the Sammlung creation buttons (see the Sammlung bullets). **Every Roboter-Studio popover goes away the same way** (`components/Workshop/usePopoverDismiss.js`, used by the chooser, „+ Neu", „Öffnen" (`OpenWorkflowPopover.jsx`) and „Verlauf"; owner decision R1-O3): a pointerdown outside heard in the CAPTURE phase (Blockly's gesture stops the workspace pointerdown before it bubbles, so a bubble-phase or `mousedown` document listener never hears a click into the editor; `MenuButton.blockly.test.jsx` proves both halves with a real workspace), focus moving outside (focus going nowhere closes nothing, so a list the R2-O3 lock arrives under stays open and says why), and Esc back to the trigger. „Öffnen" and „Verlauf" are announced popovers (`aria-haspopup="dialog"`) that move focus into their list once it has loaded and walk its buttons with the arrow keys; „Verlauf"'s „laden" buttons stay buttons with every refusal unchanged. No name is asked while the arm is limp (automatic names; the rename pencil is enabled only while locked). The 3-2-1 countdown is UI only. A take with fewer than 2 points is „no motion". The real-arm preview holds every teaching key until the follower has been SEEN to stop on `/joint_states`, never on a timer. Esc locks and then shows the summary. The overlay can always be closed offline. The hook ignores keys on interactive targets; while the CollisionModal is up the overlay is `inert`. Entry is refused offline / while a program or preview runs / in the simulator / while „Steuern" has the arm freigeschaltet / during a home glide; while open, JogPanel (except „Arm festsetzen"), the LeaderToggle, drive-to and the sim entry are locked. The first kept take of an unsaved workflow creates it through the page's `saveWorkflowNow`.
- **Leader-arm Vormachen is a MODE of the same overlay, never a refusal.** `teach/teachGates.js::teachModeFor` picks `leader` when the bridge positively reports `leaderOn` and the profile does not say `has_leader === false`; the mode is fixed when `TeachHost` opens the overlay. **A rig that may have a leader never opens silently in hand mode**: while `teachLeaderStatus` is not `known`, `TeachHost` opens it unresolved (`mode: null`, rendered as `pending`) and `teachModeResolved` sets the mode on the first definite answer. In a resolved session `leaderStatusUnknown` blocks only new teaching actions. In leader mode a window offers only its kind's key (the Bewegung window Space, the Position window P, the Ziel window Z with its light-touch hint) and the session sends only `start_leader` / `stop_leader` / `cancel_leader` and captures (only from `bereit`, never during a take): no torque change, no `hand_guide`, no keepalive, no countdown, no preview, never a glide (`releasedOnce` stays false). **`leaderGone` needs a positive follower-only answer** (`available === true && followerOnly === true`). A collision discards three ways: during `aufnahme`, an answer landing after a trip the client already saw (collision generation compared at send time), and a returned take while the e-stop is up within `TEACH_LEADER_COLLISION_GRACE_MS` (1000) of its stop answer. **`useRobotActivation` is called only inside `teach/LeaderActivationGate`**, rendered only in leader mode (its selector reads `s.ros` unconditionally, and hand mode and the page tests have no such slice).
- **Recording clean-up (`utils/recordingCleanup.js`) only removes samples or shortens still gaps, in integer milliseconds**: the wait before the first move is cut to 300 ms, the review strip's handles drop samples, a release-looking end is suggested as the end, and „Pausen kürzen" (off by default) turns gaps over 1 s into 0.5 s. Joint values are copied untouched, the time column stays non-decreasing, every positive pair step ≥ 1 ms. It runs before `compactTrajectoryPoints`, and the same cleaned rows are what a keep uploads (with `duration_s` = their last time) and what „Auf dem Roboter ansehen" sends.
- **Ziel by touch: `teach/zielTouch.js::ZIEL_TOUCH_TCP_Z_M` is per arm** (OMX 0.04 = `ik_solver._TOOL_TIP_EXT_M`, lockstep-tested; Feetech 0.0) + a 30 mm tolerance. A too-high Z asks „Als Position speichern?" and defaults to a Position, which then stands in the Ziel window's list (owner decision D3); „Trotzdem als Ziel" stores the pin.
- **„Als Programm einfügen" builds one new top-level stack in one event group, never joined to an existing stack** (`teach/insertProgram.js`; top-level stacks run in creation order). A round holds one kind (one window). **„Greifer merken"**: a Position whose captured gripper state (`joint_positions` at the profile's gripper index, only when `joint_names` names the gripper joint) differs from the last known state gets a „Greifer schließen"/„öffnen" block after its move; a kept recording advances that state to its last row. Classification is `utils/armProfile.js::gripperBand` + `classifyGripper`, the same band SimScene's grasp-attach uses.
- **Cloud persistence** (migration 034 `workflow_trajectories`): a child table of workflows, RLS with the `(SELECT auth.uid())` form, not in the realtime publication, SQL-enforced prune cap 16. Six IDOR-gated routes in `routes/workflows.py` (POST/GET list/two GETs/PATCH rename/DELETE) plus clone carry-forward. `validate_trajectory` enforces profile-correct width, finite values, ≤`MAX_TRAJECTORY_POINTS` (5000), ≤256 KB and `0<fps≤1000`; names match `TRAJECTORY_NAME_RE` (1–40 chars) across backend/frontend/cloud. Execution stays 100 % local.
### Simulation world + no-go zones

- A `sim` sibling key inside `workflow_json` (no srv change) routes execution to `SimArm`/`SimPerception` (`/sim/joint_states`). Sim runs bypass the leader gate but still serialize via `on_workflow`.
- **The sim world is mutable, and `workflow/sim_world.py::SimWorld` is its single source of truth.** `SimArm` writes it (a gripper crossing of the profile's close threshold captures the NEAREST object within `_GRASP_CAPTURE_RADIUS_M` 0.06; a still-closed gripper carries it, an opening one releases it); `SimPerception` reads it. Rules: a held object stays detectable, tracking the gripper (the React twin renders `/sim/objects`, and a cube that blinks out reads as a broken simulator); capture is nearest-wins; only an object `SimPerception` resolved is graspable (`tag_id is not None`). `world=None` reproduces the old frozen behaviour. **The sim branch never reaches a real writer**, pinned by `test_preview_node_wiring.py` (AST + behaviour).
- **`/sim/objects` is authoritative only while a run is in flight; between runs the editor is the truth, and the server returns the world to the start state on every sim-run exit.** `SimScene` gates the server scene on `runState === 'running' || paused`; three branches: not running clears the grasp and resets `graspRef.closed`; running with no server scene leaves the local geometric guess alone (older image); running with a scene, the server wins. `handleEndEffector` carries its own run gate. `simSceneRef` mirrors the raw scene as a capability signal. Server-side `_reset_sim_scene` (world → placed objects, arm → Grundstellung, forced publish) runs from the sim-only `_on_sim_workflow_finished` hook on stop, finish and error; `workflow_stop_callback` resets only on its no-run-in-flight branch. **„Simulator zurücksetzen" reuses `/workflow/stop`** (local half first) and is disabled while a program runs (that route resolves the ACTIVE manager, possibly the real arm). Vocabulary is „Simulator", never „Simulation".
- **A preview is a sim run.** `utils/simPreview.js` builds the program (replay or `bewege zu` + `warte 1,5 s`), always sends the editor's objects + zones, uses `workflow_id` `vorschau-…`, never calls `/workshop/replay` or `/workshop/jog` (`hooks/useSimPreview.js`, driven by the play control on a Sammlung card and in the drawer). Refused client-side (`previewBlockReason`, in order) offline, while running, while the opened program is still loading (`loading`), while a version restore is on its way (`restoring`), during Vormachen, with the arm hand-guided, in a tutorial outside sim, with a LIVE LEADER (a sim run gates the teleop e-stop off; owner decision B1), while a live leader cannot be ruled out (`previewLeaderGate`: an unanswered bridge probe fails CLOSED unless the server or bridge says `has_leader === false`; before the probe's first settled answer, `useRsBridgeStatus().probed === false`, the reason is `leaderPending` and WorkshopPage pushes `capabilities.previewPending`, drawing every sim-run play control disabled with „Roboterstatus wird geprüft …"), for a cross-profile recording and for an unsaved workflow. Entering the simulator for it goes through `simEntryBlockReason` (shared with `WorkshopPage::ensureSimMode`). Results are kept per asset in `studioAssets.lastPreviewResult`, finalized only from the preview's own `workflow_id`, never becoming `debuggerWarnings`. Server refusals are shown verbatim except the replay lead-in sentence (`previewMessageDe`).
- **`SimArm._simulate_held` is identity-based**: with a world bound it answers `world.is_held()`. Deliberately not fixed (shared with the rig): `check_grasp_held` compares against `last_commanded_close + margin`, so an OPEN gripper reads HELD; changing it needs Rule §2 sign-off.
- **`SimArm` is cached for the node's lifetime while `SimPerception` is rebuilt per run.** `set_objects()` re-seeds `_last_q` to the profile HOME and resets the world.
- **The sim arm plays every chunk in real time.** The node builds `SimArm(frame_sink=self._publish_sim_frame)` (never `joint_state_sink=`, the burst path), and `sim_arm._TrajectoryPlayer` emits each waypoint at `receipt + time_from_start` on a daemon thread with JTC semantics: a newer chunk replaces the one in flight after flushing frames already due; `cancel()` (called first in `set_objects`) is atomic w.r.t. emission. What the runtime reads stays synchronous: `publish()` settles `_last_q` and updates the `SimWorld` per waypoint before returning. The sink is called under the player's lock, so nothing holding `_sim_pub_lock` may call `play`/`cancel`. No `frame_sink` = the legacy synchronous burst (every unit test and the golden fixture).
- **One lock, `_sim_pub_lock` (RLock), over every `/sim/*` publish and its cache; the heartbeat re-sends, never re-derives.** `_sim_idle_republish` fires whenever `/sim/joint_states` has been quiet `_SIM_HEARTBEAT_QUIET_S` (0.45 s), mid-run too, and re-sends `_last_sim_objects_json` + `_last_sim_joints` under the lock. Do not re-gate it on `on_workflow` (`_on_sim_workflow_finished` clears that flag BEFORE it resets; the lock closes the race).
- **The sim has a table floor**: `load_calibration` supplies exactly `{'z_table': 0.0}` (adding `board_table_z` would switch it onto the pixel-projection path). Not the forbidden fallback: the virtual table is DEFINED as z=0.
- **`/sim/objects`** (`std_msgs/String` + JSON): `{epoch, held, objects:[{key,type,tag_id,x,y,yaw}]}`, `key` the index in the list the editor sent (`SimWorld.bind_tag` is where the two id spaces meet). React derives `heldObjectId` from `held`; the local guess is the fallback for an older image.
- **The `edubotics_when_object_seen` hat fires once per OBJECT**: the trigger returns the `frozenset` of unclaimed-visible tag ids and the edge is on that set changing. `when_counter_gt` keeps plain armed/disarmed semantics. A `WorkflowError` out of a hat body logs in German.
- **Integrated sim stage**: in `simMode` WorkshopPage swaps the RightDock for `SimStage`; the left `BlocklyWorkspace` column is never remounted. `SimScene layout="split"` keeps sole ownership of the single lazy `UrdfTwin` + the grasp-attach state. Sim entry is blocked while a tutorial is active. `catalogDims` sizes/colours objects and caps 2D placement per type (`simConstants.resolveMaxInstances` → `DEFAULT_MAX_INSTANCES`). The reach ring + 2D annulus share `simConstants.reachAnnulus(caps)`, falling back to `REACH_INNER_M/OUTER_M` (0.10/0.28, deliberately not the solver's wrist-centre span). `simConstants.js` must stay `three`-free. SimScene's editor modes are `object` / `zone` / `ziel`; a tap is clamped to the view window, never the reach annulus. **A sim-table Ziel is `kind:'pin', source:'sim', z: 0`**; on a calibrated real rig it re-asks the measured plane. On a rig with no touch-off the stored 0 is kept and the OMX fingertips would drive 40 mm into the table; the UI cannot start that run only because `WorkshopPage::showEditor` hides `RunControls` outside the simulator until `calibrated` (pinned in `WorkshopPage.simToggle.test.jsx`); **keep that gate** (R15 in `docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`).
- **No-go zones + IK path-guard auto-avoid** (`workflow/path_guard.py`, ladder direct → lift-and-travel → base-swing → refuse); the cloud validates zones + tempo inside `sim_scene` (033). The ladder's four geometry constants are arm-sized and profile-driven (`SAFE_TRAVEL_Z`, `_TOOL_CLEAR_M`, `_SWING_HEIGHTS`, `_SWING_RADII` ← `ArmProfile` fields; `None` → the module constant, so OMX stays bit-identical). `_TOOL_CLEAR_M` is 0.05 m on the OMX, 0 on both Feetech arms. Adding an arm ⇒ re-derive these against `solve()`.
- **`LINK_RADIUS_M + ZONE_MARGIN_M = 0.05 m` per face must not be shrunk or raised, globally or per profile.** The guard is already ~34 mm under-covered on edu6 (link6 girth is not sampled), and a raise would refuse every in-band zone. Both directions are Rule §2 changes. `test_path_guard_inflation_is_not_secretly_shrunk` pins the constants and the absence of a per-profile override. The real fix is `edu6_ik.link_points` sampling link6's girth (rig gate R7).
- **A zone that overlaps the ARM rather than its path is diagnosed as such**: `_static_overlap_refusal` short-circuits after the direct rung with a German message naming the robot and the 5 cm inflation.
- **Server-side workflow caps + pre-check**: `/workflow/start` rejects payloads > `MAX_WORKFLOW_JSON_BYTES` (256 KiB) and > `MAX_HAT_HANDLERS=16` ENABLED hats (`split_roots` drops a disabled hat; a disabled MAIN root is still walked so `_exec_chain` can follow its `next` chain); `_ik_precheck` returns `unreachable_blocks` as non-fatal German warnings. Hats are edge-triggered. `collect_concrete_destinations` resolves the way the runtime does (pin statement name→xyz first, then `destination_ref` value blocks), collecting pins across all main roots before any ref resolves; a disabled block never warns; a non-finite label value is not concrete. A JSON integer too large for a float (`OverflowError`) is a malformed value on every `/workflow/start` sibling parser (`_parse_destinations`, `_parse_tempo`, `path_guard._parse_minmax`).

### Blockly ↔ server contract

Block definitions live in React (`components/Workshop/blocks/*.js`); `edubotics_*` type ids + field/input names must match the server dispatch (`workflow/handlers/__init__.py::STATEMENT_HANDLERS` + `VALUE_EVALUATORS`, `interpreter.py::HAT_BLOCK_TYPES`). Guards: CI `tutorials-validate` (tutorial `allowed_blocks` vs handler keys) + the vitest `toolbox.consistency.test.js` (unions `SAMMLUNG_BASE_BLOCKS` for coverage and compares `TOOLBOX` with a fresh `buildToolbox()`), both blocking via `ci.yml::react-tests`. `pythonCodeGen.js` is a display-only mirror that mirrors the serializer's order: `generateWorkspace` calls `getTopBlocks(false)` (creation order), because `RunControls.handleStart` spreads `blocks.blocks` verbatim and `Interpreter.execute` walks it front-to-back.

**Two inline sentinels carry runtime state to the Debug-Panel and are not interchangeable**: `[VAR:name=json]` (`interpreter.py::_set_variable`, Blockly variables) and `[CNT:name=int]` (`handlers/counters.py::_emit`, the „Zähler" blocks), with separate Redux maps (`workshop.variables` / `workshop.counters`) because „Punkte" is a plausible name for both. Only the write paths emit (`counters.reset`, `counters.add`; never `get` or the hat's `get_count` poll), outside `ctx.var_lock` with the value captured under it (`ctx.log` IS the status publisher).

**The interpreter fails loud in German** (`InterpreterError`): divide/modulo-by-zero and complex power results, a cross-type `logic_compare` ordering, a zero or non-finite `controls_for` step, a nameless `procedures_call*` (name read before arguments). `_number_or` never coerces an explicit `0`. **Caps**: `MAX_LOOP_ITERATIONS=10000`; `MAX_LIST_ITEMS=1000` (`lists_repeat`, `lists_setIndex` INSERT); `MAX_LIST_CREATE_ITEMS=20` on `lists_create_with`, refusing when the filled sockets exceed the cap OR any index reaches it (ours, not the mutator's, which caps nothing; `blocks/savedValueWarnings.js` mirrors it); `MAX_TEXT_CHARS=10000` on `text_join`, checked on the running total (four blocks once reached 2.4 GB RSS in 5 s); `_MAX_VAR_PAYLOAD_ITEMS=200` then `_MAX_VAR_PAYLOAD_CHARS=2000` on the `[VAR:]` sentinel. Every cap raises rather than truncates. **Exactly two things are skipped silently, both Blockly's own semantics**: a disabled block (`_is_disabled`, both serialization shapes; a disabled statement's `next` chain is still followed) and a parked value block. `_BUILTIN_VALUE_TYPES` must stay exactly the set `_eval_value_impl` handles, and `lists_getIndex` is in it, so its btype-ladder branch stays gated on `_is_list_get_statement` (only MODE=REMOVE is a statement). **Every block the `_exec_block` btype ladder dispatches natively needs a manual entry in `tutorials-validate`'s allowlist**: `edubotics_forever`, `edubotics_wait_until`, `edubotics_while_visible`, `edubotics_broadcast`, and the built-ins `controls_*`, `variables_set`, `math_change`, `lists_setIndex`, `lists_getIndex`, `procedures_*`. `WAIT_UNTIL_MAX_SECONDS=300` is a plain constant, not an env var (an env name would trip `env-forwarding-guard`).

### Editor UI invariants (React)

- **Ziele and Positionen are document content, held by the `edubotics-destinations` workspace serializer** (`sammlung/destinationStore.js`, priority 90, one store per workspace in a `WeakMap`). On the SAVE allowlist, not on RUN; `RunControls` sends `destinations: [{name, kind, x, y, z}]` as an explicit sibling, always (possibly `[]`), since its presence makes the server treat the document as authoritative. `load` is total (drops invalid entries, never throws); `save` returns null when empty. Every store mutation fires the undoable `edubotics_destinations_change` event; load/clear never fire. The serializer and event are registered in `registerAllBlocksOnce`, never at import. A code document holds the same entries in a detached store (no workspace, no Blockly events) and saves them under the same key.
- **RightDock** (`components/Workshop/RightDock.jsx`): panels render via `tab.render()` invoked inside stable-keyed `<section>`s, never `<tab.render/>`, so a dock re-render never remounts a live JogPanel (a remount re-torques the arm). Busy-pin: a panel holding a live session cannot be closed/evicted; at the 2-panel cap the oldest non-busy panel is evicted, else the dock grows to 3. The dock is controlled (`WorkshopPage` owns `openIds`/`collapsed`/`width`). Below `md` the expanded dock is `h-1/2`; `SimStage` is capped `max-h-[50%]` there.
- **The Blockly canvas must be exactly the box the student sees and hear every size change** (`BlocklyWorkspace.jsx`): Blockly 12 re-measures only on a window `resize`, so the host is watched by a `ResizeObserver` → `Blockly.svgResize`. **No min-height anywhere in the editor's `overflow-hidden` chain.** The Code-Vorschau `<pre>` is capped `min(16rem, 22vh)`.
  - **Plugin modules are evaluated BEFORE the first `inject()`.** `Blockly.Css.register` throws „CSS already injected" once any workspace exists, and backpack/zoom-to-fit register CSS at module top level. `loadPluginModules()` is one module-level promise the injection effect awaits; plugins are initialised synchronously after inject and before the initial `workspaces.load`. Deliberately not loaded: `@blockly/workspace-minimap` and the multiselect plugin (both stay in `package.json`, unimported). `germanizeBackpackMenu` assigns the backpack's five English `Blockly.Msg` keys before any `Backpack` is constructed. Nothing in the shared `.then` may throw (cached for the page's lifetime).
  - **Corner controls: core first, never half-drawn.** Backpack and zoom-to-fit are re-registered at weight 10/11 (`positionPluginControlsAfterCoreControls`), after the zoom cluster. After every `resize()`, a control whose rectangle does not fit whole is `display:none` and a hidden trash/backpack loses `DRAG_TARGET` (`hideCornerControlsThatDoNotFit`); `CORNER_CONTROLS` is in priority order and the cascade drops every lower-priority control once one is dropped, so the shown set is monotone in height.
  - The wheel scrolls; Ctrl/⌘+wheel and pinch zoom (`move.wheel` AND `zoom.wheel` on). The empty „Vorschläge" category's English label is swapped for `DE.SUGGESTED_EMPTY`.
  - Pinned by `components/Workshop/__tests__/BlocklyWorkspace.test.jsx` (real Blockly, real plugin modules, real inject into jsdom); it must stay the first thing in its module graph to inject a workspace.
- **The four Sammlung toolbox groups „Variablen / Aufnahmen / Ziele / Positionen" are dynamic categories** (`blocks/toolbox.js`: `SAMMLUNG_CATEGORY_KEYS`, `SAMMLUNG_TOOLBOX_IDS`). `sammlung/toolboxCategories.js::registerSammlungCategories` registers their callbacks on every workspace (teacher page and read-only previews too: an unregistered custom key throws when opened). The page talks to them only through `sammlung/provider.js` (`EMPTY_SAMMLUNG_PROVIDER` is the default). `restrictedBlocks` is applied inside the callbacks with exactly `filterContents`'s rule (an empty list is unrestricted). The replay and destination name fields stay `field_input`; a name the program uses but the workflow lacks gets the keyed canvas warning `edubotics_asset_missing` (`sammlung/referenceValidators.js`), re-applied forced after every `debuggerWarnings` change (RunControls writes unkeyed, and an unkeyed `setWarningText(null)` disposes the whole icon). Cards, not Plan B (`SAMMLUNG_FLYOUT_MODE = 'cards'`): a card is a custom flyout item (`sammlung/AssetCardInflater.js`, class under `FLYOUT_INFLATER`); its play and more controls are icon SVG (`components/icons/svg.js::appendSvgIcon`) and bind pointerdown AND pointerup with `conditionalBind(…, true)` (a default-capture binding that stops propagation leaves the pointer id held and the editor then ignores every later touch).
  - **The creation buttons come from ONE table, `sammlung/newActions.js::NEW_ACTION_ROWS`** (id, tab, German label, icon, action, `allowed`), read by the flyout, the drawer (both notations) and the code sidebar: Vormachen and the camera pin only on the rig outside the simulator, the Sim-Tisch pin only inside it (owner decision D9); the three teach rows carry the kind icons (D10). Their flyout callback keys (`SAMMLUNG_BUTTON_KEYS`, via `NEW_ACTION_BUTTON_KEYS`) are the old ones.
  - **The flyout draws them as `edubotics_icon_button` items (`sammlung/IconButtonInflater.js`), by COMPOSITION, never a Blockly subclass evaluated at import**: nine page test files mock `blockly/core` with only `svgResize`/`Events`, and WorkshopPage reaches this module through `toolboxCategories.js`, so a top-level `extends Blockly.ButtonFlyoutInflater` would take every page test down. Blockly's own `ButtonFlyoutInflater`, built lazily, creates a real `FlyoutButton` (its callback, keyboard navigation, focus and touch stay Blockly's) and `decorateFlyoutButton` then adds the icon group (painted explicitly, so the button's fill cannot leak in) and widens both rects and `width` by the icon and its gap. It depends on FlyoutButton's DOM (a shadow rect, a background rect, one centred text), throws when that shape changes, and `sammlung/__tests__/IconButtonInflater.test.jsx` holds it to the cards' criteria (a)–(f) in the real flyout (`docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`, Open risk R4). `registerSammlungCategories` registers the inflater itself too (idempotent), since its flyouts emit the type. Plan B's play/more buttons are icon buttons as well. **A decoration failure costs only the icon**: `IconButtonInflater.load` catches it, warns once and returns Blockly's plain button (still of the icon-button type), and `decorateFlyoutButton` draws the icon before it changes anything and removes a half-drawn icon on a throw, so a Blockly bump can strip icons but never empty a category; the strict shape test is what must catch it. The icon is sized from the button text's rendered font size (`iconMetricsForFont`: 14 px at Blockly's 11 pt, 20 px at the high-contrast theme's 16 pt).
- **The BlocklyWorkspace host isolates Blockly's stacking context** (`style={{ isolation: 'isolate' }}`): the toolbox is `position:absolute; z-index:70`, so without it the category column paints over the Sammlung drawer, the Vormachen overlay and the CollisionModal. Never raise a page layer above 70 instead.
- **The Sammlung lives in the editor column, never in the dock.** `sammlung/SammlungDrawer.jsx` is absolutely positioned beside the toolbox, must not change the box's size, and closes on a `TOOLBOX_ITEM_SELECT` with a non-empty `newItem`, on the Galerie switch and when the tab is left. It offers its tab's „Neu" row (the `newActions` table, with icons) in BOTH notations (owner decision D5); „Einfügen" and dragging a row into the editor stay code-only.
- **Deleting a Sammlung item never deletes blocks** (the keyed warning explains the orphans; „Rückgängig" re-creates recording rows oldest first, waiting out the cloud's `POST /workflows` 10/min budget (`RESTORE_RATE_LIMIT_WAIT_MS` × `RESTORE_RATE_LIMIT_RETRIES`), any other failure stops the restore with `ERR_UNDO_PARTIAL`). **Renaming a recording renames the cloud rows first, rewrites every replay block in one event group, saves, and on a failed save compensates the CLOUD first** (`sammlung/assetCommands.js::renameRecording`); otherwise the blocks keep the cloud's name and a persistent banner (`studioAssets.renameSplit`) says „Bitte erneut speichern". `renameVariable` checks `getVariable(name, type)` first with the trimmed name (Blockly merges into an existing case variant silently).
- **`UrdfTwin` (`components/UrdfTwin.jsx`) paints on demand: every scene mutation must set `needsRender`.** `urdf-loader`'s `onComplete` frames nothing; `loadingManager.onLoad` is the sole framing pass, skipped once OrbitControls has fired `'start'`. Sammlung markers are a `markers` prop on the same twin (`sammlung/markers.js::buildTwinMarkers`, capped at 80, diffed by id, disposed including the label's `CanvasTexture`). `poseSource` (Daten 2.0) is a function the render loop asks once per frame for `{version, pose, ghost}`: while it is set the twin opens no rosbridge subscription and applies a RECORDED pose (and a translucent ghost in the given colour, the recorded leader action in violet) only when `version` changed; its ghost is its OWN clone, never the `ghostJoints` one, so neither layer's cleanup removes the other's. `ghostJoints` draws a translucent clone of the loaded robot (`robot.clone(true)`, built once after `onLoad`, one ghost material); **the clone shares the live robot's geometries: removing it disposes only its material**, and the mount teardown detaches it before the scene traverse. WorkshopPage feeds it the highlighted Position only when `utils/armProfile.js::ghostJointsFromEntry` says the snapshot fits this arm. `_ensure_sim_publisher()` + `_seed_sim_rest_pose()` run at boot, between `_init_core_components()` and `_init_robot_profile()`.
- **`hasJointData` is not a latch**: a 1 s watchdog returns the twin to „Wartet auf Gelenkdaten …" after `JOINT_STALE_MS` (3 s).
- **Every 3D twin draws an interpolated pose, never the newest message** (`utils/jointStateInterpolator.js`, pure): snapshot interpolation on `header.stamp`, a fixed `INTERP_DELAY_MS` (100) behind the data, clock offset = sliding-window min of (arrival − stamp), no extrapolation, STEP across a gap > `INTERP_MAX_GAP_MS` (250) or a per-joint change needing > `INTERP_MAX_JOINT_SPEED_RAD_S` (20); render time never runs backwards. **A teleport is a wire contract**: `physical_ai_server::_publish_sim_teleport` re-sends the pose the twin is showing, back-dated `_SIM_RESET_HOLD_S` (3 ms), then the Grundstellung; both halves are load-bearing. `UrdfTwin::stepPose` runs from the rAF loop and on arrival, re-applying only past `RENDER_DEADBAND_RAD` (1 mrad) while the pose is a blend. Throttles: real `/joint_states` 30 ms, sim `SimScene::SIM_JOINT_THROTTLE_MS` 20, `/sim/objects` 20. `useSimObjects` delivers a new epoch at once, skips a scene in which only the held object moved, and `SimScene` calls it with `{delayMs: INTERP_DELAY_MS}`; **the two delays are one constant.**
- **Every server-pushed Roboter-Studio field needs an event that retires it; `session/signedOut` alone is not that event.** `counters`: cleared by `RunControls.handleStart` beside `clearVariables()`, by a document switch and by `signedOut`. `detections`: cleared by `setRunState`'s terminal branch, never its `'running'` branch (fires per block tick). `workflowError`: `clearWorkflowError()` at the top of `handleStart`, before the service call. `breakpoints`, `variables`, `variableHistory` and `counters`: cleared in the `setSelectedWorkflowId` reducer, guarded `prev && prev !== next` (a run's values belong to the program that produced them; a code document lists every name `workshop.variables` holds; null → id is also the first SAVE of an unsaved document, which must keep them), by the `openWorkflow` reducer on every real change INCLUDING null → id (the student OPENS another program: „Öffnen", a gallery clone — `WorkshopPage::handlePickWorkflow`, `GalleryTab`), and by `WorkshopPage::handleNewProgram` for the one switch neither can see (unsaved to unsaved). `signedOut` resets the run too (`runState`/`phase`/`progress`/`paused` plus `detections`/`sensorSnapshot` via `emptySensorSnapshot()` at `ts: 0`). `variableHistory` (`VAR_HISTORY_LIMIT` 5, a separate map so `variables[name]` stays `{value, ts}`) is retired by `clearVariables`, a document switch and `signedOut`; a point-shaped value becomes a violet twin marker (`sammlung/markers.js::variablePointsFromValues`), routed by `WorkshopPage::previewAsset` before the sim-preview delegation. `studioAssets.preview` is finalized only by a `/workflow/status` carrying its own `workflow_id`, by a terminal `setRunState` after one such status (`sawOwnStatus`), or by `previewEnded` (a student's Stopp or Start). A document switch retires the recording list, the drawer focus, the preview results, the highlight and a rename split.
- **`utils/variableName.js::isDisplayableVariableName` is the one name gate for both inspector sentinels, at four call sites** (`useRosTopicSubscription`'s `[VAR:]` and `[CNT:]` branches, `workshopSlice::setVariable` / `::setCounter`); a second, stricter copy is the defect, not a safety net (that IS what RS-49 was). It refuses only empty/whitespace-only names, >64 chars (`VAR_NAME_MAX_LEN`), control characters and the frame's own `=`/`[`/`]`; **never re-narrow it to an identifier shape** (a student may name a variable „Anzahl Würfel"). `__proto__`/`constructor`/`prototype` stay out of the predicate and are re-checked at each site. `blocks/counters.js::counterNameValidator` is a weaker opinion that must not be tightened (a field validator also runs during deserialization). The `[CNT:]` frame's name capture is greedy (`/^\[CNT:(.+)=(-?\d+)\]$/s`) where `[VAR:]`'s is `[^=]+`; the difference is load-bearing (a counter value is digits-only; a `[VAR:]` value is JSON). Counter field caps at 40 (`NAME_MAX_LEN`), the gate at 64; the `[CNT:]` value at 15 digits; `setCounter` refuses a non-integer. `VariableInspector` renders two `<section aria-label=…>` regions, „Variablen" and „Zähler" (the heading „Zähler" is itself a legal variable name, so tests scope with `within(getByRole('region'))`). Server-side, `interpreter._UNSHOWABLE_NAME_CHARS` (`=[]`) makes `_set_variable` suppress the `[VAR:]` frame with one `[WARNUNG]` per name per run. A Blockly variable id is not usable as a sentinel name (`genUid`'s alphabet contains `=`); `_read_variable_name`'s raw-id fallback is unreachable today, and never widen its reach without changing the frame. Tests split the same way: the pure contract, the wire call site in both directions, the store, and the rendered outcome (`VariableInspector.test.jsx`); a predicate-only test cannot see this class of bug.
- **Variable NAMES are resolved server-side.** A saved workspace stores a variable reference as `fields: {"VAR": {"id": "…"}}`, so `Interpreter.__init__(variable_names=)`, fed by `from_json`'s top-level `variables` plus each `procedures_def*`'s `extraState.params`, maps id → name.
- **`SensorPanel`'s „veraltet" banner is reachable but not deterministic, a deliberate non-fix** (`docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`).
- `CameraFeedOverlay` strips one `/compressed` for the stream URL only; its `fill` prop keeps click-to-mark pixel-accurate. `UrdfTwin` is read-only (never publishes).
- **Two allowlists over one primitive, and they must stay different** (`utils/blocklyPayload.js::pickSerializerKeys`). Six workspace serializers exist: core `blocks`/`variables`/`workspaceComments`, plus `suggested-blocks`, `backpack` and `edubotics-destinations`. RUN keeps `blocks` + `variables`. SAVE additionally keeps `workspaceComments` and `edubotics-destinations` and drops the two plugin keys (`suggested-blocks` grows ~16 bytes per drag and is never trimmed; `backpack` is one student's private clipboard in a row siblings read). AUTOSAVE alone keeps the full output (local IndexedDB, per-student), storing the slimmed document when the full output would blow the 256 KB cap. An empty workspace serializes with no `blocks` key, so a missing key stays missing. The cloud's 384 KB body middleware guards the SAVE path only. Disclosed cost: a backpack stash comes back only through an autosave restore, never through a cloud-loaded workflow.
- **Two Blockly-side affordances in `blocks/control.js`**: (a) `attachControlWorkspaceValidators` flags any block under „wiederhole fortlaufend" as dead code via a keyed `setWarningText(…, 'edubotics_forever_dead_code')` (removing the block's `nextStatement` is not backward-safe: `workspaces.load` throws MissingConnection and the next edit autosaves the truncated program); (b) `registerControlsIfElseMutator` re-registers `controls_if_mutator` after the dynamic `import('@blockly/block-plus-minus')` in `loadPluginModules` (the plugin's `plus()` only ever calls `addElseIf_`, so a dragged „wenn" could never grow a „sonst"), and that ordering is also what makes ⊕/⊖ the only mutator UI a student sees. The saved shape is unchanged (`elseIfCount` then `hasElse` literal `true`, `null` for a bare if). Proven in `blocks/__tests__/controlsIfElse.test.js` + `controlsIfElseBackCompat.test.js`.
- **Deserialization never rewrites a value a student saved: field validators guard EDITS, not LOADS.** `Blockly.serialization` sets each saved value through the validator, so every name rule was also judging bytes on disk („Ablage.1" → „Ablage1", a name cut to 24, `Bewegung [2]` → `Bewegung 1`), reaching Redux and the autosave on the same tick. The seam is `Field.loadState`: `blocks/fieldLoad.js::setEditValidator` wraps it and every name validator goes through that. **Never call `field.setValidator` directly again in this folder.** The editor half is `blocks/savedValueWarnings.js` (keyed warnings for a refused name and an oversized `lists_create_with`, both halves of its rule). Numeric clamps are deliberately still enforced on load (they only arrive from a hand-edited file). `blocks/__tests__/loadKeepsSavedValues.test.js` pins the round trip.
- **The object type on a block is decided by the student.** `blocks/perception.js::makeObjectTypeField`'s `loadState` remembers a saved `OBJECT_TYPE` the catalog does not (yet) contain and offers it as „… (unbekannt)"; `refreshObjectTypeDropdowns` promotes only the `__none__` placeholder. This is the ordinary lesson-start path: the catalog fetch waits for `calibrated || simMode` while `EDUBOTICS_FORCE_RECALIBRATION` ships ON. The shipped list lives in `blocks/objectCatalogDefaults.js` (its own module because the page tests mock `perception.js`) and seeds the teacher-web template editor.
- **Removing a „Funktion" parameter must not re-shuffle the arguments.** `blocks/procedures.js::registerProcedureCallArgumentFix` patches the two call-block tables after the plugin import (Blockly keeps arguments attached only while the definition's mutator bubble is open, and the plugin writes `paramid` where core reads `paramId`). Identity is keyed on ids, falling back to names, applied when the shape shrinks and grows (the grow case is the undo); removed arguments are unplugged with events on, survivors re-seated inside `Blockly.Events.disable()`. `blocks/__tests__/procedureParams.test.js`.
- **No string a student can see in the editor is English.** `Blockly.Msg.PROCEDURE_VARIABLE` is assigned inside `loadPluginModules`'s `.then()`; `germanizeSearchBar` fixes the Ctrl+F bar's four hardcoded strings on the DOM after `init()`; `blankNonGermanHelpUrls` empties every `*_HELPURL` whose HOST is not `de.wikipedia.org` (classify by host, never by a key list: 24 of the 62 are German pages worth keeping).
- **Editor state: the Blockly editor is remounted only when the open document really changes.** `WorkshopPage`'s hydrate effect keys on `!!accessToken` and reads the token through a ref (a Supabase `TOKEN_REFRESHED` rotates the string), and skips once the id the page itself just created (`skipHydrateForIdRef`). **Every save goes through `WorkshopPage::saveWorkflowNow`, which never joins a save in flight**: a later caller gets one coalesced follow-up that serialises when it starts; the follow-up of a create is an update. **A save, a restore and a run act on the document the editor HOLDS, never on the one merely selected** (owner decision R5-O1, review round 5 MD2/MD3/MD5, Blockly and code alike). `selectedWorkflowId` moves the moment the student picks a program; the editor holds it only once its row arrived. So the page keeps the open document's identity itself: `openDocId` (its cloud row, set when a row arrived or a save created one; null for an unsaved document — „Neu", a draft, a cleared selection — AND after a load that failed, so a save then creates a row instead of overwriting the one that failed to load) and a document token (`docTokenRef`, bumped the moment the page starts replacing the document — a load, „Neu", a draft, a version restore; `getDocumentToken` hands it to the code insertion and RunControls). Both are written through refs in the same call as their state. RunControls, the history, the Sammlung drawer, Vormachen, the recording list and the sim preview all get `openDocId`. `runSave` refuses while a program loads (`DE.DOCUMENT_LOADING`) or a version restore is on its way (`DE.VERSION_RESTORE_IN_FLIGHT`) — every caller comes through it: the button, the window-level Strg+S, a rename, a keep, „Abgeben" — takes `openDocId` and the token WITH the document, marks the document saved only while that token is still the open one, and stamps a created id only onto that same document (a „Neu" opened meanwhile is unsaved too, and once received the id); a queued follow-up is dropped, and its caller told so, when the token moved. What a save serialises is the live canvas only while it was mounted for the open document (`workspaceDocTokenRef`), else the document's last reported edit, else what it opened with; every page path that replaces a Blockly document resets the page's copy of the last edit and writes the store's copy through `setUnsavedJson` synchronously (an effect ran a render too late, and a save — or Start, through RunControls' `blocklyJson` — used the PREVIOUS program's last edit under the new id). A version restore result is applied only while the document open when it was asked for is still open (`handleVersionRestored`: the same `openDocId` and token, and the row it names), else dropped. Every failure returns its German reason as `error`; `toastOnError: false` suppresses the page toast. Pinned in `pages/__tests__/WorkshopPage.hydrate.test.jsx` and `WorkshopPage.documentIdentity.test.jsx` (both notations, the real toolbar's Strg+S). **No document switch while a program runs or stands at a breakpoint** (owner decision R2-O3, Blockly and code alike), **and only while the robot link is alive**: `WorkshopPage::switchLockReason` (`selectWorkflowRunning` AND `heartbeatStatus === 'connected'`, exactly like `utils/signOut::logoutBlockReason`, since a dead link can never retire the run state → `DE.STOP_PROGRAM_FIRST`, „Stoppe zuerst dein Programm.") disables, with that reason as the title, every action that replaces the open document — „Öffnen" (`TemplatePicker`), the gallery's clone (`GalleryTab`), „Neu" (`NewProgramDialog`) and a version restore (`VersionHistoryDropdown`: the history button is disabled, and a list opened before the run keeps its „laden" buttons clickable so each is refused BEFORE the cloud is asked, or the cloud row and the editor would part) — and the handlers refuse it too (`refuseSwitch`: a popover opened before the run, a clone that finishes after it started is created but not opened). The automatic draft restore at mount is not a switch (it restores the document the student had). **Two more windows lock every switch the same way** (the lock reason, then `refuseSwitch`, which reads refs so a click React has not re-rendered for is refused too): a version restore on its way (`DE.VERSION_RESTORE_IN_FLIGHT`; a late restore of A once replaced B's editor and the next save wrote A into B), and the START window (`DE.PROGRAM_STARTING`, „Das Programm startet gerade – bitte kurz warten."): RunControls reports `onStartingChange(true)` before its first await (the breakpoints and the recordings are fetched before the run is marked running) and `false` in its `finally`, and does not send a start whose document token moved anyway (`DE.PROGRAM_CHANGED_BEFORE_START`). The history also takes no restore while a program loads (`DE.DOCUMENT_LOADING`), starts, or a save is on its way (`DE.SAVE_IN_FLIGHT`: a restore landing under a save parted the cloud row from the editor). The reverse race is closed too: while a restore is on its way (`onRestoringChange`), RunControls' Start is disabled with `DE.VERSION_RESTORE_IN_FLIGHT` and no preview starts, and `VersionHistoryDropdown` runs ONE restore at a time (every other „laden" disabled and saying why, plus an in-flight guard for a click React has not re-rendered yet; two overlapping restores once told the page [true, true, false] and unlocked it mid-restore). **While the program the student opened is still being fetched** (`hydrating`), Start and every preview refuse with `DE.DOCUMENT_LOADING` („Das Programm wird noch geladen – bitte kurz warten."): the page still holds the OLD program's files and Ziele under the NEW id (`WorkshopPage::startBlockedReason`, previewBlockReason's `loading`). The page names a new document the moment the fetch starts (the document token), so an insertion finishing in that window writes nothing. `openCodeDocument` ends the previous code document's Ziele-store subscription, so the old store can never mark the next document's Ziele as changed. Pinned by `WorkshopPage.switchLock.test.jsx`, `WorkshopPage.documentIdentity.test.jsx` and `components/Workshop/__tests__/documentSwitchLock.test.jsx`.
- Autosave scope key: the Supabase user id is `s.auth.session.user.id`, not `s.auth.user.id` (always null).
- Destination-pin names cap at 24 chars in React (the Blockly field's `NAME_MAX_LEN`); trajectory names use the full 1-40 range. A third destination-name validator: `CameraFeedOverlay` validates the label it is handed by `WorkshopPage::resolveMarkLabel` and rejects at 40 through the shared `blocks/destinations.js::destinationNameErrorDe`, whose text mirrors `handlers/destinations.py::destination_name_error_de`; the sentence states the alphabet and no character count (any number is wrong on one of the three surfaces). All three trim. The inline rename field sanitises like the Blockly field via `sammlung/destinationStore.js::sanitizeDestinationNameInput`.
## Recording, data, inference (physical_ai_server)

- **Every client-supplied path from rosbridge is confined by `data_processing/dataset_paths.py`, and "dataset path" is not the boundary.** Login-gating is browser-side only; the whole rosbridge surface stays untrusted. `confine()` judges where a path LANDS (`resolve()`, symlinks followed, ancestry over components; `<root>-evil` is outside `<root>`; non-str refused). Primitives: `safe_child` (one component), `safe_under` (multi-component under one root), `confine_any` (any of several roots; an empty root list refuses). Four root sets, not interchangeable: `dataset_root()` (edit/delete/read), `browsable_roots()` = datasets + models, `policy_roots()` = `model_root()` + `hf_hub_cache_root()`, and `MODEL_ROOT_RELATIVE` (`ros2_ws/outputs/train`), read by `training_manager.get_weight_save_root_path`, `download_huggingface_repo`, React `paths.js`. **Confine by arrival from the wire, never by what the parameter is called** (`get_training_info_callback` takes a MODEL path; `ControlHfServer.local_dir`, `GetDatasetInfo.dataset_path`, `TaskInfo.policy_path` are all wire paths). The `local_dir` confine is scoped to `mode == 'upload'`; refusals must not echo the caller's path into a toast (an arbitrary-read oracle).
  - **`ControlHfServer.repo_id` is not a path, so `mode='delete'` is refused outright** (`control_hf_server_callback`, before any worker dispatch); it reached `HfApi().delete_repo` with zero callers. `hf_api_worker`'s delete branch and `DataManager.delete_huggingface_repo` stay, wire-unreachable. Not the same `'delete'` as `edit_worker.MODE_DELETE`.
  - One residual: `START_INFERENCE` passes the raw `task_info.policy_path` to `read_policy_summary` after `validate_policy`, safe today by ordering only. (`data_manager.download_huggingface_repo` is confined since Daten 2.0: `save_dir = dataset_paths.safe_under(save_path, repo_id)`, and a dataset target that already exists is refused with `DOWNLOAD_EXISTS_DE` — the old page never mixes files into an existing dataset.)
  - The Daten engine has its own confinement on top: `v3_surgery.confine` builds every path a dataset makes it open from LeRobot's DEFAULT templates with integer indices under the dataset root, symlinks followed; a path a dataset names itself is never opened.
- **Dataset uploads are private at the WIRE, public on the PAGE, and namespace-guarded.** `TaskInfo.msg` carries `bool private_mode true`, so a client that omits the field gets a private repo; `_upload_dataset(private=True)` matches. **Never flip the `.msg` default**: it is the fail-safe for a hand-crafted rosbridge call that says nothing. The student page's default `taskSlice.defaultTaskInfo.privateMode` is `false` (owner decision N7, Daten 2.0): the cloud training reads a dataset with the platform token, which cannot read a student's private repo, and the owner accepted that classroom video is then public by default; „Privat" is one click away under „Erweitert", the save-name line says ÖFFENTLICH, and a private dataset's „Weiter zum Training" is disabled with its German reason. React always sends the field explicitly. `useRosTopicSubscription` gates the adopt of `task_info.private_mode` on `robotNamesMe` (another student's task never sets this student's switch), with `!== false` so an absent flag reads private. `_upload_dataset` refuses a namespace the rig's token does not own (the whoami ACCOUNT only since Daten 2.0, organisations refused too: `DataManager.get_huggingface_user_id` returns the account alone, and `hub_sync.upload` re-checks it inside every dataset upload), naming neither namespace nor repo in the toast; it refuses on PROOF only (no token / timeout / offline all ALLOW; keep that fail-open unchanged).
- **Recording FSM** (`data_processing/data_manager.py`): warm-up once, then run → save → reset per episode, no reset after the last. MOVE_TO_NEXT (`record_early_save`) skips a warm-up or reset and saves early only a run ≥ `EARLY_SAVE_MIN_S` (1 s); a wire RERECORD (`rerecord_from_command`) is refused once the episode is committed and stamps `_wire_rerecord_at`; FINISH drops a run < 1 s and any run that started ≤ `RERECORD_FINISH_WINDOW_S` (5 s) after a wire RERECORD, counts a committed partial episode, and still ends on a streaming frame drop; `re_record()` is a no-op in `finish`/`stop` (a collision never reopens a finishing session); an EMPTY writer buffer is nothing to commit; STOP counts only a committed episode; a finishing session skips the sensor-data gates. New attributes on these paths are read with `getattr` defaults (the contract tests build `DataManager` via `__new__`). A collision that lands between `save()`'s commit and the count (`'save'` with `_on_saving` latched) first completes the count exactly like the save-completion branch (owner decision Q7) and then rewinds — or, when that was the LAST episode, goes to `finish` instead: measured, a literal rewind to `'reset'` records no frame, but it publishes a phantom RESETTING („Episode N+1 von N") for the whole Zurücksetzen time and a RECORDING tick before READY. STOP during a latched save (or a latched FINISH that committed an episode) counts and uploads that episode. A session that ends before any dataset exists (FINISH, STOP or a forced recovery while the node still waits for sensor data) finalizes nothing and removes its crash marker. `_episode_reset` tests the writer with `is not None`, never by truthiness: `LeRobotDataset.__len__` counts SAVED frames, so a fresh dataset is falsy, and a discard in the first episode of a new dataset used to leave its frames in the buffer for the next saved episode (pre-existing, found by mutation-checking the smoke test). A discarded take (Wiederholen, collision discard, a gap or frame-loss re-record, a dropped FINISH run, skip) is cancelled through LeRobot's public API only (owner decision O6, „no private LeRobot internals"): `_episode_reset` marks it (`_discard_pending`), and `cancel_pending_discard()` — the one idempotent entry, called by the next record step before it records anything (the node's slot recording calls it before a take's first frame too) — runs `lerobot_dataset_wrapper.py::discard_episode`, i.e. LeRobot 0.5.1's `clear_episode_buffer()` (`cancel_streaming_episode` is an alias). LeRobot's own `scripts/lerobot_record.py` clears the buffer after its reset loop, right before the next take; this code clears it at the START of Zurücksetzen — both in frame-less ticks. The cancel waits for the encoder threads' 1 s queue timeout: the take after a redo starts ~1.0 s later (measured `recordingAgainAfterMs 1014`, first frames step 1, no hole), and a record command landing in that second answers „beschäftigt", except FINISH/STOP, which are queued (below); left to `start_episode()`, as on main, the identical cancel fell on the next take's first frame and put a real-time hole into a take whose timestamps do not know about it. A private encoder wake (~15 ms) was built and rejected by the owner. The run entry (`run_entered_mono`) is re-stamped after the cancel, so no slot decided before or during it belongs to the take, and Q3/`too_early` judge the real take. The cancel no longer blinds the collision detector (detection runs on the sensor thread, C1-A). After a collision recovery, `_on_resync_complete` still runs the pending cancel (plain resume) or the F1 finalize (`end_session_now`, forced resume) under the recorder lock BEFORE it publishes `/collision_flag=False`, while the arm is still frozen (the record timer has been stopped since the trip, so the lock is free), and only then releases teleop: this blocks the default group ≈ 1 s once per resume (accepted). A failed cancel never breaks the tick. The smoke test's `redo_then_keep` (a hole at frame 0 always fails; a runner stall later in a take earns one re-run) and `official_discard_timing` (< 1.5 s) fence where and how long the cancel lands. `_finalize_dataset()` runs at every terminal state before upload, idempotently; a failed finalize sets a German warning and skips the upload. `_verify_saved_video_files` checks the v3.0 layout (`videos/<key>/chunk-NNN/file-NNN.mp4`). The sibling `<dataset>.session.json` crash marker is armed only for recording sessions (detect + inform).
